The corpse half of the claim is not reachable in normal play (dead entities are GC'd from state.entities each tick and saves force alive:true), but the unconditional '✓ Builder' text at L103 is still a defect; minimal fix: add `&& entity.alive && !entity.pregnant` to the predicate and gate the success text on addToConstructionCrew's return value.

#### M4. canAssignWorkerToBuilding's unfinished-building branch omits !hasWorkAssignment, so it approves a builder the assignment refuses

- **File:** `src/game/buildingStaffingActions.ts` — lines 320-330 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L322-330: "return building.occupants.length < cap && state.entities.some(\n      (entity) =>\n        isPlayerHuman(entity)\n        && entity.alive\n        && !entity.isJuvenile\n        && !entity.pregnant\n        && !building.occupants.includes(entity.id)\n        && !isOnConstructionCrew(state, entity.id, building.id),\n    );" — the mutation path it gates requires `!hasWorkAssignment(entity)` (L92) and the shared predicate L58-68 does too.`
- **Impact:** In a village where every adult already holds a job, canAssignWorkerToBuilding returns true for an unfinished building, so App.tsx:1790 enables the "+ Fill builders" button (SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:673); clicking sends `assignWorker` (App.tsx:1754) into _applyBuilderAssignmentMut, which finds no builder (`!hasWorkAssignment`), assigns nobody and floats "No idle settlers!" (L97-100). The player is offered an action that can never succeed. The same function is the auto-play bot's eligibility test (virtualPlayer.ts:373).
- **Recommended change:** Add `&& !hasWorkAssignment(entity)` to the builder-branch filter (L324-329) so it mirrors _applyBuilderAssignmentMut and isEligibleIdleWorker.
- **Verification:** Verified: the unfinished branch of canAssignWorkerToBuilding (320-330) never tests hasWorkAssignment, while the mutation it gates (_applyBuilderAssignmentMut, 88-95) and the shared isEligibleIdleWorker (58-68) both require `!hasWorkAssignment(entity)`. With every adult employed, App.tsx:1790 feeds that true into SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:673, whose 'Fill builders' button appears because listAssignableWorkersForBuilding returns [] for unfinished buildings (line 302), and clicking runs fillBuildingWorkers -> _applyBuilderAssignmentMut which finds no builder and floats 'No idle settlers!' (98). No test covers it. Minimal fix: add `&& !hasWorkAssignment(entity)` to the filter at 324-329.

#### M5. Greenhouse food production is never recorded in the economy ledger

- **File:** `src/game/dailyBuildingEconomy.ts` — lines 638-651 · `resource-economy` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L638 `const amount = Math.floor(` … L649 `if (addResource(state, 'food', amount) > 0) {` L650 `rewardProductionSkills(state, building, 0.2, entityById);` L651 `}` — no `recordFoodProduced` call, unlike farms (L359), hunting (L454), fishing (L518) and silos (L780). `ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS` (economyLedger.ts:11-17) lists farms/hunting/silos/challenges/meals only, so 'fishing' (recorded at L518) also has no label and renders as its raw key in VillageTabPanel.tsx:85.`
- **Impact:** A staffed Greenhouse (18 + 5/worker food per day, buildings.ts:190-196) is a main food source but is invisible to the ledger. Concretely, a greenhouse-only village shows 'No food produced or eaten yet today — build farms or a hunting spot' (VillageTabPanel.tsx:71-77) and 'produced +0 · net 0' while food actually rises, and dashboardData.ts:269-279 reports the same wrong net; the archived foodHistory under-counts for up to 30 days. This contradicts economyLedger.ts's stated purpose ('why is my food low?' transparency).
- **Recommended change:** Mirror the farm path: `const added = addResource(state, 'food', amount); if (added > 0) { recordFoodProduced(state, 'greenhouse', added); rewardProductionSkills(state, building, 0.2, entityById); }` and add `greenhouse: 'Greenhouse'` plus `fishing: 'Fishing'` to ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS.
- **Verification:** The greenhouse branch (dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:626-663) calls addResource but never recordFoodProduced, unlike farms (359), hunting (454), fishing (518) and silos (780), and 'greenhouse' (and 'fishing') are absent from ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS (economyLedger.ts:11-17). A greenhouse-only village therefore reads 'No food produced or eaten yet today' / net 0 in VillageTabPanel.tsx:71-77 and dashboardData.ts:270-278 while food actually rises, and foodHistory under-counts. Minimal fix: mirror the farm path — `const added = addResource(state,'food',amount); if (added > 0) { recordFoodProduced(state,'greenhouse',added); rewardProductionSkills(...); }` and add the two labels.

#### M6. Nature-tab "Why this score" is a second, drifted implementation of the ecosystem-health formula (no preserve bonus, 4-species wildlife)

- **File:** `src/game/ecoBreakdown.ts` — lines 31-51, 66-69; dailyEcology.ts:76-101; src/components/tabPanels/NatureTabPanel.tsx:228, 277 | 33-34, 50-51 · `authority-violation` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `ecoBreakdown.ts:33 `const totalWildlife = counts.rabbits + counts.deer + counts.wolves + counts.foxes;` and ecoBreakdown.ts:51 `const health = Math.max(0, Math.min(100, 100 - buildingImpact - pollutionPenalty + wildlifeBonus));` — versus the owner at dailyEcology.ts:87-93 (`counts.rabbits + deer + wolves + foxes + werewolves + wildkin`) and dailyEcology.ts:101 `const rawEcoHealth = 100 - buildingImpact - pollutionPenalty + preserveBonus + wildlifeImpact;` with `preserveBonus += 4` per WildlifePreserve (dailyEcology.ts:76-78).`
- **Impact:** NatureTabPanel renders the authoritative value at line 228 (`state.ecosystemHealth`) and the duplicated value at line 277 (`ecoBreakdown.health`) inside the same 'Ecosystem Health' card. With one completed WildlifePreserve and, say, impact 20 / penalty 4 / wildlife 40, the owner computes 100-20-4+4-5 = 75% while the breakdown computes 71%: the panel shows two different Health numbers and the 'why this score' lines under it do not explain the number above them. A transformed Moon Howler or any wildkin makes them disagree even with no preserve, because those species are counted in the owner's wildlifeRatio only.
- **Recommended change:** Stop recomputing the score in ecoBreakdown: derive the returned `health` (and the 'Health' line) from `state.ecosystemHealth`, or import/reuse the single daily formula (werewolves+wildkin+preserveBonus) so the breakdown lines always sum to the value shown above them.
- **Verification:** getEcosystemBreakdown recomputes health with only rabbits+deer+wolves+foxes (ecoBreakdown.ts:33) and with no preserveBonus term (51), while the owner dailyEcology.ts adds counts.werewolves+counts.wildkin (87-93) and preserveBonus += 4 per completed WildlifePreserve (76-78, advertised at SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:42). NatureTabPanel renders the authoritative state.ecosystemHealth at line 228 and the drifted ecoBreakdown.health at line 277 in the same card, so building a Wildlife Preserve moves one Health number and not the other. Minimal fix: include werewolves/wildkin and the preserve bonus in the breakdown, or export and call the dailyEcology formula so the two cannot drift.

#### M7. `resolveCombatLogKind` has no event-type guard, so non-combat 'event' entries that mention a raid are reported as raids/defenses

- **File:** `src/game/eventLog.ts` — lines 48-63 | 48-63 (esp. 50, 53, 60) · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L50: `const msg = evt.message.toLowerCase();`  L53: `if (msg.includes('raid')) {`  L60: `return 'incoming_raid';` — the function never checks `evt.type`, and its caller passes the whole log: villagePortrait.ts:83-90 `const eventLog = state.eventLog ?? []; ... const kind = resolveCombatLogKind(eventLog[i]); if (kind === 'outgoing_raid') outgoingRaids++; else if (kind === 'defense' || kind === 'repelled' || kind === 'incoming_raid') defenses++;``
- **Impact:** `groupEvents.ts:818` (also 1110, 1125) logs `logEvent(state, 'event', 'Raid called off — truce with ' + rival.name, rival.name)` with no `combatKind`; the heuristic matches `'raid'`, fails the 'raid on the village'/' raid on ' tests and returns `'incoming_raid'`. Every peace treaty therefore increments `defenses` in villagePortrait, which both misreports the village's history and can silently delete the `+10` war-score bonus (`villagePortrait.ts:97 (defenses > 0 && outgoingRaids >= defenses ? 10 : 0)`), moving the portrait's war label across its 25/50 thresholds.
- **Recommended change:** Add a type guard as the first line after the kind fast-path: `if (evt.type !== 'combat') return null;` (combat writers always pass a kind, so this only removes false positives).
- **Verification:** resolveCombatLogKind (eventLog.ts:48-63) is the legacy fallback for saves without combatKind but never checks `evt.type`; villagePortrait.ts:83-92 runs it over the whole eventLog, and groupEvents.ts:818 (also 1110, 1125) writes `logEvent(state, 'event', 'Raid called off — truce with ' + rival.name)` without a combatKind, which matches only the generic `msg.includes('raid')` branch and returns 'incoming_raid' (it fails the 'raid on the village'/' raid on ' tests). Each peace treaty therefore increments `defenses`, which can remove the +10 war-score bonus (villagePortrait.ts:94-98) and shift the portrait's war label across its thresholds. Fix: add `if (evt.type !== 'combat') return null;` after the combatKind fast-path. Medium: a real misreport with limited (portrait/label) impact.

#### M8. Player commands issued while the sim worker is booting are stranded if the worker never boots (or faults), instead of being applied on the main-thread authority

- **File:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts` — lines 552-555, 196-207 (also 385-404, 225-233) | 552-555, 196-207 · `edge-case` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts:552-554: `if (this.workerBooting && this.workerHost) { this.deferredWorkerCommands.push({ cmd, sessionGen: this.sessionGen }); return;`
src/game/gameLoop.ts:198: `console.warn('[GameLoop] Worker init failed — falling back to main-thread ticks', err);``
- **Impact:** Commands are only replayed by flushDeferredWorkerCommands(), which is called exclusively on a successful worker init (line 193), recovery (line 295) or importSave (line 510). The init-failure catch (196-207) and fallbackFromWorker (385-404) set workerEnabled=false and continue on the main thread without flushing or clearing the queue, so every command the player (or useVirtualPlayer) issued during the boot window is silently dropped while the game keeps running; if a recovery later succeeds, those older commands are replayed on top of the newer commands that were already applied locally, inverting player intent. Nothing blocks input during boot — the only guard, isSimWorkerBooting() (line 410), is never called anywhere in src.
- **Recommended change:** In the init-failure catch and in fallbackFromWorker, after setting workerBooting/workerEnabled to false, call this.flushDeferredWorkerCommands() so the queued commands run through the same main-thread domain implementation (or clear the queue explicitly and document the drop).
- **Verification:** The cited code is exactly as described: `applyCommand` queues into `deferredWorkerCommands` while `workerBooting && workerHost` (552-555), and that queue is drained only by `flushDeferredWorkerCommands()` at 193, 295 and 510. The init-failure `.catch` (196-207) and `fallbackFromWorker` (385-404) set `workerBooting=false`/`workerEnabled=false` without flushing or clearing, so boot-window commands are never applied locally and the game resumes on the main thread; a later successful `attemptWorkerRecovery` then replays them via `flushDeferredWorkerCommands()` (295) on top of the newer commands that were already applied locally in the interim, inverting intent. `isSimWorkerBooting()` (410) has no caller anywhere in src and tests/gameLoop.commandDispatch.test.ts contains no deferred-queue case, so nothing locks the current behaviour. Minimal fix: in both the init-failure catch and `fallbackFromWorker`, call `this.flushDeferredWorkerCommands()` after clearing `workerBooting`/`workerEnabled` (the queue then applies through the same domain implementation via `applyCommandLocal`), or explicitly clear it and log the drop.

#### M9. frame() re-arms requestAnimationFrame only as its last statement, so one throw from gameTick permanently freezes the main-thread game (no rollback, no fallback)

- **File:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts` — lines 863-878, 900 · `edge-case` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts:865: `gameTick(this.world, focus);`
src/game/gameLoop.ts:900: `this.rafId = requestAnimationFrame(this.frame);``
- **Impact:** In main-thread mode — the documented fallback authority — the tick, view update, draw and notify calls are unguarded, and the next frame is scheduled only after all of them succeed. Any throw (e.g. assertSpatialGridInvariants at gameTick.ts:214 raises when the grid-invariant check is enabled, or a domain-owner error) escapes the rAF callback before line 900, so ticks, rendering and UI notifications stop forever with no recovery path, while the partially advanced tick (state.tick already incremented, lastProcessedCalendarDay not yet written) is left un-rolled-back. The worker path has exactly this protection (gameWorker.ts:354-357 wraps gameTick and calls applySimPrep on failure) and GameLoop even falls back to the unprotected path on a worker fault.
- **Recommended change:** Wrap the body of frame() (tick loop + updateView + draw + notify) in try/catch that logs the error, and move `this.rafId = requestAnimationFrame(this.frame)` into a `finally` block so the loop always continues.
- **Verification:** GameLoop.frame (gameLoop.ts:807-901) has no try/catch: gameTick(this.world, focus) at 865, updateView/draw at 890-891 and notify at 897 are all unguarded and `this.rafId = requestAnimationFrame(this.frame)` is the last statement (900), so any throw aborts the callback and no further frame is scheduled; there is no window.onerror/unhandledrejection handler anywhere in src (only GameWorkerHost's worker 'error' listener), so main-thread mode has no recovery. The worker path does guard the same call — src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.ts:316-357 wraps gameTick and calls applySimPrep(world, prepBackup) on failure — and fallbackFromWorker (gameLoop.ts:385-404) deliberately routes faults into this unprotected path. Fix: wrap the frame body in try/catch that logs, and move the requestAnimationFrame call into a finally block. Severity medium is right: it is a robustness gap that needs a throw (e.g. assertSpatialGridInvariants at gameTick.ts:214 when SPATIAL_GRID_INVARIANT is set), not an always-on defect.

#### M10. Irrigation's research effect target `drought_resist` is never read, so the tech has no effect

- **File:** `src/game/gameTypes.ts` — lines 909 | 909 (same pattern 908, 913, 914, 916, 917) · `dead-code` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `gameTypes.ts:909 `{ id: 'agriculture_3', ... name: 'Irrigation', description: 'Farms work 50% better in drought', ... effects: [{ target: 'drought_resist', multiplier: 1.5 }], ... }`
grassEcology.ts:55-56 `case WeatherType.Drought:` / `return 0.5;`
(whole-repo grep for `drought_resist` matches only gameTypes.ts:909; no code calls `getMultiplier(state, 'drought_resist')` and nothing checks `hasTech(state, 'agriculture_3')`)`
- **Impact:** A tier-3 research that costs 60 wood / 60 stone / 80 gold (and prerequisites agriculture_1+agriculture_2) advertises "Farms work 50% better in drought", but the drought farm penalty is applied unconditionally in `getWeatherFarmMultiplier` and the effect record is never queried, so the player pays for and receives nothing. The same 'declared target with no consumer' pattern exists for `all_food` (908), `lumber_pollution` (913), `building_health` (914), `human_lifespan` (916) and `plague_immunity` (917), but those nodes' promises are delivered by other mechanisms (building `unlockRequirement` ids, `hasTech('forestry_2')` in dailyEcology.ts:82, `hasTech('medicine_2')` in worldEvents.ts:171), so only `drought_resist` is a live defect.
- **Recommended change:** Consume the effect where the drought penalty is applied — e.g. in `getWeatherFarmMultiplier` (or its farm caller in dailyBuildingEconomy) multiply the `Drought` factor by `getMultiplier(state, 'drought_resist')` — or delete the unused `effects` entry and the drought sentence from the node description.
- **Verification:** gameTypes.ts:909 declares `effects: [{ target: 'drought_resist', multiplier: 1.5 }]` for agriculture_3 and a repo-wide grep finds no reader: getMultiplier(state, 'drought_resist') is never called and grassEcology.getWeatherFarmMultiplier returns the 0.5 Drought factor unconditionally (its only callers, dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:346/637, combine it with farm_yield/global_efficiency only). The node is reachable and purchasable — research.syncResearchUnlocks unlocks it once agriculture_2 is unlocked, and startResearch charges 60 wood / 60 stone / 80 gold — so the player pays for a promised effect that no code delivers; unlike all_food/forestry_2/medicine_2, whose promises are met by other mechanisms (buildings.ts:287 unlockRequirement, dailyEcology.ts:82 hasTech('forestry_2'), worldEvents.ts:171 hasTech('medicine_2')). Minimal fix: multiply the Drought factor by getMultiplier(state, 'drought_resist') where the penalty is applied, or delete the inert effect entry and the drought sentence from the description.

#### M11. needsMedicalCare compares griefUntilTick with 0 instead of the current tick

- **File:** `src/game/hospitalCare.ts` — lines 37 | 37 (consumer: hospitalCare.ts:183, src/game/humanHospitalBehavior.ts:40) · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `37: `if ((entity.griefUntilTick ?? 0) > 0 && entity.energy < entity.maxEnergy * 0.7) return true;``
- **Impact:** `griefUntilTick` is an absolute sim tick (set to `tick + TICKS_PER_DAY * 7` in humanLifecycleCleanup.ts:73 and `+3` for a lover at line 90) and is never reset to 0, so `> 0` stays true for the rest of the settler's life once they have grieved. Every ex-griever below 70% energy is permanently classified as needing medical care: they stay in tickHospitalDailyCare's patient list (line 183, so ward rounds treat them) and satisfy `shouldAttemptHospitalTreatment` (humanHospitalBehavior.ts:40) while on shift with energy 42-50%. All other grief checks in the codebase compare with the current tick (socialLife.ts:95, townHall.ts:176/248, citizenOverview.ts:129, storyEvents.ts:550/573).
- **Recommended change:** Take the current tick (add a `tick` parameter or pass `state.tick` from the callers) and test `(entity.griefUntilTick ?? 0) > tick`.
- **Verification:** griefUntilTick is an absolute tick written as tick + N*TICKS_PER_DAY (humanLifecycleCleanup.ts:73 and :90, humanLifecycle.ts:119-120) and never cleared, so `(entity.griefUntilTick ?? 0) > 0` stays true for life, unlike every other grief check (socialLife.ts:95, townHall.ts:176/248, citizenOverview.ts:129, storyEvents.ts:550/573) which compare against the current tick. Consequently any settler who has ever grieved is permanently listed as a patient in tickHospitalDailyCare (line 183) whenever energy < 70% and satisfies shouldAttemptHospitalTreatment (humanHospitalBehavior.ts:40, effective in the 42-50% band where the line-36 threshold has not already fired), giving free hospital routing/healing. Minimal fix: accept the current tick and test `> tick`.

#### M12. Visitor position advanced twice per tick after steerWithPath already moved the entity

- **File:** `src/game/hotelStay.ts` — lines 229-243 (233-236); pathfinding.ts:341-346 | 232-236 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `234: `visitor.x += visitor.vx;` / 235: `visitor.y += visitor.vy;` immediately after the steerWithPath call whose documented contract (pathfinding.ts:298) is "'path': entity was moved along waypoints (caller must not move it again)"`
- **Impact:** On the branch this call exists for (camp-to-hotel walk crossing water/mountain), `steerWithPath` already applies `entity.x += entity.vx; entity.y += entity.vy` (pathfinding.ts:343-344) and then the visitor is advanced a second time by the same velocity, so lodging visitors travel at double the intended 0.7x speed and skip waypoint checks; the 'direct' branch below sets velocity itself and moves once, so the two branches have inconsistent movement semantics. humanMovement.ts:150 handles the same return value correctly (returns false without moving).
- **Recommended change:** On `handled === 'path'` just `return true;` — delete lines 234-235 so the movement already performed by steerWithPath is the only one.
- **Verification:** steerWithPath applies entity.x/y += vx/vy itself on the 'path' result (pathfinding.ts:343-344) and its doc says 'caller must not move it again' (298), but steerVisitorToHotel adds vx/vy a second time at hotelStay.ts:234-235; humanMovement.ts:150 shows the correct 'return on path' pattern. Human-tick faction entities are handled in a dedicated branch that `continue`s (humanTick.ts:463-513), so nothing else moves the visitor that tick — a hotel-bound visitor routing around water/mountains really does travel two steps per tick. Minimal fix: replace lines 233-237 with `if (handled === 'path') return true;`.

#### M13. Hospital treatment gate is a day-stable roll re-evaluated every tick, so treatment (skill and food) repeats per tick

- **File:** `src/game/humanHospitalBehavior.ts` — lines 87-97 · `cadence-violation` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `humanHospitalBehavior.ts:94:
    if (hospital && personDayRoll(entity.id, state.tick, 840) < 0.2) {
      treatPatientAtHospital(state, entity, hospital);`
- **Impact:** The roll is constant for the whole colony day, so once it passes and the settler is within 36px of a staffed hospital the call happens on every tick. Human energy loss is 4.2/3 = 1.4 per tick (speciesConfig.ts:134), so `heal = min(deficit, 8 + strength*14)` is >= 0.5 nearly every tick and treatPatientAtHospital does not bail: each call grants `gainSkill(state, id, JobType.Doctor, 0.08)` to every hospital occupant (hospitalCare.ts:106-108) and has a 25% chance to spend 1 food (hospitalCare.ts:99-101). A pending mother or low-energy settler hovering at the ward therefore produces dozens of treatments per day (up to ~18 food and several skill points per occupant) instead of the intended ~20%-of-days event.
- **Recommended change:** Gate the attempt once per settler per day (a `lastHospitalTreatmentDay` marker set after a successful treatment) or move it into the daily ward round that already exists (`tickHospitalDailyCare`, hospitalCare.ts:173), rather than re-rolling a day-stable value every tick.
- **Verification:** tickHumanHospitalPatientCare is called every tick for active humans (humanTick.ts:1095) and gates treatment on personDayRoll(entity.id, state.tick, 840) < 0.2 (line 94), which humanSchedule.ts:97-107 defines as stable for a whole colony day; treatPatientAtHospital then heals, has a 25% chance to spend 1 food and grants gainSkill(JobType.Doctor, 0.08) to every hospital occupant on each call (hospitalCare.ts:99-108). shouldAttemptHospitalTreatment (lines 40-43) passes unconditionally for a pregnant settler, and with a 1.4/tick drain the heal is > 0.5 almost every tick, so a pregnant settler parked in the ward is treated ~72 times per qualifying day instead of on ~20% of days — roughly 18 food and 5.8 skill points per occupant, with doctor skill capped at 100 in ~17 days. Minimal fix: latch one treatment per settler per colony day, or move the work into the existing daily tickHospitalDailyCare round (hospitalCare.ts:173).

#### M14. Hospital routing advances the settler's position in addition to the loop-bottom integration (double movement)

- **File:** `src/game/humanHospitalBehavior.ts` — lines 79-83 | 73-85 · `authority-violation` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `humanHospitalBehavior.ts:79-83:
      entity.vx = (dx / distance) * speed * 0.55;
      entity.vy = (dy / distance) * speed * 0.55;
      entity.spriteAngle = Math.atan2(entity.vy, entity.vx);
      entity.x += entity.vx;
      entity.y += entity.vy;
(humanTick.ts:1467 then integrates the same velocity for every player human: `entity.x += entity.vx * roadMult;`; the canonical helper simulation/humanMovement.ts:154 only sets vx/vy and never touches x/y.)`
- **Impact:** Position is written by a behaviour module that does not own movement (SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md §3: movement owns 'Position, velocity, movement targets'), and the step is applied twice in the same tick, so a routed pregnant/sick settler walks to the ward at roughly 2x the intended 0.55*speed (plus the road multiplier), arriving early and bypassing the intended pacing. The same double-step also happens on ticks where the leisure block later blends vx, because the early `x += vx` already used the hospital vector.
- **Recommended change:** Delete lines 82-83 (`entity.x += entity.vx; entity.y += entity.vy;`) and keep only the vx/vy/spriteAngle assignment, matching commuteHumanToBuilding — the main loop's single integration at humanTick.ts:1467 then applies the step exactly once.
- **Verification:** tickHumanHospitalPatientCare (humanHospitalBehavior.ts:79-83) writes entity.x/y in addition to setting velocity, and it is called at humanTick.ts:1095 inside the per-human loop that integrates position once at 1467-1468 (×1.5 near roads); no `continue` separates them (all five `continue`s in the file are before line 520), so a routed settler advances 2-2.5× per tick and jitters around the hospital. Minimal fix: delete lines 82-83 so the loop-bottom integration remains the only position writer. Reachable in normal mid-game play whenever a staffed hospital exists and a pregnant settler is off-shift or near term.

#### M15. Second implementation of the settler energy-drain rule in the throttled path, with a different modifier set

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 429-434 | 429-442 (offscreen formula) vs src/game/simulation/humanNeeds.ts:127-177 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:430-434 `let minimalEnergyLoss = hasWell ? config.energyLossPerTick * 0.8 : config.energyLossPerTick; if (hasHospital) minimalEnergyLoss *= 0.9; if (isWinter && !canHeat) minimalEnergyLoss *= 1.5; minimalEnergyLoss *= traitMultiplier(entity, 'hardy', 0.85);` — the on-screen path instead calls `humanEnergyLoss(entity, config, {...})` (humanTick.ts:516). humanNeeds.ts:161-162 applies `if (isNearHome && isRestingPeriod) { loss *= ENERGY_MODIFIERS.HOME_RESTING_REDUCTION; }` (0.5), humanNeeds.ts:142 applies the greenthumb 0.7 winter resistance, and humanNeeds.ts:174 applies `traitMultiplier(entity, 'fierce', ...)` (0.9).`
- **Impact:** An off-screen settler drains ~2x the energy of an identical on-screen settler at night (resting half-rate missing), plus 11% (fierce) and up to ~43% extra in an unheated winter (greenthumb missing). Because `active` is false purely from viewport focus (humanTick.ts:377-384), exhaustion and `killFromExhaustion` (humanTick.ts:437-439, 1474-1476) become a function of where the player is looking: a settler standing in their own home can die of exhaustion off-screen while the same settler would rest on-screen.
- **Recommended change:** Call the single owner in the off-screen branch as well: `entity.energy -= humanEnergyLoss(entity, config, { hasWell, isWinter, canHeat, hasHospital, tick: state.tick, hourOfDay, buildingById });` (the helper is already imported at humanTick.ts:99 and is only a handful of multiplications), deleting the divergent inline copy.
- **Verification:** The not-active branch of tickHumans re-implements the drain at humanTick.ts:430-434 with only well/hospital/winter/hardy, while the owner humanEnergyLoss (humanNeeds.ts:127-176) also applies greenthumb, fierce and the 0.5 home-resting reduction; this same function already routes both the meal rule (tryEatColonyMeal at line 436) and the active path (lines 516-524) through the shared owners, which is exactly the pattern the CHANGELOG's larder fix established (BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-10-larder-meal-rule-duplicated.md exists). Because the `active` gate includes inFocus (lines 377-384), a settler resting at home loses 0.5x base energy on camera and 1.0x off camera, so camera position changes exhaustion-death odds. Minimal fix: replace lines 430-434 with the same humanEnergyLoss(entity, config, {...}) call used at 516-524.

#### M16. On-duty official service can never run: `ordinaryWorkplace` excludes the TownHall, so `onDayJobShift` is always false for an Official

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 538-550, 1086-1094 | 407-427 (caller gate), vs src/game/simulation/humanSocial.ts:64 and src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:205 · `cadence-violation` · partly-confirmed
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:412 `(state.tick + entity.id) % 3 === 0` gates the call `tryAmbientRandomDialogue(entity, ambientChatNeighbors(entity), state.tick, 0.036 * PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE, ...)`. The candidate provider is `simAmbientChatNeighbors` (humanTick.ts:282-283), and humanSocial.ts:64 begins `if ((tick + self.id) % SOCIAL_STAGGER !== 0) { return []; }` with adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:205 `export const SOCIAL_STAGGER = 6;`. Multiples of 3 are not all multiples of 6.`
- **Impact:** For every tick where (tick+id) % 6 === 3 (exactly half of the ticks the caller admits), ambientChatNeighbors() returns an empty array, so `tryAmbientRandomDialogue` can never pair the speaker with a nearby settler and always emits a solo remark. Paired ambient conversation therefore fires at roughly half the rate the code intends, which is precisely the monologue regression documented in tests/humanChat.ambientPairing.test.ts (BUG 2026-08-21-ambient-dialogue-often-becomes-leader-monologue) — that test calls tryAmbientRandomDialogue directly and so cannot see the caller-side gate.
- **Recommended change:** Replace the caller's `(state.tick + entity.id) % 3 === 0` with the same stagger the callee uses: `(state.tick + entity.id) % SOCIAL_STAGGER === 0` (SOCIAL_STAGGER is already imported in humanTick.ts line 113). This keeps the expensive-neighbour-query short-circuit while guaranteeing every admitted roll actually receives candidates.
- **Verification:** Verified: ordinaryWorkplace (538-541) excludes Church and TownHall, BUILDING_JOB_TYPES maps TownHall -> JobType.Official (gameTypes.ts:184), schoolTarget is set only for juveniles (364-367), so onDayJobShift (543-550) is false for every Official and officialHandlePetitioners (humanVenueBehavior.ts:85 -> townHall.ts:308) is unreachable — the hall description still promises petitions/audiences (townHall.ts:157). One impact bullet is overstated: officials do gain Official skill from petitions through the daily owner tickTownHallAudiences (townHall.ts:345-361, called every 3 days from dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:769), which grants +0.06 to hall occupants. Minimal fix: compute `onOfficialShift = entity.job === JobType.Official && workplace?.type === BuildingType.TownHall && workplace.completed && goWorkTime` and pass `onDayJobShift || onOfficialShift` at line 1092.

#### M17. tickInventionFair re-pushes a fresh Demonstration card every colony day because nothing records that the card was already offered

- **File:** `src/game/inventionFair.ts` — lines 198-228 | 186-193, 205-227 | 139-143, 188-229, 231-248 · `dead-code` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `190: `[FLAG_INVENTION]: hashSalt(`invention-${inventionId}`) % 1000,` — grep for 'invention_fair_invention' finds only the declaration (18) and this write; the demonstration tick (198-229) and resolveStage2 (231-248) never consult it.`
- **Impact:** Funding the Self-Counting Granary, the Polite Gate or the Emergency Bell differs only in wood cost: the demonstration outcome (success roll 209, descriptions 216-218) and the keep/improve/dismantle effects are identical, so the flag is a computed-but-unused value that signals a missing per-invention effect (the choice text at 107-109 promises distinct behaviours).
- **Recommended change:** Read FLAG_INVENTION in tickInventionFair/resolveStage2 to vary the demonstration description and the keep/improve effect per invention, or delete the flag and its hashSalt computation.
- **Verification:** tickInventionFair only returns on FLAG_RESOLVED or a non-funded FLAG_STATUS (lines 199-203) and pushes a card whose id embeds state.tick (line 212) without stamping any one-shot flag, unlike its sibling travelingTheatre.ts:225-230 which latches FLAG_STAGE3 before pushing; it runs once per day (dailyWorldEvents.ts:125), pushStoryCard dedupes by id only (storyHelpers.ts:27-32), cards live 4 days and App.tsx:1458-1460 renders up to two, so a duplicate 'The Demonstration' appears each day until the player answers. Answering removes only that id (storyEvents.ts:205) while resolveInventionFair still dispatches on FLAG_STATUS.funded, so each remaining duplicate re-runs resolveStage2 (line 246: +4 wood for dismantle, +1 reputation for keep) — but the impact is bounded to the ~4-5 live duplicates (<= ~20 wood or ~5 reputation) plus repeated banners, so the 'free wood source' is real but small and high is overstated; the durable fix is the suggested one-shot `invention_fair_demo_offered` flag set before pushStoryCard.

#### M18. Leader household includes adult children, so married children are dragged into the manor and other residents are evicted every day

- **File:** `src/game/leaderHouse.ts` — lines 30-40, 93-110 | 42-52 (interacts with src/game/moonHowler.ts:438) · `invariant-risk` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `if (prev?.alive && prev.occupation === LEADER_OCCUPATION) {
      prev.occupation = 'settler';`
- **Impact:** transformToWerewolfForm snapshots `occupation` into human.moonHowlerSaved (moonHowler.ts:342) and leaves the live occupation as 'leader', so a sitting leader who loses an election or is replaced while in werewolf form is stepped down here only in live state. On the dawn revert, `were.occupation = saved?.occupation === LEADER_OCCUPATION ? LEADER_OCCUPATION : 'settler';` (moonHowler.ts:438) writes 'leader' back onto a settler who no longer holds villageLeaderId. Nothing repairs it: applyLeaderOccupation only ever steps down the id captured as `prevLeaderId` (null from now on), and workforce.ts:206/227/260 all preserve LEADER_OCCUPATION, so the ex-leader is permanently displayed as 💼 leader (SelectedEntityPanel.tsx:332, PopulationPanel.tsx:52) and prepareWorkforce (workforce.ts:464-481) permanently skips the stale-workplace repair for them. Reachable whenever a reveal lands inside a full-moon night (20:00-06:00, moonHowler.ts:219-226) for a cursed settler who is the incumbent.
- **Recommended change:** Clear the snapshot too when stepping down a transformed leader: `if (prev.moonHowlerSaved?.occupation === LEADER_OCCUPATION) prev.moonHowlerSaved.occupation = 'settler';` inside the existing step-down block.
- **Verification:** collectLeaderHousehold (leaderHouse.ts:30-40) returns raw collectOwnHousehold output, which adds every living id in childrenIds with no age test (householdComposition.ts:19-22) even though isMinorChild exists in the same module (5-8) and the sibling couple path filters on it (residencyReconciliation.ts:277-283, 'adult children who moved out stay put'). syncLeaderHouseResidency (81-136) is called daily (tickLayerDaily.ts:59) and unconditionally sets each household member's residenceBuildingId to the manor (102-107), then the trailing assignMissingResidences (119) cannot put a *married* adult child back (pickResidenceForFamily skips leader houses, residencySelection.ts:615/632) and rebalanceAdultChildrenFromFamilyHomeWhenEmptyAvailable explicitly skips leader-house residents (345) — so a married child/chapter ping-pongs out each day and re-logs the '👑 … moved into the Leader's House' chronicle line every colony day, contradicting the CHANGELOG claim of a once-only event, while a single adult child simply stays in the manor. Fix: build the household from leader + partner + members where isMinorChild(member) is true.

#### M19. Autumn herd spawns deer on impassable terrain (deep water, mountains, river/bank)

- **File:** `src/game/migration.ts` — lines 37-59 (spawn positions 44-47, createEntity 48-54) · `edge-case` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `44:    if (edge === 0) { x = 40; y = height / 2 + spread; }
(48-54 createEntity(EntityType.Deer, x, y, ...) — no isPassableWildlifePosition check anywhere in spawnHerdAtEdge)`
- **Impact:** Every other spawn path in the codebase gates on terrain (worldGen.spawnWildlifeAtRandomPassable line 224, spawnWildlifeRing line 349); this one uses fixed edge coordinates. Measured by importing the repo's own isPassableWildlifePosition and running tickMigration on real maps (40 maps x 6 presets): coastal 119/400 herd deer spawn on deep/shallow water in 40/40 maps; verdant 45/400 (water/river/riverBank); harsh 76/400 (25 inside Mountains); riverlands 77/400. So a third of a coastal herd starts standing in the sea, on tiles the rest of the simulation treats as unpassable (UNPASSABLE_WILDLIFE_TERRAIN, worldGen.ts:71-78); those deer must wander out before normal land behaviour applies (120 ticks after arrival most blocked deer were still on unpassable tiles), and the player sees deer swimming in the ocean.
- **Recommended change:** In spawnHerdAtEdge, validate each candidate point before createEntity with isPassableWildlifePosition(state, x, y, 8) (nudge along the edge, or fall back to spawnWildlifeRing(state, EntityType.Deer, edgeMidX, edgeMidY, count, 40, 120) which already contains the passability retry loop).
- **Verification:** spawnHerdAtEdge (migration.ts:37-60) uses fixed edge coordinates (x=40 / width-40 / y=40 / height-40) and never calls isPassableWildlifePosition, unlike worldGen's other spawn paths (worldGen.ts:155), and I reproduced the effect with the repo's own predicate over 10 medium maps per preset: unpassable herd deer were 29/100 on coastal (in 10/10 maps), 16/100 riverlands, 13/100 harsh, 9/100 verdant. Because tickLayerSystems' deep-water sliding physics zeroes velocity for a next position on water (tickLayerSystems.ts:614-628), a deer that starts inside it is effectively stuck and eventually starves. Fix: validate each candidate point before createEntity (nudging along the edge) or spawn via the existing passability-retry helper.

#### M20. Moon-Howler cooldown state is written every full moon but absent from save, worker prep and tick delta

- **File:** `src/game/moonHowler.ts` — lines 659, 673-676, 693, 812 | 693, 812 | 693, 812 (consumers 659, 673-678) · `save-persistence` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `moonHowler.ts:693  `  state.lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick = state.tick;`
moonHowler.ts:812  `      state.moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil = state.tick + MOON_HOWLER_EXORCISM_INTERVAL_HOURS * TICKS_PER_HOUR;``
- **Impact:** Neither field is in WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS (saveSchema.ts:4-26) nor in SimPrepKeys (src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts:10-87), and simDelta's world tuple only carries renffrOmen/renffrChatterUntilTick (simDelta.ts:126-127). Both are therefore undefined after save/load or after worker preparation while world.tick is restored. Reloading during a full-moon night makes `state.tick - (state.lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick ?? -9999)` always pass the 2-hour gate (line 673-678) and `state.tick < (state.moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil ?? -1)` false (line 659), so the surviving priests immediately retry the rite within the same night instead of retreating for the declared cooldown — each failed rite still carries a 40% priest-death branch (MOON_HOWLER_OUTCOME_KILL_PRIEST).
- **Recommended change:** Add 'lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick' and 'moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil' to WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS (saveSchema.ts) and to SimPrepKeys/extractSimPrep (simPrep.ts), and scale them in migrateTickTimeline (saveLoad.ts:228-234) like the other absolute tick deadlines.
- **Verification:** Both fields are WorldState members (gameTypes.ts:751-752) used as durable pacing state — written at 693 and 812 and read at 659 and 676 (plus humanTick.ts:609) — yet they appear in neither WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS (saveSchema.ts:4-26), SimPrepKeys/SimPrepPayload (simPrep.ts:10-87) nor SimTickDelta (delta.ts:126-127 carries renffrOmen/renffrChatterUntilTick but not these), and no test references them. So a save/reload during a full-moon night clears the two-hour rite cooldown and the priests_scared window (allowing the cure outcome to be re-rolled), and a worker error rollback restores everything except these two fields. Minimal fix: add both keys to the save allow-list, the prep payload and the tick delta (or declare them intentionally transient and stop treating them as persisted pacing state).

#### M21. Rare replacement roll repeats on all three ticks of hour 20, so a 15%-per-moon event becomes ~39%

- **File:** `src/game/moonHowler.ts` — lines 151-165, 1016 · `cadence-violation` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `moonHowler.ts:161  `    && hourOfDay === NIGHT_START`
moonHowler.ts:163  `    && rng() < MOON_HOWLER_REPLACEMENT_CHANCE`
moonHowler.ts:1016  `  if (shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse(colonyDay, hourOfDay, humanPop, activeMoonCurses, rng)) {`  (called from tickLayerRealtime.ts:97 on EVERY tick)`
- **Impact:** TICKS_PER_HOUR = 3, so hourOfDay is 20 for tickOfDay 60, 61 and 62 — three consecutive ticks. Nothing latches the roll: the only guard is `activeCursed === 0`, which a failed roll does not change, and tickMoonHowlerCycle runs once per tick via tickLayerRealtime. A quiet full moon therefore gets three independent 15% rolls: P = 1 - 0.85^3 = 38.6% per full moon, i.e. ~10 replacement Howlers per 360-day year instead of the ~3.9/year implied by the constant (and the ~1-2/year the comment at lines 78-84 claims). SIMULATION_AUTHORITY §5 says a replacement appears 'only through a rare replacement roll'; the one-Howler invariant still holds, but the declared rare-event pacing is ~2.6x off. The existing tests call the pure predicate once per case (tests/moonHowler.rare.test.ts:129-219) so the per-tick repetition is not locked in.
- **Recommended change:** Roll once per moon instead of once per hour-20 tick: in tickMoonHowlerCycle line 1016 use `if (isStartOfClockHour(state.tick) && shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse(colonyDay, hourOfDay, humanPop, activeMoonCurses, rng))` (isStartOfClockHour is already re-exported by dayCycle.ts:26), or latch the roll on a per-colony-day field.
- **Verification:** shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse (moonHowler.ts:151-165) is evaluated from tickMoonHowlerCycle line 1016, which runs on every tick via tickLayerRealtime.ts:97-105 with no latch and with a fresh rng() draw; since TICKS_PER_HOUR is 3 (hour 20 spans tickOfDay 60-62) and NIGHT_START is 20 (dayCycleConstants.ts:15), a quiet full moon gets three independent 15% rolls, i.e. 1 − 0.85³ ≈ 38.6% instead of 15% (with DAYS_PER_MOON_CYCLE = 14, ~10 replacements/year vs the ~1-2/year claimed at lines 78-84). tests/moonHowler.rare.test.ts:129-156 calls the pure predicate once per case, so the per-tick repetition is not locked in. Fix: gate the roll to one tick per moon, e.g. `isStartOfClockHour(state.tick) && shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse(...)`, or latch it on a per-colony-day field. Downgraded from high to medium: the one-Howler invariant and the 'no guaranteed Howler' rule still hold — only the declared rare-event pacing is ~2.6× off.

#### M22. pathCache stores origin-dependent waypoint lists under origin-independent keys, so a settler can be steered back to a stale path origin

- **File:** `src/game/pathfinding.ts` — lines 284, 318-336 | 267-269 · `numeric-safety` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `269: `const steps = Math.max(4, Math.min(128, Math.ceil(span / (TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE * 0.5))));` (comment 268: "Sub-tile sampling steps prevent tunneling through 1-tile diagonal obstacles")`
- **Impact:** `TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE` is 10 (gameTypes.ts:992) and the intended step is 5px, but the 128 cap makes the real spacing span/128: 11.7px for a full medium-map line (1200x900 -> span ~1500px) and ~25px on a huge map (2560x1920 -> span ~3200px), i.e. larger than the tile itself. A 1-tile river or mountain strip can fall between two samples, so `lineCrossesBlocked` returns false, `steerWithPath` returns 'direct' (316/349) and the human walks straight through water/mountain — the exact failure the module was written to prevent. Humans have no terrain collision (only wildlife has the deep-water guard, tickLayerSystems.ts:609-628), so nothing downstream corrects it.
- **Recommended change:** Raise the cap so the step can never exceed half a tile (and the tile width for every supported map size), e.g. `const steps = Math.max(4, Math.min(1024, Math.ceil(span / (TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE * 0.5))));`.
- **Verification:** steerWithPath caches pathWaypoints(findPath(...)) under the caller's key (lines 319-331) and pathCache is cleared only when the PathGrid object changes (lines 288-291) or the cache exceeds 200 entries (line 329), while simulation/humanMovement.commutePathCacheKey builds `c_<entityId>_<buildingId>_<h|w>` from the entity id alone (lines 92-93). pathWaypoints includes the start tile as wp[0] (pathfinding.ts:254-257) and the skip loop at line 335 can only move forward, so when the same settler's home leg later starts somewhere else (humanTick.ts:710, humanLeisureBehavior.ts:62/104 all call commuteHumanToBuilding with arrivingHome=true from wherever the settler stands) the entity steers toward the old origin — a visible wrong-direction detour, travelled by direct velocity with no collision check. Minimal fix: cache { originX, originY, wp } and recompute when the entity is farther than ~2 tiles from the stored origin.

#### M23. Housing pickers accept rival-camp houses; two callers pass buildings.filter(isResidenceBuilding), which keeps faction 'rival' residences

- **File:** `src/game/residencySelection.ts` — lines 631-633 (call sites humanTick.ts:914, humanRelationships.ts:908) | 263-281 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `residencySelection.ts:267-268 `const unit: Entity[] = [custodian]; visited.add(custodian.id);` — the bucket loop never checks `visited.has(custodianId)` before building the unit (contrast the fallback loop at line 284: `if (visited.has(human.id)) continue;` and the partner guard at line 270 `if (partner && !visited.has(partner.id))`).`
- **Impact:** Concrete failure: mother M has minor child A (custodian M), father F has minor child B (custodian F, B's mother dead), and M and F are partners (a blended family created by remarriage — reachable through humanTick.ts:910/tryCompleteCourtshipMarriage). Bucket[M]=[A] builds unit [M,F,A] and marks F visited, but bucket[F]=[B] still builds unit [F,B] containing F again. In assignMissingResidences (residencyReconciliation.ts:230-239) every unit that needs reassignment clears and re-places ALL of its members, so F is moved twice per pass and the two units keep stealing him: [F,B] is placed in an empty house, then [M,F,A] (now invalid) is cleared and placed in another empty house, and the 24-pass loop never records `reassigned === 0`, so it runs all 24 passes on every assign pulse (4x/day). The stable end state leaves the minor child B alone in one house while F, M and A occupy another — the exact opposite of assignFamilyToResidence's contract ('keeps children with custodian'); ensureOrphanAdoption cannot repair it because B still has a living natural custodian, so the split persists across pulses.
- **Recommended change:** Add the missing guard at the top of the bucket loop: `if (visited.has(custodianId)) continue;` (and attach that bucket's children to the custodian's existing unit, or rebuild the children buckets from the emitted units) so each settler belongs to exactly one housing unit; the child whose custodian is already in a unit must join that unit rather than fall through to the single-entity fallback at line 283.
- **Verification:** The pickers only skip leader houses and do not filter faction (`pickResidenceForFamily` 631-633, likewise `pickSharedResidenceForFamily` 582-584 and `pickLeastCrowdedResidence` 58-63), while `listPlayerResidences` (35-37) is the sanctioned filter; `humanTick.ts:914` and `humanRelationships.ts:908` both pass `buildings.filter(isResidenceBuilding)`, and rival camps really do create completed `House` buildings with `faction: 'rival'` (groupEvents.ts:447-456 -> createRivalBuilding 308-324; rivalEvents.ts:172 can add more). Because `countResidentsInBuilding` is called with player-only humans, a rival house counts 0 residents and scores as empty (EMPTY_HOUSE_BONUS for a lone settler, or score 0 versus 2 for an already-housed couple), so `syncPartnerResidence` (residencyReconciliation.ts:269-291) and `reassignDivorcedResidences` can move player settlers into the enemy camp; `syncResidenceOccupants` skips rival buildings (115) so the section-5 residence/occupants invariant is false until the next assign pulse clears the stale id in `assignMissingResidences` (205-212, ≤18 ticks). Fix: add `if (residence.faction === 'rival') continue;` beside the leader-house skip in the three pickers, or pass `listPlayerResidences(buildings)` at both call sites.

#### M24. migrateTickTimeline scales some absolute-tick fields but not pregnancyDueProgress or pendingStoryEvents deadlines

- **File:** `src/game/saveLoad.ts` — lines 244-252 (with 267-282) | 244-245; 236-252 | 236-252 (244-245), 218-288 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L244-245: `// pregnancyProgress is 0..PREGNANCY_TICKS absolute progress — scale with day length` / `scaleField(rec, 'pregnancyProgress');` — the sibling `entity.pregnancyDueProgress` (set from the same constant at humanRelationships.ts:640) is not in the scaled list; `pendingStoryEvents[].createdAtTick/expiresAtTick` are also skipped even though storyEvents.ts:158 compares them against `state.tick`.`
- **Impact:** In any load that needs a rescale (`_ticksPerDay !== TICKS_PER_DAY`), progress is multiplied by `scale` while the threshold keeps the old unit. With scale>1 (24→72 ticks/day) a mid-pregnancy settler's `pregnancyProgress` jumps past `pregnancyDueProgress`, so humanLifecycle.ts:63-66 delivers on the next pregnancy tick; with scale<1 the pregnancy never reaches term. Pending story cards likewise expire instantly (scale>1) or linger far past their window (scale<1). Currently latent only because `parseSaveJson` rejects every `_version` except the current one and current saves always write `_ticksPerDay = TICKS_PER_DAY`.
- **Recommended change:** Add `scaleField(rec, 'pregnancyDueProgress');` next to the existing `pregnancyProgress` line, and scale `createdAtTick`/`expiresAtTick` for each entry of `world.pendingStoryEvents` in the same migration.
- **Verification:** migrateTickTimeline (218-288) scales pregnancyProgress (245) and the nested moonHowlerSaved copy (249) but not pregnancyDueProgress, which is produced from the same PREGNANCY_TICKS constant (entityFactory.ts:166, humanRelationships.ts:640/660/676) and compared as `pregnancyProgress < dueProgress` (humanLifecycle.ts:64-66), so on a 24-tick legacy save the progress triples while the due value does not and every pregnant settler gives birth on the first tick after load. It also has no loop for world.pendingStoryEvents, whose expiresAtTick is written with TICKS_PER_DAY*3 (storyEvents.ts:305) and read as `state.tick < e.expiresAtTick` (storyEvents.ts:158), so all open story cards are filtered away on the same load. Fix: add scaleField(rec, 'pregnancyDueProgress') (and the snapshot copy) and a pendingStoryEvents loop beside the raid loops.

#### M25. Save allow-list omits live simulation state: visitor quest, Moon Howler cooldowns, village happiness, ledger history

- **File:** `src/game/saveSchema.ts` — lines 4-26 | 4-26 (omissions), cf. 14-23 · `save-persistence` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `saveSchema.ts:16 `'visitorGroups', 'activeVillageRequest', 'villageRequestCooldownUntilDay', 'villageRequestHistory',`
saveSchema.ts:23 `'dismissedNotificationIds', 'lastWildlifeReplenishLogDay', 'eventsThisYear',`
gameTypes.ts:740-741 `foodHistory?: FoodDaySample[];` / `visitorQuest?: VisitorQuest;`
gameTypes.ts:751-752 `lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick?: number;` / `moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil?: number;`
moonHowler.ts:673 `const last = state.lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick ?? -9999;``
- **Impact:** `WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS` is the only list that `pickWorldFieldsForSave` writes and `pickWorldStateFromSave` reads, so after save -> load `state.visitorQuest` is undefined: an accepted smith quest (its `progress`, `expiresDay`, and the 30 gold/4 reputation reward) silently disappears, and `deliverVisitorQuest` can no longer be satisfied (it only sets `status:'completed'`, never grants a new quest for the same group). The same field is also absent from `SimPrepKeys`/`SimTickDelta` (no match anywhere in simWorker/simBuffers), so in worker mode the main thread never receives a quest the worker created and the quest card cannot appear at all. `lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick`/`moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil` are the church-rite rate limit and priest-fear timer: after a reload line 673 reads `-9999`, so the rite can fire on the first full-moon night regardless of the cooldown (save-scumming the cure roll), and already-scared priests stop being scared. `villageHappiness` reverts to the `?? 50` default in PopulationPanel, and `foodHistory`/`economyLedger` history is dropped (the delta carries both, so this is a save-only gap).
- **Recommended change:** Add the sim-owned fields to the allow-list (`'visitorQuest'`, `'lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick'`, `'moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil'`, `'villageHappiness'`, `'foodHistory'`, `'economyLedger'`) and carry `visitorQuest` through `SimPrepKeys`/`SimTickDelta` and the load defaults in loadGameFromParsed; keep genuinely transient presentation fields (screenShakeImpulse, huntVisuals) out.
- **Verification:** `WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS` (saveSchema.ts:4-26) is indeed the only list used by `pickWorldFieldsForSave` (46-51) and `pickWorldStateFromSave` (saveLoad.ts:65-71), and it contains none of `visitorQuest`, `lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick`, `moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil`, `villageHappiness`, `foodHistory`, `economyLedger` (the only other persisted fields are the four transient ones in `pickTransientWorldFieldsForSave`, viewState.ts:338-345). `visitorQuest` is likewise absent from `SimPrepKeys`/`SimTickDelta` (simPrep.ts:10-87, simDelta.ts), so in worker mode `groupEvents.ts:416 -> maybeStartVisitorQuest` writes a quest the main thread never receives and App.tsx:1545 can never render it, while Moon Howler's rite cooldown falls back to `-9999` after any reload (moonHowler.ts:673), letting the cure roll fire again on the first full-moon night. Fix: add `visitorQuest`, `lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick`, `moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil` (and, if persistence is wanted, `villageHappiness`/`foodHistory`/`economyLedger`) to the allow-list and carry `visitorQuest` through `SimPrepKeys`/`SimTickDelta`; note `pushCompletedDayToHistory` documents food history as transient, so that part is the weakest of the listed gaps.

#### M26. Big News ids are re-minted from 1 after a save load while persisted dismissal ids keep the old bn_N — every new card is silently hidden

- **File:** `src/game/simEffects.ts` — lines 84-111 (with src/game/saveLoad.ts:367, 446) | 84-111 | 81, 84-93, 102-104 · `save-persistence` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `simEffects.ts:93 `nextBigNewsId = maxSeq + 1;` driven only by simEffects.ts:86-87 `for (const item of state.bigNews) { const match = /^bn_(\d+)$/.exec(item.id) ...`; simEffects.ts:102-104 `const seq = nextBigNewsId++; state.bigNews.push({ id: \`bn_${seq}\`, ...`. The load path empties that array first: saveLoad.ts:367 `bigNews: [],` then saveLoad.ts:446 `syncBigNewsIdFromState(world);` — while the dismissal ids ARE persisted (saveSchema.ts:22 `'villageForge', 'tutorialSeen', 'dismissedBigNewsIds', ...`) and re-applied as a display filter (useTransientGameFeedback.ts:304-311 `const dismissedIds = new Set(loadedWorld.dismissedBigNewsIds ?? []); ... setHiddenBigNewsIds(dismissedIds);` and :319-321 `.filter((news) => !news.dismissed && !hiddenBigNewsIds.has(news.id))`).`
- **Impact:** Every big-news card a player dismisses (manually or by the 8s auto-dismiss timer, useTransientGameFeedback.ts:241-244 -> dismissBigNewsItem -> dismissedBigNewsIds) leaves a `bn_N` id in the save. After reloading that save, `syncBigNewsIdFromState` sees an empty bigNews list, so the counter restarts at 1 (and the worker thread, which never syncs at all, also starts at 1) and re-issues ids that are already in the persisted dismissed set. `activeBigNews` filters on id, so the first N announcements after a load — festival starts, full-moon Howler warnings, raid-incoming banners — are never shown, where N is the number of announcements dismissed in the previous session (hundreds after a long game). No test covers this (grep of tests for `bigNews`/`dismissedBigNews` returns nothing).
- **Recommended change:** Make ids collision-proof across sessions: change `syncBigNewsIdFromState` to take `Pick<WorldState,'bigNews'|'dismissedBigNewsIds'>` and also scan `state.dismissedBigNewsIds` for `/^bn_(\d+)$/` when computing `maxSeq`, and call it from the worker's session/import path (`src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.ts` `resetWorkerSession`/`importSave` handler) as well as from `loadGameFromParsed`; alternatively mint tick-scoped ids (`bn_${state.tick}_${seq}`) and update both regexes in syncBigNewsIdFromState.
- **Verification:** syncBigNewsIdFromState (simEffects.ts:84-94) derives maxSeq only from state.bigNews, and the load path empties that array (saveLoad.ts:367 `bigNews: []`) before calling it at :446, so the module counter restarts at 1 — in the worker too, since gameWorker.ts never calls it (grep finds it only in saveLoad/gameEngine) — while dismissedBigNewsIds is a persisted allow-listed field (saveSchema.ts:22) reused both as a display filter (useTransientGameFeedback.ts:304-311, 319-321) and as a delta filter (simDelta.ts:20-25). Re-issued bn_N ids already in that set are therefore dropped for the first N announcements after a load. Fix: also scan state.dismissedBigNewsIds for /^bn_(\d+)$/ in syncBigNewsIdFromState and call it from the worker import path, or mint tick-scoped ids; severity medium because it silently hides UI cards but corrupts no state and self-heals once the counter passes the old maximum.

#### M27. Commute path branch moves the entity and then the caller's single movement apply moves it again

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/humanMovement.ts` — lines 142-157 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Impact:** Verified: steerWithPath documents 'path' as 'caller must not move it again' (pathfinding.ts:298-299) and itself applies `entity.x += entity.vx` (341-344), but commuteHumanToBuilding returns plain `false` for 'path' (150) and the humanTick commute callers ignore the return value (e.g. 768) while the loop-bottom integration `entity.x += entity.vx * roadMult` (1467) always runs — the only `continue`s are at lines 317-513, all before the commute block. Pathed commuters therefore integrate the same velocity twice per tick (~2x off-road; ~1.67x on road, not the cited ~2.1x). Minimal fix: return a tri-state and let the caller skip its integration, or zero vx/vy before returning false.
- **Recommended change:** return a tri-state and let the caller skip its integration, or zero vx/vy before returning false.
- **Verification:** Verified: steerWithPath documents 'path' as 'caller must not move it again' (pathfinding.ts:298-299) and itself applies `entity.x += entity.vx` (341-344), but commuteHumanToBuilding returns plain `false` for 'path' (150) and the humanTick commute callers ignore the return value (e.g. 768) while the loop-bottom integration `entity.x += entity.vx * roadMult` (1467) always runs — the only `continue`s are at lines 317-513, all before the commute block. Pathed commuters therefore integrate the same velocity twice per tick (~2x off-road; ~1.67x on road, not the cited ~2.1x). Minimal fix: return a tri-state and let the caller skip its integration, or zero vx/vy before returning false.

#### M28. Affair conception path creates ages 14-17 pregnancies without the documented youth-love gate

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/humanRelationships.ts` — lines 789-799 · `invariant-risk` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `lines 789-799: the affair branch gates only on `hasAffairPartner(entity, ctx.entityById) && entity.energy > ... && !isSpouseNearby(...)` and then rolls `if (Math.random() < HUMAN_DAILY_AFFAIR_PREGNANCY_CHANCE * fertility) {` (line 799). The youth branch directly above instead requires `entity.age >= HUMAN_FERTILITY_START && entity.age < HUMAN_YOUTH_FERTILITY_END` (lines 754-755).`
- **Impact:** isValidAffairTarget (line 622) admits targets from HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE = 16, so a 16-17-year-old single settler can become an established affair partner through the daily owner. tryDailyConception is called for every non-prisoner player human (humanTick.ts:330), so that settler then rolls the full adult affair rate 0.14 x fertility (fertility = 1 up to age 35) - roughly 45x (age 17) to 60x (age 16) the youth-gate chance (0.0045 x 0.50 at 16) - with no mutual youth-love link and no age check, contradicting SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md section 5: "At ages 14-17 it requires the documented mutual youth-love, nearby, energy, and reduced-probability gate".
- **Recommended change:** Restrict the affair branch to adults, e.g. add `&& !(entity.age >= HUMAN_FERTILITY_START && entity.age < HUMAN_YOUTH_FERTILITY_END)` to the condition at line 789 (or require `entity.age >= HUMAN_YOUTH_FERTILITY_END`), so ages 14-17 can only conceive through the youth branch.
- **Verification:** Verified in tryDailyConception: the affair branch (789-799) gates only on hasAffairPartner, energy, spouse distance and tryst site, then rolls HUMAN_DAILY_AFFAIR_PREGNANCY_CHANCE * fertility with no 14-17 restriction, whereas the youth branch above requires age 14-17 plus a mutual youth-love partner (753-786). isValidAffairTarget (617-627) admits targets from HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE = 16 and tryDailyAffairEncounter (1339-1443) establishes mutual affairPartnerId for such a paramour, so a 16-17-year-old mother conceives once per colony day (humanTick.ts:330-331) at 0.14 x fertility instead of the ~0.00225 youth rate, contradicting SIMULATION_AUTHORITY section 5. Minimal fix: add `&& entity.age >= HUMAN_YOUTH_FERTILITY_END` (or an equivalent non-youth guard) to the affair branch.

#### M29. StripPlacementPiece.replacesBuildingId is never written, so the wall/gate replacement rule and its 50% refund are unreachable and a gate dropped on a wall silently places nothing

- **File:** `src/game/stripTopology.ts` — lines 131, 157-162 (consumer: buildingPlacementActions.ts 207-223, 269-284) | 156-162 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `line 159: `if (existing && (existing.type === stripType || existing.type === BuildingType.Wall)) {` — `existing` comes from `findStripBuildingAt(..., 'wall')` whose family set is `WALL_STRIP_TYPES = {Wall, WallGate}` (stripTopology.ts:21-24, 229-233).`
- **Impact:** The guard is asymmetric. Dragging a Wall run: existing Wall -> skipped (`existing.type === BuildingType.Wall`), the run continues; existing WallGate -> neither clause is true, so a piece is emitted at/near the gate, `buildStripPreview` marks it invalid (overlapsAnyBuilding sees the gate), and because the UI commits only when `preview.segments.every((seg) => seg.valid)` (useCanvasInteractions.ts:166 and :479) the player gets NO wall at all — not even the segments that were clear of the gate. Dragging a WallGate run over an existing Wall is handled (second clause), which shows the intended 'same strip family' rule.
- **Recommended change:** Skip any existing wall-family piece: `if (existing && isWallStripType(existing.type)) {` (the local helper already exists at line 28 and covers Wall + WallGate).
- **Verification:** A workspace grep finds `replacesBuildingId` only as a declaration (stripTopology.ts:131, stripBuild.ts:29) and as readers (buildingPlacementActions.ts:207-230, 269-284) — no producer ever assigns it, so the replacement/refund path is dead. resolveWallStripPlan (stripTopology.ts:159-162) drops the piece whenever the existing building is any wall-family piece, including a plain Wall, so dragging a WallGate over a Wall yields an empty plan and useCanvasInteractions.ts:479 sends nothing: no gate, no 50% refund, no feedback. Minimal fix: emit `{type: stripType, x, y, rotation: stripRotation, replacesBuildingId: existing.id}` for the Wall→WallGate case, or delete the field and both unreachable branches.

#### M30. Wall-run planning skips existing walls but not existing wall gates, so a run that touches a gate is refused wholesale

- **File:** `src/game/stripTopology.ts` — lines 156-162 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `line 159: `if (existing && (existing.type === stripType || existing.type === BuildingType.Wall)) {` — `existing` comes from `findStripBuildingAt(..., 'wall')` whose family set is `WALL_STRIP_TYPES = {Wall, WallGate}` (stripTopology.ts:21-24, 229-233).`
- **Impact:** The guard is asymmetric. Dragging a Wall run: existing Wall -> skipped (`existing.type === BuildingType.Wall`), the run continues; existing WallGate -> neither clause is true, so a piece is emitted at/near the gate, `buildStripPreview` marks it invalid (overlapsAnyBuilding sees the gate), and because the UI commits only when `preview.segments.every((seg) => seg.valid)` (useCanvasInteractions.ts:166 and :479) the player gets NO wall at all — not even the segments that were clear of the gate. Dragging a WallGate run over an existing Wall is handled (second clause), which shows the intended 'same strip family' rule.
- **Recommended change:** Skip any existing wall-family piece: `if (existing && isWallStripType(existing.type)) {` (the local helper already exists at line 28 and covers Wall + WallGate).
- **Verification:** Verified in resolveWallStripPlan: the skip test at line 159 covers `existing.type === stripType || existing.type === BuildingType.Wall`, but findStripBuildingAt(..., 'wall') matches WALL_STRIP_TYPES = {Wall, WallGate} (21-24, 229-241), so an existing WallGate is never skipped. Wall and WallGate both snap x to multiples of 60 and y to the shared grid (snapBuildingCenter, buildingRotation.ts:63-83), so any Wall drag spanning a gate emits a piece exactly on it; that segment fails canPlaceBuilding via overlapsAnyBuilding (placementUtils.ts:131) and useCanvasInteractions.ts:166/479 commits only when `preview.segments.every(seg => seg.valid)`, so the whole run — including clear segments — is silently refused. Minimal fix: `if (existing && isWallStripType(existing.type))` (helper already at line 28).

#### M31. Off-screen wildlife throttle counts ticks, not wildlife-layer calls, so it was never rescaled for the 72-tick day / 4-tick layer

- **File:** `src/game/tickLayerSystems.ts` — lines 253-254 (candidate said 252-254) | 252-254 · `cadence-violation` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `src/game/tickLayerSystems.ts:253-254: `const wildlifeActive = wildlifeInFocus || (state.tick + entity.id) % OFFSCREEN_WILDLIFE_THROTTLE === 0;`
src/game/simFocus.ts:25-26: `/** How often off-screen wildlife runs full AI (every N wildlife-layer calls). */ export const OFFSCREEN_WILDLIFE_THROTTLE = 8;``
- **Impact:** tickWildlife runs only when `state.tick % WILDLIFE_LAYER_INTERVAL === 0` (LAYER_SYSTEMS_INTERVAL = 4, gameTick.ts:198), but the throttle is tested against the raw tick, so off-screen fauna get full AI on every second layer pulse (every 8 ticks), not one in eight pulses as the constant's declared unit states. The sibling OFFSCREEN_HUMAN_THROTTLE was explicitly rescaled for TICKS_PER_DAY=72 (8 -> 24, locked by tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts:95-99) but this one was left at 8, so off-screen wildlife decisions per in-game day are 3x the legacy rate (9/day vs 3/day) and the off-screen AI budget is 4x the declared one.
- **Recommended change:** Either count layer pulses (`(Math.floor(state.tick / WILDLIFE_LAYER_INTERVAL) + entity.id) % OFFSCREEN_WILDLIFE_THROTTLE === 0`) or keep tick units and set the constant to `8 * TICKS_PER_HOUR`; update the docstring and extend tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts with the wildlife throttle assertion.
- **Verification:** gameTick.ts:198 runs tickLayerSystems only when state.tick % 4 === 0, while tickLayerSystems.ts:253-254 tests (state.tick + entity.id) % 8 on the raw tick, and simFocus.ts:25 states the unit as 'every N wildlife-layer calls'; the human sibling was rescaled to 24 and is locked by tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts:95-99. The consequence is worse than the claimed 3x rate: since the layer tick is always a multiple of 4, (tick+id) % 8 === 0 has no solution for ids ≡ 1, 2 or 3 (mod 4), so ~75% of out-of-focus fauna never pass the throttle gate (the `continue` at 255-259 skips movement, hunting, grazing at 526-571 and reproduction at 681-736) while the unconditional metabolic drain at line 242 still runs every pulse — they starve off-camera; the ids that do pass (≡ 0 mod 4) run AI every second pulse, not one in eight. Minimal fix: gate on the layer-pulse index, e.g. (Math.floor(state.tick / WILDLIFE_LAYER_INTERVAL) + entity.id) % OFFSCREEN_WILDLIFE_THROTTLE === 0, or keep tick units and set the constant to 8 * TICKS_PER_HOUR with the docstring corrected, and add the assertion to tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts.

#### M32. availableScripts uses eventLog.slice(-40), which is the oldest 40 entries of a newest-first log, not the recent history the comment claims

- **File:** `src/game/travelingTheatre.ts` — lines 61-79 | 61-78 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `62: `const recent = state.eventLog.slice(-40);` with the local named `recent` and the module comment promising 'script selection from real history' (line 4). eventLog is newest-first: eventLog.ts:23 `state.eventLog.unshift({...})`, and simDelta.ts:236 takes `world.eventLog.slice(0, EVENT_LOG_DELTA_TAIL_MAX)` as the recent tail. `slice(-40)` therefore returns the last 40 entries of the array, i.e. the colony's oldest recorded events.`
- **Impact:** The theatre only becomes eligible at colony day 50-110 (MIN_DAY 50 + seeded window, line 23/54) by which time the 2000-entry log holds far more than 40 entries, so the offered plays (and the /foot/i famine detection at 75) are decided by the first days of the colony and freeze permanently — new disasters/combats/scandals can no longer unlock a script. If those oldest entries yield no script, the function returns at 89 without setting any flag and retries the same dead history every day, so the story never starts.
- **Recommended change:** `const recent = state.eventLog.slice(0, 40);`
- **Verification:** The event log is newest-first (logEvent unshifts, eventLog.ts:23; simDelta takes slice(0,n) as the tail, simBuffers/simDelta.ts:236; EventLogPanel renders from the front, EventLogPanel.tsx:66-73), so travelingTheatre.ts:62's slice(-40) selects the 40 OLDEST retained entries out of up to 2000. Script availability (64-77) is therefore decided by early-game history, and since maybeOfferTravelingTheatre returns early when the script list is empty (88-89) the whole theatre story can be suppressed forever once the first 40 events contain no matching type. Minimal fix: `const recent = state.eventLog.slice(0, 40);`.

#### M33. Time-cyclic `winter_watch` predicate makes the first-spring guide regress and reappear every year instead of finishing

- **File:** `src/game/tutorialCampaign.ts` — lines 66, 78-80 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L66: `isComplete: (w) => w.dayInYear >= 250,`  L79: `const idx = TUTORIAL_CAMPAIGN.findIndex((s) => !s.isComplete(world));`  L80: `return idx === -1 ? null : TUTORIAL_CAMPAIGN[idx];` — `dayInYear` is `getCalendarDay(tick)` (gameTick.ts:77), which wraps modulo `DAYS_PER_YEAR` (dayCycleClock.ts:23-26), so it returns to 0 at every year boundary.`
- **Impact:** The step list assumes predicates only ever go false→true, but `winter_watch` goes true→false every new year. In year 2 day 0 the later `year_two` step (`w.year >= 2`) stays complete while `winter_watch` becomes incomplete again, so `findIndex` returns index 5 and `currentCampaignStep` reports `winter_watch`: App.tsx:1645-1652 renders TutorialCampaignBanner as 'Guide 6/7 — Prepare for winter' for the first 250 days of every year from year 2 onward, and the condition `campaignStep === null` that App.tsx:417-419 documents ('the banner simply stops rendering') is never reached outside winter.
- **Recommended change:** Make the predicate monotonic: `isComplete: (w) => w.year >= 2 || w.dayInYear >= 250,` (keeps tests/tutorialCampaign.test.ts:92-111 green and stops the regression).
- **Verification:** winter_watch's predicate is `w.dayInYear >= 250` (tutorialCampaign.ts:66) and dayInYear is assigned from getCalendarDay in gameTick.ts:77, which wraps modulo DAYS_PER_YEAR (dayCycleClock.ts:23-26); so at the start of every year >= 2 the step goes true -> false while year_two stays complete, and currentCampaignStep's findIndex (line 79) returns index 5, rendering 'Guide 6/7 - Prepare for winter' for days 0-249 of every year and never reaching the null/finished state App.tsx:417-419 documents. tests/tutorialCampaign.test.ts only exercises year 2 with dayInYear = 260 (lines 102-111), so nothing locks the regression. Minimal fix: make the predicate monotonic, `isComplete: (w) => w.year >= 2 || w.dayInYear >= 250`, which keeps both existing assertions green.

#### M34. chronicleChapters is written by the daily layer but carried by neither the worker tick delta nor the worker prep/rollback snapshot

- **File:** `src/game/valleyChronicle.ts` — lines 89; missing in src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts:52-139 and src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts:10-87 | 89 (write); missing in src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts field list 109-133 and src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts 58-87 | 83-99 (89) · `save-persistence` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `89:       state.chronicleChapters = [...(state.chronicleChapters ?? []), ch.id];   (SimTickDelta lists challenges/storyFlags/pendingStoryEvents but has no chronicleChapters key; SimPrepKeys likewise omits it)`
- **Impact:** In the default worker mode the main-thread world is updated only by applySimTickDelta (GameWorkerHost.ts:546) and is never given chronicleChapters, so ValleyChroniclePanel.tsx:6-8 keeps showing 0/N chapters completed and the chapter-unlock card in useTransientGameFeedback.ts:83-86 never fires (it only appears if a full save export later replaces the display world, gameLoop.ts:675). Secondarily, because SimPrepKeys omits the field, a failed tick's rollback (gameWorker.ts:355 applySimPrep) restores resources but leaves the chapter id recorded, so the chapter reward can never be granted again.
- **Recommended change:** Add chronicleChapters to SimTickDelta (interface, extractSimTickDelta, applySimTickDelta) and to SimPrepKeys/extractSimPrep/applySimPrep, the same way storyFlags/pendingStoryEvents are carried; extend tests/simDelta.storyRoundtrip.test.ts to assert the round-trip.
- **Verification:** advanceValleyChronicle writes state.chronicleChapters (valleyChronicle.ts:89) and is called from tickLayerDaily:65, but SimTickDelta has no chronicleChapters key (grep for 'chronicle' in simDelta.ts returns nothing) and neither does SimPrepKeys/extractSimPrep/applySimPrep, although the field is in the save allow-list (saveSchema.ts:15). In the default worker mode (isGameWorkerEnabled returns true unless VITE_USE_GAME_WORKER is off) the main-thread world is only updated by applySimTickDelta, so ValleyChroniclePanel.tsx:6 and useTransientGameFeedback.ts:83/143 never see progress — the panel stays 0/N and the unlock card never fires; the prep gap additionally lets a failed tick record a chapter without its reward. Fix: carry chronicleChapters in the delta and the prep snapshot exactly like storyFlags/pendingStoryEvents (and extend tests/simDelta.storyRoundtrip.test.ts); severity medium because the simulation state and rewards are correct and only the display/rollback consistency is broken.

#### M35. Zero-candidate election reveal clears villageLeaderId without stepping the office down or scheduling a successor election

- **File:** `src/game/villageLeadership.ts` — lines 791-802; tickLeaderVacancy 888-905; leaderHouse.ts:42-79 | 891-900 (with 902-905 as the only place that schedules an election; caller src/game/dailyWorldEvents.ts:230) · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `const leaderId = state.villageLeaderId;
  if (leaderId == null) return null;
...
  if (!state.entities.some((entity) => isEligibleForLeadership(entity, state))) {
    state.villageLeaderId = null;
    return null;
  }`
- **Impact:** When the leader dies (or is otherwise non-acting) and every remaining adult is ineligible (e.g. the only other adult is serving a scandal sentence, or only juveniles remain - isEligibleForLeadership requires alive/Human/player/adult/not imprisoned), this branch nulls villageLeaderId and returns WITHOUT setting pendingElectionYear. Every later daily call then returns at the `leaderId == null` guard, so the vacancy-election schedule is never re-established even after settlers grow up or are released from prison: the colony has no head, no 'leadership vacancy' notice (focusHints.ts:109 and getElectionCeremonyStatus both require pendingElectionYear != null), and only reaches a leader again at the next even-year term election (tryStartTermElectionCeremony requires year % 2 === 0) - up to 2 game years with no election, while the leader invariant check (simulationInvariants.ts:271) is silently skipped because villageLeaderId is null. tickLeaderVacancy is also the only scheduler of pendingElectionYear besides save-load (validateVillageLeaderOnLoad:1019), so this state survives until the player reloads.
- **Recommended change:** Schedule the vacancy election in this branch instead of only clearing the office, i.e. replace the early `return null` with the same `state.pendingElectionYear = state.year + (state.dayInYear ?? 0) / DAYS_PER_YEAR + VACANCY_ELECTION_DELAY_YEARS` write used below (keeping the office cleared and logging the vacancy), or re-arm when `state.villageLeaderId == null && state.pendingElectionYear == null && state.lastElectionYear !== 0`.
- **Verification:** runVillageElection's zero-candidate branch (villageLeadership.ts:792-802) clears villageLeaderId without calling applyLeaderOccupation(prevId) or syncLeaderHouseResidency and without setting pendingElectionYear, and tickLeaderVacancy returns immediately when leaderId == null (892), so no successor election is ever scheduled; the deposition is reachable because startElectionCeremony only needs one candidate at start (629-639) while the ~60-day gossip/tension phases (getPhaseTicks:39-49) can outlive or imprison every candidate. The deposed incumbent keeps occupation 'leader' indefinitely because applyLeaderOccupation only steps down the id passed in (leaderHouse.ts:42-52) and workforce's keepOffice preserves the label (workforce.ts:206-209, 260-262). Minimal fix: capture prevId, call applyLeaderOccupation + syncLeaderHouseResidency, and schedule the successor using the vacancy delay.

#### M36. Rival-owned watchtowers count as player early-warning towers and reveal their own raiders

- **File:** `src/game/watchtowerDetection.ts` — lines 15 · `logic-error` · confirmed
