- **Impact:** Verified: detectRaidersFromWatchtowers filters only `b.completed && b.type === Watchtower` (15), unlike countCompletedDefenseBuildings which excludes `faction === 'rival'` (defenseStructures.ts:54-56), and rival camps do build completed faction-'rival' Watchtowers (createRivalBuilding, groupEvents.ts:308-324; picked for competitive/tense camps in rivalEvents.ts:107-114) in slots within ~90px of their own camp (rivalEvents.ts:35-44). The daily scan (dailyWorldEvents.ts:134) therefore clears hiddenFromPlayer and sets detectedByPatrol for the camp's own marching war-band (36-41), which entityCache.ts:86 renders and humanTick.ts:472 keeps sticky, giving free player early warning while the test only uses a faction-less tower. Minimal fix: add `&& b.faction !== 'rival'`.
- **Recommended change:** add `&& b.faction !== 'rival'`.
- **Verification:** Verified: detectRaidersFromWatchtowers filters only `b.completed && b.type === Watchtower` (15), unlike countCompletedDefenseBuildings which excludes `faction === 'rival'` (defenseStructures.ts:54-56), and rival camps do build completed faction-'rival' Watchtowers (createRivalBuilding, groupEvents.ts:308-324; picked for competitive/tense camps in rivalEvents.ts:107-114) in slots within ~90px of their own camp (rivalEvents.ts:35-44). The daily scan (dailyWorldEvents.ts:134) therefore clears hiddenFromPlayer and sets detectedByPatrol for the camp's own marching war-band (36-41), which entityCache.ts:86 renders and humanTick.ts:472 keeps sticky, giving free player early warning while the test only uses a faction-less tower. Minimal fix: add `&& b.faction !== 'rival'`.

#### M37. removeWorkerTransition strips the settler out of residence occupants, breaking the residence↔occupants invariant

- **File:** `src/game/workforce.ts` — lines 219-229 · `invariant-risk` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `L220-225: "for (let i = 0; i < buildings.length; i++) {\n    const building = buildings[i];\n    if (building.occupants.includes(human.id)) {\n      building.occupants = building.occupants.filter((id) => id !== human.id);\n    }\n  }" — the loop covers every building (completed House/Mansion/LeaderHouse included) while only `human.homeBuildingId` is cleared at L226.`
- **Impact:** A housed settler released from a job is deleted from their home's occupants list while `residenceBuildingId` still points at that home, so SIMULATION_AUTHORITY §5 residence occupancy is false. `collectSimulationInvariantErrors` reports "human N residenceBuildingId H missing from that residence's occupants" (simulationInvariants.ts:191-194) and the dev pulse in gameTick.ts:282 logs it. Reachable from (a) the player command `removeWorkerFromBuilding` (buildingStaffingActions.ts:290), (b) the assignment layer's builder soft-steal `clearJobAssignment` (L385-387) called from assignBuilderInPlace L449, and (c) `clearAutoFilledChurches` on save load (saveLoad.ts:531), which runs after the load-time residence sync at saveLoad.ts:486 so the mismatch survives into the session. Until the next LAYER_ASSIGN_INTERVAL pulse (≤18 ticks) `collectHousingDiagnostics` (housingDiagnostics.ts:37,56) and `getResidenceCapacity(b) - b.occupants.length` bed checks (storyEvents.ts:50,319) read a phantom open bed, and it also writes `residence building.occupants`, a field decisionRegistry.ts:84 declares housing-owned.
- **Recommended change:** Inside the loop, skip buildings that are not workplaces or crews: `if (building.completed && !BUILDING_JOB_TYPES[building.type]) continue;` so the residence list is left to `syncResidenceOccupants`.
- **Verification:** removeWorkerTransition (workforce.ts:219-229) filters the human id out of every building's occupants, and residence lists use the same field (syncResidenceOccupants, residencyReconciliation.ts:112-120, filters to residenceBuildingId), while only homeBuildingId is cleared at 226 — so a housed settler removed from a job is deleted from their house's occupants while residenceBuildingId still points at it, which collectSimulationInvariantErrors reports as "human N residenceBuildingId H missing from that residence's occupants" (simulationInvariants.ts:189-194). Real callers pass the full list: buildingStaffingActions.ts:290, clearJobAssignment (workforce.ts:385-387) from assignBuilderInPlace:449, and saveLoad.ts:328 (clearAutoFilledChurches, called at saveLoad.ts:531 *after* the load-time residence sync at 486). The existing test tests/workforce.transitions.test.ts:288-303 asserts invariants stay clean after removal but only passes `[farmB]`, so it does not lock the all-buildings behaviour in. Downgraded from high to medium because residence occupant lists are a UI mirror, housing decisions read residenceBuildingId (countResidentsInBuilding, residencyOccupancy.ts:83), and the mismatch is repaired by the next LAYER_ASSIGN_INTERVAL pulse (tickLayerAssign.ts:35, ≤18 ticks) — but storyEvents.countFreeShelterBeds (storyEvents.ts:47-51) can read a phantom bed if the strip happens later in the same daily tick. Fix: skip non-workplace buildings in the loop.

#### M38. Storm damage heals any building that is already below the 20 HP floor and hides it from the storm damage report

- **File:** `src/game/worldEvents.ts` — lines 117-120 | 253-259 · `logic-error` · confirmed
- **Evidence:** `253:     } else if (type === 'earthquake') {
254:       impulseScreenShake(state, 15);
255:       for (let i = 0; i < state.buildings.length; i++) {
256:         const b = state.buildings[i];
257:         const currentHp = b.health ?? b.maxHealth;
258:         b.health = Math.max(10, Math.round(currentHp - 15 * resistMult));`
- **Impact:** Every other damage branch tests the stored radius (`dx*dx+dy*dy < radiusSq`: fire line 212, flood 232, tornado 245, plague 267), and the storm owner explicitly skips incomplete and rival buildings (line 116). The earthquake applies 15*resistMult damage to all buildings regardless of distance, completion or faction, so the x/y/radius pushed at lines 181-188 for the quake has no effect and a 'quake' anywhere damages distant rival structures.
- **Recommended change:** Skip buildings outside the radius and non-player/incomplete ones the way the storm path does, e.g. compute dx/dy and `if (dx*dx + dy*dy >= radiusSq) continue;` (plus `if (!b.completed || b.faction === 'rival') continue;`) before applying damage.
- **Verification:** Verified in applyStormDamageToBuildings: `b.health = Math.max(20, before - dmg)` (119) raises a building already below 20 HP, and buildings do sit there — raids floor at 5 (frontierCombat.ts:510) and fire/earthquake at 10 (worldEvents.ts:214, 258) — after which `b.health < before` (120) is false so the building is omitted from `damaged` and gets no 'Storm damage!' float and no notification (133-159). The existing test only pins 25 -> 20 (weatherConsequences.test.ts:66-70). Minimal fix: compute `b.health = Math.min(before, Math.max(20, before - dmg))`.

</details>

<details>
<summary>Low findings — impact and recommended change</summary>

#### L1. Candidate metric is counted before the predicate in grid mode but after it in naive mode

- **File:** `src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts` — lines 123-136 (with 62-63) · `logic-error` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Impact:** Verified: forEachAdaptiveInRadius's grid branch (125-128) calls grid.forEachInRadius with the default `recordCandidates = true` (spatialGrid.ts:295, 329), which counts every in-radius entity before the caller's predicate at line 126, whereas the naive branch counts after the predicate (56/63) and findClosestInRadius passes false and records after (spatialGrid.ts:383-394). The mismatch only inflates the opt-in benchmark report (spatialQueryMetrics.ts:224-251), not gameplay, so it is a low-severity diagnostics defect. Minimal fix: pass `false` as forEachInRadius's last argument and call recordSpatialCandidate() inside the callback after the predicate.
- **Recommended change:** pass `false` as forEachInRadius's last argument and call recordSpatialCandidate() inside the callback after the predicate.

#### L2. Tamed animals whose owner died keep eating rations forever and can never be hunted

- **File:** `src/game/animalCare.ts` — lines 35-41, 82-90 · `logic-error` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Impact:** `countTamedAnimals` counts every living entity with `tamedBy != null` without checking the owner (38), and the owner lookup at 84-86 silently yields nothing when the owner is dead, so such a pet contributes to the daily `tamed * ANIMAL_DAILY_FOOD` ration with no energy bonus; the only writer that clears `tamedBy` is `cureMoonHowler` (moonHowler.ts:533) and no death path clears it, while `isValidHuntPrey` rejects any prey with `tamedBy != null` (simulationEntities.ts:60). The result is a permanent food sink and un-huntable animals in long games — real but small and self-inflicted only when a pet outlives its owner. Fix: count only pets whose `tamedBy` resolves to a living human (reuse the lookup `tickAnimalCare` already performs), or clear `tamedBy` in the human-death cleanup owner.
- **Recommended change:** count only pets whose `tamedBy` resolves to a living human (reuse the lookup `tickAnimalCare` already performs), or clear `tamedBy` in the human-death cleanup owner.

#### L3. Demolition reports the nominal refund even when the capped add added nothing

- **File:** `src/game/buildingMaintenanceActions.ts` — lines 188-201 | 185-201 · `resource-economy` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Evidence:** `lines 188-190 `addResource(state, 'wood', refundWood);` (its capped return value is discarded) followed by line 201 `addFloatingText(state, building.x, building.y - 10, \`Refunded: ${refundWood}w ${refundStone}s\`, '#eab308');`.`
- **Impact:** `addResource` -> `addCappedResource` (resourceUtils.ts:38-48) clamps to `storageMax` and returns the amount actually added. With a full wood store (worldGen.ts:577 caps wood at 1000, stone at 500) demolishing a Wall reports 'Refunded: 4w 7s' while 0 wood and 0 stone were received, so the player-facing ledger disagrees with the authoritative resources.
- **Recommended change:** Capture the returns and print what was actually granted, e.g. `const gotWood = addResource(state, 'wood', refundWood); const gotStone = addResource(state, 'stone', refundStone);` and use `gotWood`/`gotStone` in the floating text.

#### L4. Third copy of UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN is exported but no consumer reads it

- **File:** `src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts` — lines 25-34 | 25-32 · `dead-code` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Evidence:** `lines 25-32 `export const UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN = new Set<TerrainType>([...])` plus line 34 `export { isFootprintOnBuildableTerrain, isFootprintWithinMapBounds } from './placementUtils';`; the same set is defined privately at placementUtils.ts:10-17 (used by `isUnbuildableTerrainType`, line 25) and a third time at terrainGen.ts:113. Nothing imports the exported set — the only other references are the pass-through re-exports in buildingActions.ts:19 and gameEngine.ts:245.`
- **Impact:** The authoritative terrain rule lives in placementUtils (`UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN` + `isUnbuildableTerrainType`), so a new unbuildable terrain type added there will not affect this file's copy or terrainGen's, and the public façade advertises a stale second source of truth for placement legality. Any future caller that imports `UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN` from buildingActions/gameEngine gets one of the copies rather than the owner's predicate.
- **Recommended change:** Delete lines 25-32 and the `UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN` entry in the buildingActions/gameEngine re-export lists, and import `isUnbuildableTerrainType` from placementUtils where a membership test is needed.

#### L5. buildings.ts exports a second, unused getBuildingConfig with a different contract

- **File:** `src/game/buildings.ts` — lines 686-696 · `logic-error` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Impact:** buildings.ts:687-690 returns `BUILDING_CONFIGS[type]` (possibly undefined) while buildingConfig.ts:18-20 returns the same value `?? FALLBACK_BUILDING_CONFIG`, and a repo-wide grep shows every consumer (GameBuildRail, SelectedBuildingPanel, SelectedEntityPanel) imports the buildingConfig version — nothing imports the buildings.ts one, nor isDecorBuilding (693), isResidentialBuilding/isResidenceBuilding (699-709, distinct from the widely used residencyOccupancy.ts:7) or isUniqueBuilding (712). It is a real single-source-of-truth violation with no runtime effect today, hence low; minimal fix: delete the unused accessor and predicates or re-export buildingConfig's version.
- **Recommended change:** delete the unused accessor and predicates or re-export buildingConfig's version.

#### L6. Best-skill candidate scan in _assignIdleWorkerToBuildingMut is unreachable

- **File:** `src/game/buildingStaffingActions.ts` — lines 152-166 (guard at 153) | 152-166 · `dead-code` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Evidence:** `L153: "if (!idleHuman && preferredHumanId === undefined) {" guards an O(N) best-skill scan (L154-165), but `idleHuman` was set on L146-150 by pickAdultSettler, which always returns the first matching entity as a fallback (L49-51); the scan re-applies exactly the same predicate (L157 `isEligibleIdleWorker(entity, state)`), so the guard is true only when the scan can find nothing.`
- **Impact:** The documented optimisation on L152 ("O(N) scan for best skill instead of O(N log N) full array sort") never executes; the fallback manual worker is always the first eligible settler in entity order, so the intended highest-skill preference for manual assignment is silently lost (also two full entity scans are implied where a comment claims one).
- **Recommended change:** Delete L152-166, or change pickAdultSettler to return only an explicit preferred match and keep the scan as the genuine fallback selection.

#### L7. canAssignWorkerToBuilding's builder preview omits hasWorkAssignment/isImprisoned, enabling a button that always fails

- **File:** `src/game/buildingStaffingActions.ts` — lines 320-330 · `logic-error` · **fixed — B1 staffing (with M4)**
- **Evidence:** `L322-330: "return building.occupants.length < cap && state.entities.some(\n      (entity) =>\n        isPlayerHuman(entity)\n        && entity.alive\n        && !entity.isJuvenile\n        && !entity.pregnant\n        && !building.occupants.includes(entity.id)\n        && !isOnConstructionCrew(state, entity.id, building.id),\n    );" — the mutation path it gates requires `!hasWorkAssignment(entity)` (L92) and the shared predicate L58-68 does too.`
- **Impact:** In a village where every adult already holds a job, canAssignWorkerToBuilding returns true for an unfinished building, so App.tsx:1790 enables the "+ Fill builders" button (SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:673); clicking sends `assignWorker` (App.tsx:1754) into _applyBuilderAssignmentMut, which finds no builder (`!hasWorkAssignment`), assigns nobody and floats "No idle settlers!" (L97-100). The player is offered an action that can never succeed. The same function is the auto-play bot's eligibility test (virtualPlayer.ts:373).
- **Recommended change:** Add `&& !hasWorkAssignment(entity)` to the builder-branch filter (L324-329) so it mirrors _applyBuilderAssignmentMut and isEligibleIdleWorker.

#### L8. removeStaffWorkerFromBuilding never validates that the human belongs to the building

- **File:** `src/game/buildingStaffingActions.ts` — lines 285-290 | 320-330 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `L322-330: "return building.occupants.length < cap && state.entities.some(\n      (entity) =>\n        isPlayerHuman(entity)\n        && entity.alive\n        && !entity.isJuvenile\n        && !entity.pregnant\n        && !building.occupants.includes(entity.id)\n        && !isOnConstructionCrew(state, entity.id, building.id),\n    );" — the mutation path it gates requires `!hasWorkAssignment(entity)` (L92) and the shared predicate L58-68 does too.`
- **Impact:** In a village where every adult already holds a job, canAssignWorkerToBuilding returns true for an unfinished building, so App.tsx:1790 enables the "+ Fill builders" button (SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:673); clicking sends `assignWorker` (App.tsx:1754) into _applyBuilderAssignmentMut, which finds no builder (`!hasWorkAssignment`), assigns nobody and floats "No idle settlers!" (L97-100). The player is offered an action that can never succeed. The same function is the auto-play bot's eligibility test (virtualPlayer.ts:373).
- **Recommended change:** Add `&& !hasWorkAssignment(entity)` to the builder-branch filter (L324-329) so it mirrors _applyBuilderAssignmentMut and isEligibleIdleWorker.

#### L9. Town Hall tax base is built from state.entities, which still contains settlers who died earlier this tick

- **File:** `src/game/dailyBuildingEconomy.ts` — lines 767 | 767-769 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `L767 `const villagers = state.entities.filter(isPlayerHuman);` L768 `tickTownHallCivic(state, building, villagers);` L769 `tickTownHallAudiences(state, building, villagers);` — `isPlayerHuman` (playerHuman.ts:12-17) does not test `alive`, and gameTick only prunes `state.entities` at the very end of the tick (gameTick.ts:234 `state.entities = allAlive;`), after the daily layer at gameTick.ts:220-222. The same function already has an alive-filtered list in scope: L292 `const playerWorkers = allAlive.filter(isPlayerHuman);`.`
- **Impact:** A settler killed earlier in the same tick (predator, illness, old age) is still a Human with `alive === false` in `state.entities` during the daily layer, so `tickTownHallCivic` counts them in `const adults = playerHumans.filter((h) => !h.isJuvenile).length;` (townHall.ts:88) and pays `adults * 0.4` extra tax gold for that pulse; the dead entity is also handed to `tickTownHallAudiences`. tickTownHallAudiences happens to re-filter `h.alive` (townHall.ts:352), so the visible damage is limited to the tax count, but the module mixes two different population sources in one function.
- **Recommended change:** Pass the already-built alive-filtered list: `const villagers = playerWorkers;` (or `ctx.playerHumans`) at L767 instead of re-filtering `state.entities`.

#### L10. Town Hall daily pulse is fed from state.entities, so settlers who died earlier in the same tick are counted

- **File:** `src/game/dailyBuildingEconomy.ts` — lines 767-769 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-9 (same fix as L9)**
- **Evidence:** `L767 `const villagers = state.entities.filter(isPlayerHuman);` L768 `tickTownHallCivic(state, building, villagers);` L769 `tickTownHallAudiences(state, building, villagers);` — `isPlayerHuman` (playerHuman.ts:12-17) does not test `alive`, and gameTick only prunes `state.entities` at the very end of the tick (gameTick.ts:234 `state.entities = allAlive;`), after the daily layer at gameTick.ts:220-222. The same function already has an alive-filtered list in scope: L292 `const playerWorkers = allAlive.filter(isPlayerHuman);`.`
- **Impact:** A settler killed earlier in the same tick (predator, illness, old age) is still a Human with `alive === false` in `state.entities` during the daily layer, so `tickTownHallCivic` counts them in `const adults = playerHumans.filter((h) => !h.isJuvenile).length;` (townHall.ts:88) and pays `adults * 0.4` extra tax gold for that pulse; the dead entity is also handed to `tickTownHallAudiences`. tickTownHallAudiences happens to re-filter `h.alive` (townHall.ts:352), so the visible damage is limited to the tax count, but the module mixes two different population sources in one function.
- **Recommended change:** Pass the already-built alive-filtered list: `const villagers = playerWorkers;` (or `ctx.playerHumans`) at L767 instead of re-filtering `state.entities`.

#### L11. Challenge ledger records the nominal food reward instead of the amount storage actually accepted

- **File:** `src/game/dailyChallenges.ts` — lines 23-24 · `resource-economy` · **fixed — lead review**
- **Evidence:** `23:       recordFoodProduced(state, 'challenges', c.reward.food || 0);
24:       addResource(state, 'food', c.reward.food || 0);`
- **Impact:** addResource returns the quantity actually added (addCappedResource, resourceUtils.ts:44-47), and the established convention is to record the return value (dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:780: `recordFoodProduced(state, 'silos', addResource(state, 'food', amount))`). When food is near storageMax the ledger credits the full reward (up to +500 for 'century') while only the headroom entered storage, so the 'why is my food low' ledger (economyLedger.ts:52 'food that actually entered storage') over-reports produced food by the shortfall.
- **Recommended change:** Use the returned amount: `recordFoodProduced(state, 'challenges', addResource(state, 'food', c.reward.food || 0));` and drop the separate addResource call.

#### L12. The 'split a married couple' repair is unreachable, so canAdmitAll is always true

- **File:** `src/game/dailyPopulation.ts` — lines 82-95 | 85-95 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `86:     const canAdmitAll = newcomers.length <= (state.maxHumanPopulation - counts.humans);
87:     const toAdmit = canAdmitAll ? newcomers : newcomers.slice(0, 1);
90:     if (toAdmit.length === 1 && newcomers.length > 1) {`
- **Impact:** createImmigrantSettler is called with maxMembers = openSlots (line 82) and only returns a 2-member couple when `maxMembers >= 2` (worldGen.ts:493). Consequently a 2-person result implies openSlots >= 2, and the only other result is a single settler, so `newcomers.length <= maxHumanPopulation - counts.humans` is always true: the else-branch at 87 and the entire spouse-unlink block at 90-95 (setting relationshipStatus/partnerId/pregnant on newcomers[0], which is the never-pregnant husband) can never run. The dangling-partnerId protection it appears to provide is dead.
- **Recommended change:** Either delete lines 86-95 (createImmigrantSettler already caps by maxMembers) or, if the guard is meant to be real, make createImmigrantSettler able to exceed the passed cap (e.g. always roll the couple and let the caller truncate).

#### L13. Seasonal festival is cancelled for the whole season when a random festival is still active on its single check day

- **File:** `src/game/dailyWorldEvents.ts` — lines 46-64 (gate at 51) | 46-51 (random festival length at 75) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `49:   const dayInYear = getAbsoluteCalendarDay(state.tick) % DAYS_PER_YEAR;
50:   const seasonalStart = Math.floor(dayInYear / 90) * 90;
51:   if (!state.festival && dayInYear === seasonalStart + 3 && counts.humans >= 2) {`
- **Impact:** The comment at lines 46-48 promises '5 days at the start of each season: 20 guaranteed festival days per year', but the seasonal festival has exactly one eligible tick per season and the `!state.festival` condition blocks it whenever a random festival is active. Random festivals last 20-39 days (line 75) and can be running on days 3 (a day-350 start carrying over), 183 (a day-150 start) and 273 (a day-250 start), and there is no retry, so that season's seasonal festival is silently lost - the 'guaranteed' 20 days can be 0, 5 or 10.
- **Recommended change:** Record a pending seasonal festival (or a per-season flag) and start it on the first free day, e.g. `if (!state.festival && dayInYear >= seasonalStart + 3 && dayInYear < seasonalStart + 8 && !state.seasonalFestivalDone?.includes(seasonalStart))` setting the flag when it starts.

#### L14. Seasonal festivals are not actually guaranteed; a lone eligible tick can be blocked by a random festival

- **File:** `src/game/dailyWorldEvents.ts` — lines 46-51 (random festival length at 75) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `49:   const dayInYear = getAbsoluteCalendarDay(state.tick) % DAYS_PER_YEAR;
50:   const seasonalStart = Math.floor(dayInYear / 90) * 90;
51:   if (!state.festival && dayInYear === seasonalStart + 3 && counts.humans >= 2) {`
- **Impact:** The comment at lines 46-48 promises '5 days at the start of each season: 20 guaranteed festival days per year', but the seasonal festival has exactly one eligible tick per season and the `!state.festival` condition blocks it whenever a random festival is active. Random festivals last 20-39 days (line 75) and can be running on days 3 (a day-350 start carrying over), 183 (a day-150 start) and 273 (a day-250 start), and there is no retry, so that season's seasonal festival is silently lost - the 'guaranteed' 20 days can be 0, 5 or 10.
- **Recommended change:** Record a pending seasonal festival (or a per-season flag) and start it on the first free day, e.g. `if (!state.festival && dayInYear >= seasonalStart + 3 && dayInYear < seasonalStart + 8 && !state.seasonalFestivalDone?.includes(seasonalStart))` setting the flag when it starts.

#### L15. pruneDeadBarracksOccupants has no callers, so the only barracks-occupant repair never runs

- **File:** `src/game/defenseStructures.ts` — lines 113-137 (definition 117) | 113-137 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `114:  * Removes dead / missing occupants from barracks. Call this during tick
115:  * or load - NOT inside a getter.
117: export function pruneDeadBarracksOccupants(state: WorldState, buildings: Building[]): void {
...
129:       if (human && human.alive && human.job === JobType.Soldier && !isImprisoned(human)) {
130:         liveOccupants.push(humanId);
...
134:       b.occupants = liveOccupants;`
- **Impact:** A whole-repo search finds no caller (only the declaration at line 117), so the documented tick/load cleanup never runs; the function is dead code whose stated contract is unfulfilled. Its body also drops any occupant who is dead, missing, non-Soldier, or imprisoned without clearing human.homeBuildingId, so if it were ever invoked for a live-but-imprisoned or reassigned soldier it would leave homeBuildingId pointing at the barracks while that id is gone from occupants - the exact section-5 invariant that collectSimulationInvariantErrors reports as 'missing from that workplace'. Death cleanup is already handled by killHuman -> removeHumanFromBuildingOccupants (humanLifecycleCleanup.ts:45-47), so the function's only unique effect is that hazard.
- **Recommended change:** Delete pruneDeadBarracksOccupants (its removal case is already covered by killHuman), or, if load-time pruning is genuinely wanted, clear homeBuildingId for every pruned id and call it from a named transition.

#### L16. `avoidTreeId` retry loop can never run (no caller supplies it) and, if wired, its stride can be a no-op

- **File:** `src/game/dialogueTrees.ts` — lines 263, 288-293 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `L288-293: `if (avoidTreeId && usePool.length > 1) { for (let attempt = 0; attempt < usePool.length && tree.id === avoidTreeId; attempt++) { index = (index + 5 + entityId) % usePool.length; tree = usePool[index]!; } }` — the only producer of `ChatPickOptions` is `chatHintsFromWorld` (humanChat.ts:94-101), which returns only `{ season, weather, festivalActive, foodLow }`, so `options.avoidTreeId` is always `undefined` at both pass-through sites (humanChat.ts:354, 391).`
- **Impact:** The repetition-avoidance feature is inert dead code: an entity whose formula picks the same tree twice in a row (same `entityId`/pool) repeats the line, and the guard's name/parameter promises otherwise. Separately, if the field were ever populated, the stride `5 + entityId` is `0 mod usePool.length` whenever `pool.length` divides `5 + entityId`, so all `usePool.length` attempts land on the avoided tree and the loop returns it anyway (bounded, but a silent no-op).
- **Recommended change:** Either delete the parameter and block, or make progress guaranteed: `index = (index + 1) % usePool.length;` inside the loop (and set `avoidTreeId` at a call site if repetition avoidance is actually wanted).

#### L17. NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS guard can never fire, so valley stage changes are never rate-limited

- **File:** `src/game/ecologyStage.ts` — lines 365-370 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `ecologyStage.ts:365-368 `const cooldownOk = day - (state.valleyLastStageNotifyDay ?? -999) >= NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS || rising || valleyStageIndex(next) <= valleyStageIndex(prev) - 1;` and line 370 `if (!cooldownOk && !rising) return;``
- **Impact:** `announceStage` is only reached when `next !== current` (line 453), and every transition it can produce is exactly one step (line 445 `STAGE_ORDER[Math.min(newIdx, 3)]` with newIdx = currentIdx+1 capped at 2, line 450 `currentIdx - 1`). Therefore `!rising` implies `valleyStageIndex(next) === valleyStageIndex(prev) - 1`, the third disjunct is always true, `cooldownOk` is always true, and the early return at line 370 is unreachable: the 3-day re-notify cooldown and the `valleyLastStageNotifyDay` field it depends on can never suppress a message.
- **Recommended change:** Either drop the third disjunct and gate on the cooldown alone (`if (day - (state.valleyLastStageNotifyDay ?? -999) < NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS) return;`), or delete NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS, `valleyLastStageNotifyDay` and the guard if one notification per transition is the intent.

#### L18. Redundant pressureRatio branch: ratio ≥ 2.0 is already covered by the ≥ 1.6 branch above it

- **File:** `src/game/ecologyStage.ts` — lines 133-134 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `ecologyStage.ts:133 `if (grazing.pressureRatio >= 1.6) grazingStress = Math.max(grazingStress, 2);` / line 134 `if (grazing.pressureRatio >= 2.0) grazingStress = 2;``
- **Impact:** Line 133 already assigns 2 for every ratio >= 1.6, so line 134 is a no-op for all inputs. If the second threshold was meant to be a distinct tier (e.g. requiring ratio >= 2.0 for the two-bad-driver stage-3 boost at line 222), that behaviour is not implemented anywhere and the grazing driver under-reports at extreme ratios.
- **Recommended change:** Delete line 134, or make the >= 2.0 tier drive something real (e.g. count it toward `badCount` / the stage-3 condition) instead of re-assigning the same value.

#### L19. Silo spoilage clamp 0.01 defeats the formula's own 0.8% result

- **File:** `src/game/economy.ts` — lines 20 | 8-21 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `L8 `export function updateStorageCaps(state: WorldState) {` ... L17 `gold: 20000,` ... L20 `state.foodSpoilageRate = Math.max(0.01, 0.02 - silos * 0.012);` — a repo-wide search for `updateStorageCaps` returns only this definition, `docs`-adjacent notes and tests/economyAudit.storageCaps.test.ts; no production path (tickDailyBuildingEconomy at dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:245-254, building completion at dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:166-201, demolition, upgrade, save load) ever calls it.`
- **Impact:** The only writer of `state.storageMax`/`state.foodSpoilageRate` after world creation never runs, so the caps stay at worldGen.ts:577 `{ wood: 1000, stone: 500, food: 1000, gold: 2000, iron: 500 }` and worldGen.ts:618 `foodSpoilageRate: 0.03` forever. Concrete: a new game's gold cap is 2000 instead of the designed 20000 (frontierCombat.ts:669 calls storageMax.gold 'effectively unlimited'), so gold gifts/taxes/rewards saturate 10x early; building a Wood Storehouse ('+800 wood storage', buildings.ts:231), Barn ('extends wood storage +300', buildings.ts:205) or Silo ('Passive food storage bonus', buildings.ts:218) changes nothing; spoilage stays 3%/day instead of the intended 2% (and a Silo's 0.8%/day, which tests/economyAudit.storageCaps.test.ts asserts). The existing passing test therefore verifies a function that is dead in the shipped game.
- **Recommended change:** Call `updateStorageCaps(state)` from `tickDailyBuildingEconomy` (dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:245, before `tickBuildingProgress`) and/or from `tickBuildingProgress` when `completedAny`/demolition/upgrade changes building counts; since the function then writes `foodSpoilageRate` during a tick, add `'foodSpoilageRate'` to `SimPrepKeys` and to `extractSimPrep`/`applySimPrep` (simPrep.ts:10-87, 116-117) so the tick rollback restores it.

#### L20. Lazy day rollover leaves the raw economyLedger field stale for consumers that bypass getEconomyLedger

- **File:** `src/game/economyLedger.ts` — lines 42-49 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Impact:** `ensureLedger` only resets the counters when the new day's first record happens (44-48), so `state.economyLedger.day` stays on yesterday until the first meal or production tick, while `getEconomyLedger` (67-71) exists precisely to return null for a stale day. dashboardData.ts:269-279 and 368-388 read `state.economyLedger` directly and label the totals "Food today"/"foodProducedToday" (rendered as "Food produced today" in GameDashboard.tsx), so early in a day the dashboard reports the previous day's totals as today's and contradicts the Village tab, which goes through `getEconomyLedger` (VillageTabPanel.tsx/citizenOverview.ts). Player-visible but informational only. Fix: roll the ledger at the day boundary from the daily layer (exported `rollEconomyLedgerForDay(state)`), or route dashboardData through `getEconomyLedger(state)`.
- **Recommended change:** roll the ledger at the day boundary from the daily layer (exported `rollEconomyLedgerForDay(state)`), or route dashboardData through `getEconomyLedger(state)`.

#### L21. Juvenile size uses HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE (16) while isJuvenile is later overwritten from HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS (12), producing half-size adults

- **File:** `src/game/entityFactory.ts` — lines 65-66, 110, 117, 158-161 | 66, 110, 158-160 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `entityFactory.ts:66 `isJuvenile ?? (isHuman && opts?.ageYears !== undefined ? opts.ageYears < HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE : false);` with `HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE = 16` (dayCycleConstants.ts:12), used at entityFactory.ts:110 `size: effectiveJuvenile ? config.size * 0.5 : config.size,`; but the same function then calls entityFactory.ts:159 `setHumanBirthFromAge(entity, opts.ageYears, opts.colonyDay ?? 0);`, which recomputes the flag with a different threshold — dayCycle.ts:212 `entity.isJuvenile = entity.age < HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS;` (HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS = 12, dayCycle.ts:126), and `syncHumanAgeFromCalendar` (dayCycle.ts:246) uses 12 as well.`
- **Impact:** For any caller that passes `ageYears` without an explicit `isJuvenile` (the exported factory API and the branch the code was written for), an entity aged 12–15 is created with `isJuvenile === false` but `size === config.size * 0.5`; `tryGraduateHumanChild` (dayCycle.ts:135 `if (!entity.isJuvenile || entity.age < HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS) return false;`) then refuses to promote it, so the settler renders at half size for life while every age-gated rule treats them as an adult. Today's in-repo callers all pass `isJuvenile` explicitly (worldGen.ts:499/510/539/770/781, groupEvents.ts:284, settlerInteractionActions.ts:91) so the path is latent, not live.
- **Recommended change:** Use the dayCycle owner's threshold for the auto-detect branch (`opts.ageYears < HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS`) so `size` and `isJuvenile` agree after `setHumanBirthFromAge`, or drop the auto-detect branch and require the caller's `isJuvenile` argument.

#### L23. Legacy combat-kind fallback is applied to every chronicle line and classifies any message containing "raid" as a combat event

- **File:** `src/game/eventLog.ts` — lines 48-62 | 48-63 (esp. 50, 53, 60) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `L50: `const msg = evt.message.toLowerCase();`  L53: `if (msg.includes('raid')) {`  L60: `return 'incoming_raid';` — the function never checks `evt.type`, and its caller passes the whole log: villagePortrait.ts:83-90 `const eventLog = state.eventLog ?? []; ... const kind = resolveCombatLogKind(eventLog[i]); if (kind === 'outgoing_raid') outgoingRaids++; else if (kind === 'defense' || kind === 'repelled' || kind === 'incoming_raid') defenses++;``
- **Impact:** `groupEvents.ts:818` (also 1110, 1125) logs `logEvent(state, 'event', 'Raid called off — truce with ' + rival.name, rival.name)` with no `combatKind`; the heuristic matches `'raid'`, fails the 'raid on the village'/' raid on ' tests and returns `'incoming_raid'`. Every peace treaty therefore increments `defenses` in villagePortrait, which both misreports the village's history and can silently delete the `+10` war-score bonus (`villagePortrait.ts:97 (defenses > 0 && outgoingRaids >= defenses ? 10 : 0)`), moving the portrait's war label across its 25/50 thresholds.
- **Recommended change:** Add a type guard as the first line after the kind fast-path: `if (evt.type !== 'combat') return null;` (combat writers always pass a kind, so this only removes false positives).

#### L24. The 'divorce' event type has no Chronicle filter although the file contract promises one filter per stored type

- **File:** `src/game/eventLogFilters.ts` — lines 7-22 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `L3-6 comment: 'Each stored event type receives one direct filter' — but the option list (L7-22) contains all/conception/birth/death/marriage/scandal/building/research/trade/migration/disaster/combat/event/season/milestone and no `divorce` entry, while `GameEventLog['type']` includes `| 'divorce'` (gameTypes.ts:784), writers emit it (humanRelationships.ts:885, 997, 1015) and the panel has art for it (EventLogPanel.tsx:23 `divorce: '💔'`).`
- **Impact:** Divorce chronicle entries can never be isolated with the category buttons; they are only visible in the combined 'All' view, contradicting the module's stated contract. The filter test (tests/eventLogPanel.filters.test.ts:5-27) lists only 13 types and does not assert anything about `divorce`, so it does not lock the omission.
- **Recommended change:** Add `{ id: 'divorce', label: 'Divorces' },` to `EVENT_LOG_FILTER_OPTIONS` (next to `marriage`) and add `'divorce'` to the test's `EVENT_TYPES`.
