#### L26. grantRaidSpoils subtracts resources instead of capping when stores are above their storage cap

- **File:** `src/game/frontierCombat.ts` — lines 681-699 · `resource-economy` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `682:     const room = state.storageMax.food - state.resources.food;
683:     gained.food = Math.min(room, spoils.food);
684:     state.resources.food += gained.food;
(same pattern for wood at 687-689, stone at 692-694, gold at 697-698)`
- **Impact:** room is not floored at 0, so when a resource already exceeds its cap a successful outgoing raid or accepted tribute removes that resource. Example: food 1000 with a cap that has dropped to 800 (demolishing a Barn or Silo recomputes caps in economy.ts:8-21 while stores stay put; demolition refunds add resources uncapped in buildingPlacementActions.ts:180-182) - winning a raid with 40 food spoils evaluates Math.min(800-1000, 40) = -200 and drops food to 800. The rest of the codebase floors headroom (resourceUtils.ts:43, VisitorCampPanel.tsx:30) and clampRaidGoldGain already floors gold, so this is an omission, not the established convention.
- **Recommended change:** Floor each room term: const room = Math.max(0, state.storageMax.food - state.resources.food); (and the same for wood, stone, gold).

#### L27. Local pushNews builds big-news ids from array length, producing duplicate ids when two pushes happen on one tick at the cap

- **File:** `src/game/frontierCombat.ts` — lines 373 | 681-699 · `resource-economy` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `682:     const room = state.storageMax.food - state.resources.food;
683:     gained.food = Math.min(room, spoils.food);
684:     state.resources.food += gained.food;
(same pattern for wood at 687-689, stone at 692-694, gold at 697-698)`
- **Impact:** room is not floored at 0, so when a resource already exceeds its cap a successful outgoing raid or accepted tribute removes that resource. Example: food 1000 with a cap that has dropped to 800 (demolishing a Barn or Silo recomputes caps in economy.ts:8-21 while stores stay put; demolition refunds add resources uncapped in buildingPlacementActions.ts:180-182) - winning a raid with 40 food spoils evaluates Math.min(800-1000, 40) = -200 and drops food to 800. The rest of the codebase floors headroom (resourceUtils.ts:43, VisitorCampPanel.tsx:30) and clampRaidGoldGain already floors gold, so this is an omission, not the established convention.
- **Recommended change:** Floor each room term: const room = Math.max(0, state.storageMax.food - state.resources.food); (and the same for wood, stone, gold).

#### L28. RenderSnapshot dirty key omits view.selectedEntityIds, so a shift-deselect can reuse a stale snapshot and keep drawing the removed settler's selection ring

- **File:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts` — lines 769-805 (key at 791), 939-952 | 787-804, 942 · `mutation-safety` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `src/game/gameLoop.ts:791: `v.selectedEntityId ?? '',`
src/game/gameLoop.ts:942: `if (!snapshot || dirtyKey !== this.snapshotKey) {``
- **Impact:** renderSnapshot.ts:88 feeds view.selectedEntityIds into the render state and renderer/humans.ts:442 / renderer/animals.ts:32 draw the selection ring from it, but snapshotDirtyKey only hashes the primary selectedEntityId. useCanvasInteractions.ts:265-277 removes a non-last id on shift-click while keeping the same primary (selected [A,B,C] with primary C, shift-click A -> selectedEntityId stays C), so dirtyKey is unchanged; while the game is paused (tick and camera unchanged) the cached snapshot is reused and the deselect is not drawn until some unrelated key field (tick, camera, floaters) changes.
- **Recommended change:** Add `(v.selectedEntityIds ?? []).join(',')` (and `v.showTechTree ? 1 : 0`) to the array returned by snapshotDirtyKey() in gameLoop.ts.

#### L29. Hunting-Spot output reads the research key `hunt_yield`, which no research node defines, so hunt research can never boost it

- **File:** `src/game/gameTypes.ts` — lines 923 (consumer: src/game/dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:432) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `gameTypes.ts:923 `... description: 'Unlocks Barracks · settlers hunt farther (+20% range) and bring home more meat (+25% food)', ... effects: [{ target: 'hunt_range', multiplier: 1.2 }, { target: 'hunt_food', multiplier: 1.25 }], ...`
dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:432 `const huntMult = getMultiplier(state, 'hunt_yield');`
simHelpers.ts:67-90 `export function getMultiplier(state: WorldState, key: string): number {` ... `return multiplier + add;``
- **Impact:** No research node in the game declares the target `hunt_yield` (whole-repo grep: only this call site), so `getMultiplier` finds no match and returns 1 for ever: the Hunting Spot production line is completely immune to `hunt_food` (defense_2 +25%, defense_4 +30%, defense_8 +15%). Settler hunts do receive the bonus (combat.getHuntFoodMultiplier -> humanNeeds.ts:107), so the node's promise is only half-delivered. If the intent is that building-based hunting should also scale with Stone/Iron Spears, the key is a typo for `hunt_food`; if not, the lookup is a permanent no-op.
- **Recommended change:** Either change dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:432 to `getMultiplier(state, 'hunt_food')` (or call `getHuntFoodMultiplier(state)`), or remove the dead lookup and document that building hunting is intentionally unaffected by spear research.

#### L30. getGrazerDailyDemand is exported but never called, so the winter penalty is never applied to grazing demand

- **File:** `src/game/grassEcology.ts` — lines 90-95 · `dead-code` · **fixed — lead review (follow-up)**
- **Evidence:** `grassEcology.ts:91-94 `export function getGrazerDailyDemand(type: GrazerType, season: Season): number { const meta = GRAZER_METABOLISM[type]; const penalty = getWinterEnergyPenalty(season); return grazerGrassEnergyDemandPerDay(meta.energyLossPerTick, meta.grassEnergyGain, penalty); }` — while the only production consumer passes a literal 0: ecosystemPressure.ts:68 `grazerGrassEnergyDemandPerDay(GRAZER_METABOLISM.deer.energyLossPerTick, GRAZER_METABOLISM.deer.grassEnergyGain, 0) * deerCount`.`
- **Impact:** A repo-wide grep finds no caller of `getGrazerDailyDemand` (only its definition), so grazing demand is always computed with `winterPenalty = 0`. In winter the report understates demand by the winter term, the sole helper that encodes that term is dead, and the seasonal-penalty rule has no effective owner.
- **Recommended change:** Either call `getGrazerDailyDemand('deer', state.season)` etc. from getGrazingPressureReport (passing the season through) or delete `getGrazerDailyDemand` and `GrazerType` so the winter-penalty rule has one owner.

#### L31. tryFirstWeekVisitor doc comment contradicts its own guard (day 4-7 versus tick window for days 7-13)

- **File:** `src/game/groupEvents.ts` — lines 1853, 1861 | 1853 and 1861 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `line 1853 `/** Once per game: friendly visitors on day 4–7 if the player has shelter and no camp yet. */` versus line 1861 `if (state.tick < 7 * TICKS_PER_DAY || state.tick >= 14 * TICKS_PER_DAY) return null;``
- **Impact:** The comment is the only specification for this once-per-game beat and it is wrong twice: the window is days 7-13, and the 'no camp yet' clause is never tested (the function relies on state.firstWeekVisitorSpawned, line 1859, plus the fact that nothing else can spawn a camp that early). A maintainer aligning the code with the comment would silently undo the deliberate move from day 3-7 to day 7-14 made in commit e1a6d8c, and the comment keeps the reader from noticing that a colony whose first House completes after day 13 never gets the visit at all.
- **Recommended change:** Rewrite the comment as `/** Once per game: friendly visitors on days 7-13 (tick < 14 * TICKS_PER_DAY) if the player has a completed House or Mansion; state.firstWeekVisitorSpawned makes it one-shot. */` and drop the 'no camp yet' clause that the code never checks.

#### L32. Unreachable storage-shortfall branch in tradeWithVisitors would return a paid-for, partially delivered world

- **File:** `src/game/groupEvents.ts` — lines 1449-1459 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `line 1451 `const added = addCappedResource(state, key, amount);` / line 1452 `if (added < amount) {` / line 1453 `return rejectVisitorTrade(` ... `'Trade cancelled — not enough storage space.'``
- **Impact:** getVisitorTradeEligibility already refuses any deal whose received goods do not fit (line 1410 `if ((amount ?? 0) > 0 && !canFitPurchase(state, key, amount)) {`) using the same headroom arithmetic on a deep-cloned copy of the same world, and applyTradeCost only frees additional room, so `added < amount` can never be true — the guard is dead. If it ever did fire it would not repair anything: line 1445 `applyTradeCost(state, effectivePay)` has already removed the payment, the group purse has already been credited (line 1447) and earlier keys of the loop have already been added, yet the function returns that mutated clone with a 'Trade cancelled' float, so the player pays and receives nothing (or a partial delivery) with no refund path.
- **Recommended change:** Delete the branch (the eligibility helper owns the rule) or, if kept defensively, `return originalState;` so an unexpected shortfall cannot leave the payment deducted and goods partially delivered.

#### L33. Diplomacy accept rule exists twice: respondToDiplomacyEvent re-checks every cost inline instead of applying getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility

- **File:** `src/game/groupEvents.ts` — lines 947-983, 1013-1145 | 947-983 vs 1013-1145 (checks at 1016, 1026, 1073, 1084, 1103) · `authority-violation` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `eligibility owner: line 954 `if (choiceId === 'pay' && state.resources.food < 30) {`, line 969 `if (choiceId === 'accept' && state.resources.gold < 20) {`; mutation: line 1016 `if (state.resources.food >= 30) {`, line 1073 `if (state.resources.gold >= 20) {`, line 1103 `if (state.resources.gold >= 15 && state.resources.food >= 10) {``
- **Impact:** The auto-play bot proposes a diplomacy answer only when getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility says ok (virtualPlayer.ts:146,151), while the owner re-implements the same thresholds inline. The two copies agree today, but they are edited independently: if either side changes, the bot will again spend its one act per in-game hour re-proposing an answer the owner refuses and the card stays open — the exact stall class fixed for raids and story cards in BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-13-autoplay-card-answer-stall.md. frontierCombat.respondToRaidEvent now applies its guards through getRaidChoiceEligibility (frontierCombat.ts:991,1008,1059); this is the only remaining card owner that does not, and the sibling owners in this same file (tradeWithVisitors, line 1440; negotiateRefugees, line 1537) do gate through their eligibility helper.
- **Recommended change:** In respondToDiplomacyEvent, gate each branch through the owner rule instead of restating it, e.g. inside each case: `const gate = getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility(state, event, choiceId); if (!gate.ok) { pushFloat(state, rival.campX, rival.campY - 20, gate.blockReason ?? 'Cannot answer', '#f97316'); break; }`, and delete the inline `state.resources.food >= 30` / `gold >= 20` / `food >= 25 && gold >= 15` / `gold >= 15 && food >= 10` tests (legitimate free choices still return ok, so behaviour is unchanged).

#### L34. Recurring world events reuse one constant GameEvent id, so dismissing one banner suppresses that event type for the rest of the save

- **File:** `src/game/groupEvents.ts` — lines 419, 496, 1721 (also 1728, 1735, 1750, 1770, 1780, 1790, 1796) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `line 419 `id: `visitor_${kind}`,`; line 496 `id: 'rival_settlement',`; line 1721 `event: { id: 'wolf_migration', title: 'Wolf Pack Migration', ... }``
- **Impact:** dismissActiveEvent permanently records event.id in world.dismissedActiveEventIds (useTransientGameFeedback.ts:277-279), and both the banner render (line 326 `!hiddenActiveEventIds.has(world.activeEvent.id)`) and the worker delta (simDelta.ts:462-464 preserveActiveEventDismissal) hide any later activeEvent carrying that id. Concrete: dismiss the year-0 first-week pilgrim banner (id `visitor_pilgrims`, line 419) and every later `pilgrim_caravan` yearly event — and every later first-week performer/pilgrim arrival after a save/load — never shows its banner again in that save, because the id is the same. The event still happens (groups spawn, toast still fires via addNotification), so the loss is silent: the player sees the effect with no banner explaining it.
- **Recommended change:** Make the id instance-unique, e.g. line 419 `id: `visitor_${kind}_${state.tick}``, line 496 `id: `rival_settlement_${state.tick}``, and each rollYearlyWorldEvent case `event: { id: `wolf_migration_${state.tick}`, ... }`; keep the `visitor_` prefix (the hook's startsWith check at useTransientGameFeedback.ts:255 still works) and prune ids that are no longer in use from dismissedActiveEventIds, which stays bounded today only because ids repeat.

#### L35. tickHospitalDailyCare passes patient proximity as doctorPresent

- **File:** `src/game/hospitalCare.ts` — lines 189-192 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `189: `const near = Math.hypot(p.x - hx, p.y - hy) < 70;` … 192: `if (treatPatientAtHospital(state, p, hospital, { doctorPresent: near })) {``
- **Impact:** `doctorPresent` is documented/implemented as "a doctor is on site" — its default is a proximity scan of the hospital's occupants (86-89). Passing the PATIENT's distance to the ward makes every patient in the ward round receive the +0.35 doctor strength bonus (~+5 energy per treatment) and the '❤ Treated' floating text even when no assigned occupant is anywhere near the ward, i.e. it is less accurate than the default it overrides.
- **Recommended change:** Drop the option (`treatPatientAtHospital(state, p, hospital)`) so the real doctor check runs, or compute the occupant proximity explicitly and pass that boolean.

#### L36. Medicine food cost bypasses the economy ledger

- **File:** `src/game/hospitalCare.ts` — lines 99-101 | 92-107 (gate at 156) · `cadence-violation` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `156: `if (personDayRoll(doctor.id, state.tick, 901 + best.id) > 0.35) return false;` then 92-95 `const heal = Math.min(..., (8 + strength * 14) * ...)`; 99 `if (state.resources.food >= 1 && Math.random() < 0.25) {`; 107 `gainSkill(state, id, JobType.Doctor, 0.08);``
- **Impact:** `personDayRoll` is constant for a whole colony day, and doctorTreatNearby runs from the per-tick human tick (humanTick.ts:1079 -> humanHospitalBehavior.ts:57). On an eligible day the doctor therefore re-applies the whole treatment every tick: ~11+ energy per tick per patient, 0.08 Doctor skill per occupant per tick (skill 100 within a couple of in-game hours), a 25%/tick chance of spending 1 food (tens of food per shift), and repeated '❤ Treated' feedback. hospitalCare imports no PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE at all, while comparable per-tick effects are scaled (hotelStay.ts:248 `0.8 * PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE`). The same per-day-roll-per-tick shape governs patient self-treatment (humanHospitalBehavior.ts:94), so this is a module-level cadence choice, not a single-statement slip.
- **Recommended change:** Give the treatment an explicit cadence: e.g. `if (!isStartOfClockHour(state.tick)) return false;` in doctorTreatNearby/patient care, or scale heal, skill gain and the 25% food roll by PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE so one passing day yields a bounded amount of care.

#### L37. clearEntityChat leaves chatDialogueSessionKey set, so a vanished session leaves the settler flagged dialogue-busy

- **File:** `src/game/humanChat.ts` — lines 167-170 (with 363-372) | 332-341 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `humanChat.ts:337-340:
  entity.chatDialogueSessionKey = undefined;
  entity.chatPartnerId = undefined;
  entity.chatPhrase = formatChatLine(phrase, entity);
  entity.chatTicks = activeChatTicksFromLegacyDuration(legacyDurationTicks);
(no `dialogueSessions.delete(...)`; startDialogueTreeChat then bails at humanChat.ts:258 `if (dialogueSessions.has(key)) return;`.)`
- **Impact:** Callers force phrases without checking isDialogueBusy/chatTicks — e.g. humanLeisureBehavior.ts:100 (`sayHumanChatPhrase(entity, impulse.bubble, 55)`), humanRelationships.ts:1494-1495, townHall.ts:237+, villageLeadership.ts:621. If the speaker currently holds a solo session (`solo:<id>`), the entry stays in the module-level dialogueSessions Map forever: every later solo attempt reuses the same key, hits `dialogueSessions.has(key)` and returns without showing anything, so that settler never speaks a tree dialogue again and the Map entry leaks for the session lifetime. For a pair key the entry is only reclaimed when the other participant finishes; if both sides are force-phrased in the same window (humanRelationships.ts:1494-1495 does exactly that) the pair can never dialogue again.
- **Recommended change:** Delete the entry before clearing the fields: `const staleKey = entity.chatDialogueSessionKey; if (staleKey) dialogueSessions.delete(staleKey);` and, in tickHumanChat, clear `chatDialogueSessionKey`/`chatPartnerId` when `dialogueSessions` has no entry for the key (the resolveSessionEntities null path currently only calls clearEntityChat), so the counterpart cannot stay isDialogueBusy forever.

#### L38. Kid-play chat roll uses Math.random() while the sibling bubble roll in the same function uses the run-seeded RNG

- **File:** `src/game/humanLeisureBehavior.ts` — lines 36 · `determinism` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `humanLeisureBehavior.ts:36:
      if (kidImpulse.bubble && Math.random() < 0.06 * PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE) sayHumanChatPhrase(entity, kidImpulse.bubble, 40);
(compare humanLeisureBehavior.ts:100: `seededRandomForRun(`chat-bubble:${entity.id}:${state.tick}`) < 0.08 * PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE`.)`
- **Impact:** The decision to emit a child bubble - and therefore the resulting chatPhrase/chatTicks written into authoritative entity state (transported in simDelta field list, simBuffers/simDelta.ts:175) - is decided by the unseeded global RNG, so a replayed or re-run identical seed produces different dialogue state. The seeded helper is used for the equivalent roll eleven lines below and in humanChat, so the seeded stream is the intended owner; tests/deterministicSeed.test.ts snapshots tick/resources/entities without chat fields, so this divergence is not covered.
- **Recommended change:** Replace `Math.random()` with `seededRandomForRun(`chat-bubble-kid:${entity.id}:${state.tick}`)`, matching the seeded pattern used at line 100 and in humanChat.ts.

#### L39. Ambient-dialogue stagger in humanTick (mod 3) is misaligned with the neighbour-scan stagger (SOCIAL_STAGGER = 6), so half of all ambient rolls get an empty candidate list and can only produce a solo line

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 407-427 (caller gate 412) vs src/game/simulation/humanSocial.ts:64 and src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:205 | 407-427 (caller gate), vs src/game/simulation/humanSocial.ts:64 and src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:205 · `cadence-violation` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:412 `(state.tick + entity.id) % 3 === 0` gates the call `tryAmbientRandomDialogue(entity, ambientChatNeighbors(entity), state.tick, 0.036 * PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE, ...)`. The candidate provider is `simAmbientChatNeighbors` (humanTick.ts:282-283), and humanSocial.ts:64 begins `if ((tick + self.id) % SOCIAL_STAGGER !== 0) { return []; }` with adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:205 `export const SOCIAL_STAGGER = 6;`. Multiples of 3 are not all multiples of 6.`
- **Impact:** For every tick where (tick+id) % 6 === 3 (exactly half of the ticks the caller admits), ambientChatNeighbors() returns an empty array, so `tryAmbientRandomDialogue` can never pair the speaker with a nearby settler and always emits a solo remark. Paired ambient conversation therefore fires at roughly half the rate the code intends, which is precisely the monologue regression documented in tests/humanChat.ambientPairing.test.ts (BUG 2026-08-21-ambient-dialogue-often-becomes-leader-monologue) — that test calls tryAmbientRandomDialogue directly and so cannot see the caller-side gate.
- **Recommended change:** Replace the caller's `(state.tick + entity.id) % 3 === 0` with the same stagger the callee uses: `(state.tick + entity.id) % SOCIAL_STAGGER === 0` (SOCIAL_STAGGER is already imported in humanTick.ts line 113). This keeps the expensive-neighbour-query short-circuit while guaranteeing every admitted roll actually receives candidates.

#### L40. Prisoner early-exit skips shared per-tick upkeep: chat/dialogue timers freeze and pregnancy progress stops

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 388-405, 794-802 | 388-403 vs 794-802 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:388-403 `if (isPrisoner) { ... syncEntityGrids(ctx, entity); continue; }` exits the iteration before humanTick.ts:794-801 `if (isPlayerHuman(entity) && entity.gender === 'female' && entity.pregnant && !conceivedToday && entity.pregnancyProgress !== undefined) { tickPregnancyAndBirth(state, ctx, entity, { livingHumanAt }); }`. Pregnancy can coexist with imprisonment: arrest happens in exposeAffair -> arrestForScandal (humanRelationships.ts:1149-1152, 1203) and the daily gossip path only excludes a pregnancy fathered by the lover (humanRelationships.ts:313-318 `if ((entity.pregnant && entity.pregnantById === lover.id) || ...) return;`), so a married woman pregnant by her husband can be arrested as 'caught'.`
- **Impact:** While imprisoned, `pregnancyProgress` never advances (and the mother also takes no energy loss and no meal), so the term silently stretches by the 2.5-6 day sentence; if a save is taken during that window the progress field and the birth date the player sees diverge from the declared 24-day pregnancy. Any invariant/test that expects a pregnancy to advance every tick while `entity.pregnant` holds is violated for this edge case.
- **Recommended change:** Move the pregnancy gate above the prisoner branch (or run it for prisoners before `continue`), i.e. advance existing pregnancy for imprisoned pregnant settlers while still skipping movement/needs.

#### L41. `canPursueSecretAffair` is called without the colony work schedule, so the realtime affair gate uses the legacy 07:00-16:00 window

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 948, 1036, 1209 | 220-222 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:220-222 `const nurturingSettlerCount = allHumans.filter((h) => h.alive && !h.isJuvenile && h.traits?.includes('nurturing')).length;` — `allHumans` is built from `byType[EntityType.Human]` plus newborn entities (humanTick.ts:198-208) with no `isPlayerHuman` filter, while every other social list in the file filters it (`playerHumans` at :116, `isPlayerHuman` at :212, :330, :409). The value is fed to `getSchoolAgeMultiplier(entity, updatedBuildings, nurturingSettlerCount)` (humanTick.ts:298), whose contract is education.ts:78-79 `// Nurturing settlers in the village help every child mature a bit faster. const nurtureBoost = Math.min(0.2, nurturingCount * 0.04);`.`
- **Impact:** Up to three visiting/rival 'nurturing' settlers (entityFactory.ts:74 rolls traits for every human, including visitors, rivals and caravan carriers) contribute up to +0.12 to every colony child's maturation multiplier while they stand in the valley, silently shortening childhood based on who happens to be visiting.
- **Recommended change:** Filter to colony settlers: `allHumans.filter((h) => h.alive && isPlayerHuman(h) && !h.isJuvenile && h.traits?.includes('nurturing'))`.

#### L42. Velocity is blended twice in the hangWithCompany leisure branch (0.45/0.55 then 0.5/0.5 in the shared tail), so company-following movement uses a different weight than every other leisure kind

- **File:** `src/game/humanTick.ts` — lines 1293-1297 and 1440-1445 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `humanTick.ts:1293-1294 (inside `if (hangWithCompany && company)`) `entity.vx = entity.vx * 0.45 + idleVx * 0.55; entity.vy = entity.vy * 0.45 + idleVy * 0.55;`, then the chain is followed by the shared tail humanTick.ts:1441-1442 `entity.vx = entity.vx * 0.5 + idleVx * 0.5; entity.vy = entity.vy * 0.5 + idleVy * 0.5;` which is still inside the `if (!suppressIdle) {` block opened at humanTick.ts:1197 and therefore also runs for the company branch (idleVx/idleVy are non-zero there).`
- **Impact:** The tail re-applies the blend, so a settler walking with partner/child/coworker moves with an effective idle weight of 0.55*0.5 + 0.5 = 0.775 instead of the intended 0.55 (other leisure kinds get 0.5), and `spriteAngle` is computed twice from two different velocity vectors. Company-following steering is therefore much stronger/more snap-to-target than every other idle behaviour, i.e. the branch does not do what its own 0.45/0.55 line implies.
- **Recommended change:** Delete the duplicate blend at humanTick.ts:1293-1294 (and its redundant spriteAngle assignment) and let the shared tail at 1440-1445 perform the single blend, as it already does for all other leisure kinds.

#### L43. isValidHuntingSpotPrey uses `in`, so Object.prototype keys validate as prey ids

- **File:** `src/game/huntingSpots.ts` — lines 33-35 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `huntingSpots.ts:34:
  return typeof value === 'string' && value in PREY_OPTIONS_MAP;`
- **Impact:** PREY_OPTIONS_MAP is a plain object literal, so `'toString' in PREY_OPTIONS_MAP`, `'constructor' in ...` and `'__proto__' in ...` are all true. The worker command boundary uses this as its only guard (simWorker/commands.ts:237 `return isFiniteNumber(cmd.buildingId) && isValidHuntingSpotPrey(cmd.prey);`), so a crafted `setHuntingSpotPrey` command carrying `prey: 'toString'` is accepted and stored on the building via buildingConfigurationActions.ts:109; dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:380 then compares `building.huntingSpotPrey ?? 'auto'` against real prey types, so the post silently harvests nothing, and getHuntingSpotPreyOption('constructor') returns Object.prototype.constructor (UI reads `.label`/`.hint` as undefined).
- **Recommended change:** Use an own-property check: `return typeof value === 'string' && Object.hasOwn(PREY_OPTIONS_MAP, value);` (or `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(PREY_OPTIONS_MAP, value)`).

#### L44. clampToMapBounds lets NaN positions through unclamped

- **File:** `src/game/mapBounds.ts` — lines 17-20 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Impact:** The code fact is exact: mapBounds.ts:17-20 uses only `< 0` / `> width` comparisons, which are false for NaN, and it is the last statement of both position-advancing paths (humanTick.ts:1467-1470, tickLayerSystems.ts:679). But no NaN producer exists in the repo (every `dx/dist` site I checked guards the divisor), so the described failure is latent; additionally simInvariants.ts:20 already detects non-finite positions and logs them, so this is a hardening gap in the last line of defence rather than a live defect. A minimal cheap guard (`if (!Number.isFinite(entity.x)) entity.x = 0;`) is still worthwhile.
- **Recommended change:** See the independent-verification paragraph below — it states the exact minimal change.

#### L45. Herd deer bypass the entity-id index that every other spawn path maintains

- **File:** `src/game/migration.ts` — lines 48-58 (push at 58) | 37-59 (spawn positions 44-47, createEntity 48-54) · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `44:    if (edge === 0) { x = 40; y = height / 2 + spread; }
(48-54 createEntity(EntityType.Deer, x, y, ...) — no isPassableWildlifePosition check anywhere in spawnHerdAtEdge)`
- **Impact:** Every other spawn path in the codebase gates on terrain (worldGen.spawnWildlifeAtRandomPassable line 224, spawnWildlifeRing line 349); this one uses fixed edge coordinates. Measured by importing the repo's own isPassableWildlifePosition and running tickMigration on real maps (40 maps x 6 presets): coastal 119/400 herd deer spawn on deep/shallow water in 40/40 maps; verdant 45/400 (water/river/riverBank); harsh 76/400 (25 inside Mountains); riverlands 77/400. So a third of a coastal herd starts standing in the sea, on tiles the rest of the simulation treats as unpassable (UNPASSABLE_WILDLIFE_TERRAIN, worldGen.ts:71-78); those deer must wander out before normal land behaviour applies (120 ticks after arrival most blocked deer were still on unpassable tiles), and the player sees deer swimming in the ocean.
- **Recommended change:** In spawnHerdAtEdge, validate each candidate point before createEntity with isPassableWildlifePosition(state, x, y, 8) (nudge along the edge, or fall back to spawnWildlifeRing(state, EntityType.Deer, edgeMidX, edgeMidY, count, 40, 120) which already contains the passability retry loop).

#### L46. Every herd death is reported and accounted as 'hunted'

- **File:** `src/game/migration.ts` — lines 74-86 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `81:    const killed = active.spawned - alive;
85:      addBigNews(state, '🦌 The herds remember', `${killed} deer taken from the passing herd — next autumn will bring fewer.`, 'negative');`
- **Impact:** `alive` counts surviving tagged deer, so a deer killed by wolves, a predator, or any non-player cause is counted as taken and permanently shrinks migrationNextHerdSize (line 84). Concrete scenario: the player never hunts, wolves thin the passing herd by three, and the player still gets the negative 'the herds remember ... 3 deer taken' news plus a smaller herd next autumn, while the header comment ('let them pass and next autumn brings them back, fat as ever') and the log text ('herd deer hunted') describe a player hunt that never happened.
- **Recommended change:** Count only player-caused kills (e.g. record a hunted count when markWildlifeDead is invoked from a hunter for an entity with migrationTag === active.herdYear), or reword the news/log to 'deer lost from the passing herd' and leave the size memory keyed on that number deliberately.

#### L47. Wall-cap label is hardcoded to +72 even though forged wall plates raise the cap to 96

- **File:** `src/game/militiaBalance.ts` — lines 214 (duplicated at defenseStructures.ts:159) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `214:       lines.push(`Barricade only: +${wallBonus} wall segments (${walls} built, max +72)`);`
- **Impact:** getWallSegmentBonus raises the cap to FORGE_BONUSES.wallPlateCap (96) when the wall_plates forge order is complete (defenseStructures.ts:70-73), so the combat preview tells a plated village its walls cap at +72 while up to +96 is actually granted. defenseStructures.ts:159 repeats the same literal, and the literal is the only duplicated copy of a tuning value that otherwise lives in FORGE_BONUSES (forge.ts:16).
- **Recommended change:** Derive the cap from the same source the bonus uses, e.g. ${isForgeOrderComplete(state.villageForge ?? EMPTY_FORGE, 'wall_plates') ? FORGE_BONUSES.wallPlateCap : 72}, or drop the '(max +NN)' suffix.

#### L48. transformToWerewolfForm clears assignment ids but only detaches building occupants when `buildings` is passed

- **File:** `src/game/moonHowler.ts` — lines 363-367, 381-383 | 151-165, 1016 · `cadence-violation` · **fixed — low-3**
- **Evidence:** `moonHowler.ts:161  `    && hourOfDay === NIGHT_START`
moonHowler.ts:163  `    && rng() < MOON_HOWLER_REPLACEMENT_CHANCE`
moonHowler.ts:1016  `  if (shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse(colonyDay, hourOfDay, humanPop, activeMoonCurses, rng)) {`  (called from tickLayerRealtime.ts:97 on EVERY tick)`
- **Impact:** TICKS_PER_HOUR = 3, so hourOfDay is 20 for tickOfDay 60, 61 and 62 — three consecutive ticks. Nothing latches the roll: the only guard is `activeCursed === 0`, which a failed roll does not change, and tickMoonHowlerCycle runs once per tick via tickLayerRealtime. A quiet full moon therefore gets three independent 15% rolls: P = 1 - 0.85^3 = 38.6% per full moon, i.e. ~10 replacement Howlers per 360-day year instead of the ~3.9/year implied by the constant (and the ~1-2/year the comment at lines 78-84 claims). SIMULATION_AUTHORITY §5 says a replacement appears 'only through a rare replacement roll'; the one-Howler invariant still holds, but the declared rare-event pacing is ~2.6x off. The existing tests call the pure predicate once per case (tests/moonHowler.rare.test.ts:129-219) so the per-tick repetition is not locked in.
- **Recommended change:** Roll once per moon instead of once per hour-20 tick: in tickMoonHowlerCycle line 1016 use `if (isStartOfClockHour(state.tick) && shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse(colonyDay, hourOfDay, humanPop, activeMoonCurses, rng))` (isStartOfClockHour is already re-exported by dayCycle.ts:26), or latch the roll on a per-colony-day field.

#### L49. tickMoonHowlerCycle's injectable rng is not forwarded to the church rite or the flavour picks

- **File:** `src/game/moonHowler.ts` — lines 1044 (with 952 and 642) | 1044, 990, 1030-1034, 1048 · `determinism` · **fixed — low-3**
- **Evidence:** `moonHowler.ts:1044  `  const dawnCures = tryMoonHowlerChurchCures(state, aliveEntities, buildings, colonyDay, hourOfDay, entityById);`
(compared with moonHowler.ts:952 `  rng: () => number = Math.random,` and moonHowler.ts:642 `  rng: () => number = Math.random,`)`
- **Impact:** tickMoonHowlerCycle accepts an injected `rng` but only uses it for the replacement roll (line 1016). The rite path opened here falls back to the default Math.random for the weighted cure/kill/flee roll (line 743), the per-guard save rolls (line 780), the flee-position jitter (line 827) and the four WEREWOLF_*_LINES picks (lines 990, 1030, 1034, 1048). A caller that injects a deterministic stream to make a full-moon cycle reproducible (the seam documented in SIMULATION_AUTHORITY §11 step 4 and in CHANGELOG.md:626-628, and used by tests/moonHowler.rare.test.ts:164-213) cannot control whether the priest is cured or killed, so the 'deterministic full moon' guarantee only covers the replacement roll.
- **Recommended change:** Forward the stream: `const dawnCures = tryMoonHowlerChurchCures(state, aliveEntities, buildings, colonyDay, hourOfDay, entityById, rng);` and replace the `Math.random()` line picks at 990/1030/1034/1048 with `rng()`.

#### L50. getPathGrid cache key omits the map preset, although tiles (and therefore blocked cells) depend on it

- **File:** `src/game/pathfinding.ts` — lines 62-68 | 267-269 · `numeric-safety` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `269: `const steps = Math.max(4, Math.min(128, Math.ceil(span / (TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE * 0.5))));` (comment 268: "Sub-tile sampling steps prevent tunneling through 1-tile diagonal obstacles")`
- **Impact:** `TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE` is 10 (gameTypes.ts:992) and the intended step is 5px, but the 128 cap makes the real spacing span/128: 11.7px for a full medium-map line (1200x900 -> span ~1500px) and ~25px on a huge map (2560x1920 -> span ~3200px), i.e. larger than the tile itself. A 1-tile river or mountain strip can fall between two samples, so `lineCrossesBlocked` returns false, `steerWithPath` returns 'direct' (316/349) and the human walks straight through water/mountain — the exact failure the module was written to prevent. Humans have no terrain collision (only wildlife has the deep-water guard, tickLayerSystems.ts:609-628), so nothing downstream corrects it.
- **Recommended change:** Raise the cap so the step can never exceed half a tile (and the tile width for every supported map size), e.g. `const steps = Math.max(4, Math.min(1024, Math.ceil(span / (TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE * 0.5))));`.

#### L51. An escaped prisoner is never removed from prison.occupants and keeps prisonSentenceCrime

- **File:** `src/game/prisonGuardDuty.ts` — lines 58-61 | 56-71 · `invariant-risk` · **fixed — low-2**
- **Evidence:** `L60-61: "escapee.prisonBuildingId = undefined;\n      escapee.prisonerUntilTick = undefined;" — no `prison.occupants` update and no `escapee.prisonSentenceCrime = undefined`, unlike the other release paths (workforce.releasePrisoners L670-677; buildingMaintenanceActions.ts L171-174).`
- **Impact:** tickPrisonGuardDuty runs from tickDailyWorldEvents.ts:129, after tickDailyBuildingEconomy's assignMissingWorkers has just rebuilt `prison.occupants` to include the escapee; nothing re-syncs afterwards in that tick, so the dev invariant pulse (gameTick.ts:277-286) reports "human N in prison #P is neither prisoner (prisonBuildingId) nor guard (homeBuildingId)" (simulationInvariants.ts:141-145). The Prison panel keeps listing the free settler as a prisoner until the next assign pulse (≤18 ticks), and `prisonSentenceCrime: 'scandal'` is carried in the save forever, unlike every other release path that clears it.
- **Recommended change:** After L61 add: `prison.occupants = prison.occupants.filter((id) => id !== escapee.id);` and `escapee.prisonSentenceCrime = undefined;`

#### L52. resetRelationshipDiagnostics does not restore the `enabled` flag

- **File:** `src/game/relationshipDiagnostics.ts` — lines 154-158 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Impact:** The omission is real: resetRelationshipDiagnostics (154-158) restores consoleLoggingEnabled but not `enabled`, while recordRelationshipDiagnostic (98-101) and flushRelationshipDiagnostics (129) no-op when disabled, so a reset after a disable would silently keep all counters at 0 and getLatestRelationshipDiagnostics stale. No production code path disables or resets diagnostics, however — the only callers are in tests/relationshipDiagnostics.test.ts, whose beforeEach sets enabled true before calling reset, so the described false reading cannot occur today. Minimal fix: add `enabled = true;` to the reset.
- **Recommended change:** add `enabled = true;` to the reset.

#### L53. Friendship map keys are never pruned, so dead settlers keep counting as friends and boosting energy

- **File:** `src/game/relationships.ts` — lines 188-203 (candidate said 188-202) | 189-202 (contrast 153-156) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `193: `for (const val of Object.values(p.friendships)) {` … 201: `p.energy = Math.min(maxEnergy, currentEnergy + boost);` — no liveness check, unlike the feud loop's 153: `if (!other) { delete feuds[key]; continue; }``
- **Impact:** The feud half of the same function explicitly deletes records whose counterpart is no longer among the living (`byId`), but nothing prunes `friendships` — reconcileFamilyReferencesAfterRemoval clears partner/affair/children links only. A survivor therefore keeps every dead friend's score and keeps collecting up to +2 energy/day from them forever ('Strong friends lift each other's spirits'), and each settler's friendship record grows monotonically as roommates and co-workers die. friendCount() (45-52) likewise counts dead friends.
- **Recommended change:** In the same daily pass, delete friendship keys whose counterpart is missing from `byId` (mirroring the feud cleanup) and compute `strongCount` only over living counterparts.

#### L54. The pickSharedResidenceForFamily fallback in assignFamilyToResidence is unreachable

- **File:** `src/game/residencyReconciliation.ts` — lines 130-131 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `residencyReconciliation.ts:130-131 `const picked = pickResidenceForFamily(family, alive, residences, occupancy)\n    ?? pickSharedResidenceForFamily(family, alive, residences, occupancy);` — residencySelection.ts:631-633 and 582-584 show both functions skip leader houses and require the identical `familyFitsInResidence(family, residence, humans, occupancy)` predicate before a residence can become `best`.`
- **Impact:** For any non-empty family, whenever `pickResidenceForFamily` yields `undefined` the second call iterates the same residences with the same fit test and unchanged occupancy and also yields `undefined`, so the right-hand side can never contribute a placement; the `??` branch is dead and hides the fact that a family that fits nowhere is handled entirely by the split path below (adults individually, children by custodian). (Only a zero-length family — never produced by buildHousingUnits or syncPartnerResidence — could make the two disagree, via pickResidenceForFamily's `family.length === 0` early return.)
- **Recommended change:** Delete the `?? pickSharedResidenceForFamily(family, alive, residences, occupancy)` fallback (and the now-unused import/private helper if nothing else uses it), or change one of the two scorers so the fallback expresses a real policy difference.

#### L55. auditHousingSharingIssues/isUnnecessarilySharingHousing (and residenceHasCapacity) have no callers, and the dead audit counts rival and leader residences as empty housing

- **File:** `src/game/residencySelection.ts` — lines 496-499 | 263-281 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `residencySelection.ts:267-268 `const unit: Entity[] = [custodian]; visited.add(custodian.id);` — the bucket loop never checks `visited.has(custodianId)` before building the unit (contrast the fallback loop at line 284: `if (visited.has(human.id)) continue;` and the partner guard at line 270 `if (partner && !visited.has(partner.id))`).`
- **Impact:** Concrete failure: mother M has minor child A (custodian M), father F has minor child B (custodian F, B's mother dead), and M and F are partners (a blended family created by remarriage — reachable through humanTick.ts:910/tryCompleteCourtshipMarriage). Bucket[M]=[A] builds unit [M,F,A] and marks F visited, but bucket[F]=[B] still builds unit [F,B] containing F again. In assignMissingResidences (residencyReconciliation.ts:230-239) every unit that needs reassignment clears and re-places ALL of its members, so F is moved twice per pass and the two units keep stealing him: [F,B] is placed in an empty house, then [M,F,A] (now invalid) is cleared and placed in another empty house, and the 24-pass loop never records `reassigned === 0`, so it runs all 24 passes on every assign pulse (4x/day). The stable end state leaves the minor child B alone in one house while F, M and A occupy another — the exact opposite of assignFamilyToResidence's contract ('keeps children with custodian'); ensureOrphanAdoption cannot repair it because B still has a living natural custodian, so the split persists across pulses.
- **Recommended change:** Add the missing guard at the top of the bucket loop: `if (visited.has(custodianId)) continue;` (and attach that bucket's children to the custodian's existing unit, or rebuild the children buckets from the emitted units) so each settler belongs to exactly one housing unit; the child whose custodian is already in a unit must join that unit rather than fall through to the single-entity fallback at line 283.

#### L56. selectRivalDailyAction offers 'fortify' at 8 wood while applyRivalDailyAction requires 12, so a shelter-priority rival silently wastes its action day

- **File:** `src/game/rivalProfiles.ts` — lines 74, 105 | 70-78, 104-108 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `74: `if (profile.priority === 'shelter' && ledger.wood >= 8) return 'fortify';` versus 105: `if (ledger.wood < 12) return { changed: false, summary: 'not enough wood to fortify' };` (the comparable security branch at 71 correctly requires wood >= 12).`
- **Impact:** A shelter-priority rival holding 8-11 wood selects 'fortify' every action day — the branch is evaluated before the 'gather' fallback at 75 — applyRivalDailyAction then refuses it without mutating any ledger value, and rivalEvents.ts:233 records `profile.lastAction = 'none'`. Because nothing is ever spent or gathered the ledger stays in the 8-11 window, so that rival's economy stops permanently: no gathers, no recovery, no trades, and the UI reports 'Quiet at camp' (rivalPresence.ts:23/34) forever.
- **Recommended change:** Match the select threshold to the cost: `if (profile.priority === 'shelter' && ledger.wood >= 12) return 'fortify';`, mirroring the security branch on line 71.

#### L57. recentSourceKind scans from the oldest end of a newest-first log and records the wrong source event

- **File:** `src/game/rumourLedger.ts` — lines 80-98, 124 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `81 comment: `* Scans backwards through the event log to find the most recent matching event category.`; 85: `const scanLimit = Math.max(0, log.length - 40);`; 87: `for (let i = log.length - 1; i >= scanLimit; i--) {`. Because logEvent unshifts (eventLog.ts:23), index log.length-1 is the OLDEST entry, so the loop walks entries len-40..len-1 (the 41 oldest) and returns the first match found from that end.`
- **Impact:** The rumour's subject and text (59-65, 134) come from the colony's earliest events instead of the newest: a birth, combat or scandal that just happened is ignored in favour of day-1 scaffolding, so the card narrates stale history. Line 124 compounds it: `[FLAG_SOURCE_EVENT]: state.eventLog[state.eventLog.length - 1]?.id ?? 0` stores the oldest entry's id, not the matched event, and no code ever reads FLAG_SOURCE_EVENT (only the declaration at 28 and this write exist).
- **Recommended change:** Iterate the newest end — `for (let i = 0, last = Math.min(log.length, 40); i < last; i++)` — return the matched entry along with its kind, and store that entry's id in FLAG_SOURCE_EVENT (or delete the unused flag).

#### L58. Ordinary-workplace preview set is defined by exclusion, so residences, roads and decor are counted as affected workplaces and their residents as assigned workers

- **File:** `src/game/scheduleFeedback.ts` — lines 13-24, 33-34 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-6**
- **Evidence:** `scheduleFeedback.ts:23:
  return (Object.values(BuildingTypeValues) as BuildingType[]).filter((type) => !fixedTypes.has(type));
line 34 counts `sum + building.occupants.filter((id) => state.entities.some((entity) => entity.id === id && entity.alive && !entity.faction)).length`.`
- **Impact:** fixedTypes only excludes Church/TownHall/School/Tavern/Hotel, so the 'ordinary' set also contains House/Mansion/LeaderHouse (whose `occupants` are residents, per SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md §3) as well as Road, Bridge, Wall, Well, Garden, Statue, Lamp, Fence, Silo, WildlifePreserve. `affectedWorkplaces` counts every completed road segment and every house, and `assignedWorkers` sums all housed residents - the WorkSchedulePanel (WorkSchedulePanel.tsx:104-107) then shows the player a badly inflated 'N workplaces / M assigned workers are affected' preview for the ordinary work window. Existing test tests/scheduleFeedback.test.ts only builds Farm/Tavern/Hotel so it cannot catch this.
- **Recommended change:** Build the ordinary set from actual staffable workplaces instead of excluding five types — e.g. drop residence types with `isResidenceBuildingType(type)` and OR on an explicit workplace allow-list (the same predicate workforce.ts uses to choose a workplace), so Road/Bridge/Wall/decor and houses are not counted.

#### L59. `huntVisuals` is written only in the worker and never shipped, so hunt lines stop rendering

- **File:** `src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts` — lines 284-285 (extract), 407-408 (apply) | 78-79; extract 284-289; apply 407-408 · `save-persistence` · **fixed — low-1**
- **Evidence:** `L78-79: `floatingTexts: WorldState['floatingTexts'];` / `deathParticles: WorldState['deathParticles'];` — the neighbouring per-tick presentation arrays are carried, but `WorldState.huntVisuals` (gameTypes.ts:750) is absent from `SimTickDelta` and from both extract/apply.`
- **Impact:** Hunt visuals are produced and pruned entirely in the worker (`humanHuntingBehavior.ts:125` and `dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:412` → `addHuntVisual`; `tickLayerRealtime.ts:238` → `pruneHuntVisuals`) and the render sidecar (`EntityRenderMeta`) does not carry them. renderSnapshot.ts:112 copies `world.huntVisuals` into the snapshot and renderer/humans.ts:353 draws them, so with the worker active the array on the display world is always undefined and no hunt arrow/lunge is ever drawn.
- **Recommended change:** Carry it like the other transient arrays: add `huntVisuals: WorldState['huntVisuals']` to `SimTickDelta`, `huntVisuals: deltaClone(world.huntVisuals ?? [], cloneMode)` in extract, and `world.huntVisuals = deltaClone(delta.huntVisuals, cloneMode)` in apply.

#### L60. reassignDivorcedResidences re-houses an imprisoned custodian and moves minors into the prisoner's home

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/humanRelationships.ts` — lines 936-947 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `line 938 `custodian.residenceBuildingId = pickResidenceForHuman(custodian, villagers, residences);` has no prison guard, while the leaver branch immediately below does: line 942 `if (leaver.prisonBuildingId == null) {` ... line 949 `leaver.residenceBuildingId = undefined;``
- **Impact:** exposeAffair arrests both offenders first (lines 1150-1151) and arrestForScandal clears residenceBuildingId (line 1202) before tryDivorceOnCaughtCheater calls reassignDivorcedResidences. When the offending partner is the wife she becomes the custodian (lines 910-912), so she is immediately handed a fresh residence, the free spouse is evicted to another house (line 947) and the minor children are assigned to the imprisoned mother's house (lines 953-966). The asymmetry with the leaver guard shows the prisoner case was considered only for one of the two settlers.
- **Recommended change:** Mirror the leaver guard for the custodian, e.g. wrap lines 936-939 in `if (custodian.prisonBuildingId == null) { ... } else { custodian.residenceBuildingId = undefined; }`, and skip the minor-custody assignment (lines 953-966) when the custodian is imprisoned.

#### L61. RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG carries a second, unread definition of the amicable-divorce rate

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/humanRelationships.ts` — lines 63-64, 859-877 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `lines 63-64 inside RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG: `AMICABLE_DIVORCE_ANNUAL_RATE: 0.075,` / `AMICABLE_DIVORCE_DAILY_CHANCE: 0.075 / DAYS_PER_YEAR,`; the value actually used is the exported duplicate at line 860 `export const MARRIAGE_DAILY_AMICABLE_DIVORCE_CHANCE = MARRIAGE_ANNUAL_AMICABLE_DIVORCE_RATE / DAYS_PER_YEAR;`, read at line 877 `if (rng() >= MARRIAGE_DAILY_AMICABLE_DIVORCE_CHANCE) return;``
- **Impact:** A repository-wide search finds no reader of either RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG field, so two independent definitions of the same gameplay rate exist (AGENTS.md 5.2 / 5 'one source of truth'). Tuning `RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG.AMICABLE_DIVORCE_ANNUAL_RATE` silently has no effect while the exported constant keeps the old value, which is the classic split-brain tuning hazard.
- **Recommended change:** Delete lines 63-64 from RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG (the exported MARRIAGE_* constants are the live source of truth), or make line 860 read `RELATIONSHIP_CONFIG.AMICABLE_DIVORCE_ANNUAL_RATE` so there is exactly one definition.

#### L63. clearHuntersTargetingPrey early-returns on a tick-start index, so hunters that acquired the prey mid-tick keep a dangling target

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/simulationEntities.ts` — lines 163-173 (early return), 175-179 (full scan) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Impact:** clearHuntersTargetingPrey (simulationEntities.ts:158-180) returns as soon as the tick-start index contains the prey (163-172), so the aliveness-scanning loop at 175-179 never runs for the ordinary case where another wolf/hunter already had that prey indexed (index built once per tick at gameTick.ts:184 from buildHuntTargetByPreyIndex, 125-156). Hunters that acquire the prey later in the same tick (humanHuntingBehavior.ts:171, tickLayerSystems.ts:381) keep a huntTargetId that points at a just-removed entity until their own next human tick clears it (humanTick.ts:310-313). Minimal fix: after clearing the indexed hunters, also run the entityById scan (or, cheaper, delete the prey entry only after clearing and keep the scan unconditional). Low severity: every reader guards on aliveness (humanStatus.ts:153-154, renderer/humans.ts:325-326, humanTick.ts:310-312) and chases are re-decided by a fresh spatial query each tick.
- **Recommended change:** after clearing the indexed hunters, also run the entityById scan (or, cheaper, delete the prey entry only after clearing and keep the scan unconditional). Low severity: every reader guards on aliveness (humanStatus.ts:153-154, renderer/humans.ts:325-326, humanTick.ts:310-312) and chases are re-decided by a fresh spatial query each tick.

#### L64. Moon Howler scan computes `howlerIds` that is never read, contains an empty `if` block, and its 'zero allocations' comment is false

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/simulationInvariants.ts` — lines 246-268 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-9**
- **Evidence:** `simulationInvariants.ts:247 `let howlerIds: number[] = [];` is only written (lines 252-253 `if (howlerCount <= 2) { howlerIds.push(e.id); }`) and never read; lines 255-258 are a no-op block (`if (howlerCount > 1) { // We can break early ... }`) and the message re-scans the whole entity list at line 264 `const allHowlerIds = state.entities.filter(e => e.alive && e.moonHowlerCursed).map(e => e.id);`.`
- **Impact:** Dead accounting in the governance collector: the '🚀 OPTIMIZED ... (zero allocations)' comment advertises an optimisation the error path does not have (a second full scan plus a map allocation), and the unused buffer invites a future reader to trust ids that are truncated at 2 for any count > 2.
- **Recommended change:** Delete line 247 and the push at 252-253, delete the empty `if (howlerCount > 1)` block at 255-258, and build the message from a single array collected in the same pass (`const howlerIds: number[] = []; ... howlerIds.push(e.id);` unconditionally, keeping only the `howlerCount > 1` check for the error).

#### L65. economyLedger/foodHistory are written by the tick but missing from the worker rollback backup

- **File:** `src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts` — lines 10-87, 116-117 · `save-persistence` · **fixed — low-1**
- **Evidence:** ``SimPrepKeys` (L10-87) lists `resources`, `storageMax`, `villageForge`, `researchProgress`, … but not `economyLedger` or `foodHistory`; `extractSimPrep` copies only those keys (e.g. L116 `resources: { ...state.resources },` L117 `storageMax: { ...state.storageMax },`) and `applySimPrep` restores only them (L185-265). Meanwhile the daily tick writes them: economyLedger.ts:47 `state.economyLedger = { day, produced: {}, consumed: {} };` / L56 `ledger.produced[source] = (ledger.produced[source] ?? 0) + amount;` / L31-39 push into `state.foodHistory`. The backup is used to undo an already-applied tick: gameWorker.ts:314 `const prepBackup = extractSimPrep(world);` … L345 `applySimPrep(world, prepBackup);` ('Render buffer pool exhausted').`
- **Impact:** That rollback restores resources/entities but leaves the rolled-back tick's ledger entries in place. The same tick is then retried (or simply lost), so `ledger.produced`/`consumed` and the archived `foodHistory` day double-count or claim food that was never kept — the exact numbers the Food ledger, dashboard 'Food today' (dashboardData.ts:269-279) and the 30-day history display. Same class of gap applies to `foodSpoilageRate`, which the tick would write once updateStorageCaps is wired in.
- **Recommended change:** Add `'economyLedger' | 'foodHistory' | 'foodSpoilageRate'` to `SimPrepKeys` and clone them in `extractSimPrep` (e.g. `economyLedger: state.economyLedger ? { day: state.economyLedger.day, produced: { ...state.economyLedger.produced }, consumed: { ...state.economyLedger.consumed } } : undefined`, `foodHistory: state.foodHistory ? state.foodHistory.map((s) => ({ ...s, produced: { ...s.produced }, consumed: { ...s.consumed } })) : undefined`) and assign them back in `applySimPrep`.

#### L66. Prep backup shallow-clones entities, so nested fields survive a failed-command rollback

- **File:** `src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts` — lines 102-103 | 141, 145-149 (with gameWorker.ts:223, 355) | 102-103; 141-149; 172 · `mutation-safety` · **fixed — low-1**
- **Evidence:** `L102-103: `// Clone entity objects shallowly to decouple from in-place property mutations` / `entities: (state.entities ?? []).map((e) => ({ ...e })),``
- **Impact:** The backup shares every nested object with the live world, so in-place nested mutations are not rolled back: `ensureEntitySkills(human)[job] = ...` (workforce.ts:211 and 264, skills.ts:37, apprenticeships.ts:95) mutates the shared `skills` map, `parent.childrenIds.push(childId)` (humanLifecycle.ts:40) the shared array, and moon-howler save/restore rewrites the shared `moonHowlerSaved`. gameWorker.ts:222-233 then rolls back via `applySimPrep` and answers `commandResult ok:false` with `safeExtractCommandDelta`, so the surviving mutations are shipped to the main thread as authoritative. The same O(entities) backup is also taken on every tick (gameWorker.ts:314) even though a tick error always ends the worker session (GameLoop.fallbackFromWorker), so the tick-path backup never changes the outcome.
- **Recommended change:** Also copy the mutable nested fields in the entity clone, e.g. `({ ...e, skills: e.skills ? { ...e.skills } : e.skills, childrenIds: e.childrenIds ? [...e.childrenIds] : e.childrenIds, moonHowlerSaved: e.moonHowlerSaved ? { ...e.moonHowlerSaved } : e.moonHowlerSaved })`.

#### L67. syncTreeGrid rebuilds only when the grid object is replaced; a reused tree grid is never reconciled

- **File:** `src/game/spatialGrid.ts` — lines 489-501 · `mutation-safety` · **fixed — low-1**
- **Evidence:** `497: `if (grid !== existing) {`  498: `grid.rebuild(treeEntities, isTreeGridEntity);` — unlike syncGrassRenderGrid/syncMobileSimGrid/syncHumanSocialGrid there is no `else grid.reconcile(...)`, and `syncSpatialGridEntity` (574-582) only updates the grass and mobile grids.`
- **Impact:** The tree grid is a snapshot taken the first time it is created and is then frozen: any tree that enters the world afterwards is invisible to `findClosestEntityInRadius(ctx.treeGrid, ...)` (humanTick.ts:1404-1412 leisure tree sitting, blueberryForaging.ts:94-102 berry search) because those callers only use the `byType[Tree]` fallback when the grid is undefined. The yearly `nature_boom` event adds 15 trees (groupEvents.ts:1688-1693), so the grid's contract ('Trees - static scenery; indexed for leisure visits') is not upheld. Today the effect is masked because nature_boom pushes its trees only into `allAlive`/entityById and not into `state.entities` (see notes), so this is a latent-but-real cache-invalidation hole rather than an observed one.
- **Recommended change:** Add `else grid.reconcile(treeEntities, isTreeGridEntity);` after the rebuild branch so a reused tree grid tracks additions and removals like the other grids.

#### L68. Unused grid API: forEachNeighborCell, ensurePresent, hasEntity, distSq, buildGrassGrid, buildMobileGrid

- **File:** `src/game/spatialGrid.ts` — lines 70, 191, 227, 340-369, 465-473, 795-803 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-1**
- **Evidence:** `340: `forEachNeighborCell(` / 191: `ensurePresent(entity: Entity): void {` / 227: `hasEntity(id: number): boolean {` / 70: `export function distSq(ax: number, ay: number, bx: number, by: number): number {` / 465: `export function buildGrassGrid(` / 795: `export function buildMobileGrid(` — a repo-wide search (src, tests, scripts) finds no call site for any of them.`
- **Impact:** A 30-line 3x3 neighbourhood walker plus two index helpers and two grid builders are carried as if they were part of the public grid contract but are never exercised, so they can silently drift from the invariants the grid enforces — forEachNeighborCell already disagrees with cellCoords (123-128): it returns false for out-of-map coordinates instead of clamping, so a caller written against cellCoords' semantics would get 'no neighbours' at map edges. The project's own CHANGELOG records removing exactly this class of dead spatial alias (forEachEntityInRadius, generateHumanSprites).
- **Recommended change:** Delete the six unused members (or, if forEachNeighborCell is intended as public API, add a focused grid test that pins its edge behaviour).

#### L69. buildings.upgraded subtracts last year's per-year upgrade count from a cumulative total

- **File:** `src/game/stats.ts` — lines 83-88 | 83-88, 114, 138 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `stats.ts:83-86 `const currentUpgrades = state.buildings.reduce(` / `(sum, b) => sum + (b.level > 1 ? b.level - 1 : 0),` / `0,` / `);`
stats.ts:87 `const prevUpgrades = prevYearStats?.buildings.upgraded ?? 0;`
stats.ts:88 `const upgradesThisYear = Math.max(0, currentUpgrades - prevUpgrades);`
stats.ts:114 `upgraded: upgradesThisYear,`
stats.ts:138 `s.totalBuildingsUpgraded += latestYear.buildings.upgraded;``
- **Impact:** `currentUpgrades` is a cumulative total of all upgrade levels in the village, while the field it is compared against (`buildings.upgraded`) is written as that year's *increase* (line 114) and read again as a level. Example: 2 upgrades in year 0 (cumulative 2, recorded 2); no upgrades in year 1 -> 2-2 = 0 (correct); no upgrades in year 2 -> 2-0 = 2 reported (phantom). `updateLifetimeStats` accumulates these into `lifetimeStats.totalBuildingsUpgraded`, which drifts and over-counts. Contrast `marriages`, which correctly uses the previous snapshot `prevYearStats.marriedCount`.
- **Recommended change:** Give YearlyStats a cumulative baseline (`upgradedTotal: currentUpgrades`) and use `const prevUpgrades = prevYearStats?.upgradedTotal ?? 0;`, keeping `upgraded` as the per-year delta; or drop the subtraction and increment `lifetimeStats.totalBuildingsUpgraded` at the upgrade owner.

#### L70. The whole strip replacement/refund machinery is unreachable because nothing ever writes replacesBuildingId

- **File:** `src/game/stripBuild.ts` — lines 19-30 (field 29); branch buildingPlacementActions.ts:207-223, 269-284 | 28-29, 19-30 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** ``readonly replacesBuildingId?: number;` (stripBuild.ts:29) and `replacesBuildingId?: number;` (stripTopology.ts:131) are only ever READ: the complete cross-repo grep for `replacesBuildingId` returns reads in buildingPlacementActions.ts (lines 207-208, 230, 269) and the two type declarations — no producer exists (`resolveWallStripPlan`, `resolveRoadStripPlan` and `resolveStripPlan` never set it, and both UI call sites send the preview segments unchanged).`
- **Impact:** The documented feature ('Existing player building to be demolished/replaced (refunded at 50%)', stripBuild.ts:28; 'Existing player building removed (corner swap or straight-through rebuild)', stripTopology.ts:130) never runs: `refundHalfBuildingCost` (buildingPlacementActions.ts:178) and the whole `replacing`/`replacementId` branch (lines 207-223, 269-284) are dead, so no replaced building is ever refunded and no corner/straight-through rebuild exists. The only way to populate the field is a hand-built `placeStripChain` payload, which the command door accepts because `isStripSegment` validates only finite x/y (commands.ts:199-203).
- **Recommended change:** Either populate it at the source (emit `replacesBuildingId: existing.id` from resolveWallStripPlan's 'existing piece' branch, replacing the silent `continue`, so corner/straight-through swaps actually refund) or delete the field, the `replacing` preview branch and `refundHalfBuildingCost` so the code stops advertising a live rule.

#### L71. connectionsAt's 'lone crossing' repair guard can never fire

- **File:** `src/game/stripJunction.ts` — lines 165-176 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Evidence:** `lines 166-174: `north: north || (!south && !north), south, east: east || (!west && !east), west,` … `if (countConnections(conn) < 2) { return { north: true, east: true, south: false, west: false }; }`.`
- **Impact:** `north || (!south && !north)` is true whenever `south` is false, so N+S >= 1; identically E+W >= 1, so `countConnections(conn) >= 2` always. The fallback return is unreachable, which means the comment's stated purpose ('a lone crossing reads as zero connections and skips corners') is not achieved by this branch — a lone crossing is instead forced to `{north:true, east:true}` by the line-167/169 coercions, i.e. it is classified as a `straight`/`elbow` rather than through any fallback. Any future reader relying on the guard will not get the documented behavior.
- **Recommended change:** Delete the unreachable `if (countConnections(conn) < 2)` block and state the actual coercion contract in the comment, or replace the coercions with the intended explicit check (`if (!north && !south && !east && !west) return { north: true, east: true, south: false, west: false };`).

107:  if (temperature > 0.7 && moisture < 0.25) return TerrainType.Grassland;
109:  return TerrainType.Grassland;`

- **Recommended change:** Delete the redundant branch (keep `return TerrainType.Grassland;`), or return the actually intended distinct TerrainType member for the hot/dry case.

#### L73. Both daily-layer entry guards are always true, so they document a tick-0 path that cannot exist

- **File:** `src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts` — lines 46, 51 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts:46: `if (isNewCalendarDayTick(state)) {`
src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts:51: `if (state.tick > 0) {``
- **Impact:** gameTick is the only caller (gameTick.ts:220) and invokes the layer only when `state.tick % TICKS_PER_DAY === 0`; state.tick is incremented before that test (gameTick.ts:76), so the first possible call is tick 72, and markCalendarDayProcessed (dayCycle.ts:320-324) writes lastProcessedCalendarDay = floor(tick/72) at every boundary, so `day > lastProcessed` holds at every call. Both guards are therefore unconditionally true: the 'skip tick 0 initialization' comment is misleading, the schedule invariant is enforced only in gameTick, and any future direct caller (the module is exported through gameEngine.ts:308) would silently skip the social/chronicle block instead of failing loudly.
- **Recommended change:** Delete the redundant `if (isNewCalendarDayTick(state))` and `if (state.tick > 0)` wrappers (or replace them with an explicit assertion that the tick is a day boundary) and drop the tick-0 comment.

#### L74. FLAG_SCRIPT is written but never read, so the stage-1 script choice has no effect
