#### L75. Load reconciliation schedules a vacancy election but leaves a dead villageLeaderId in place

- **File:** `src/game/villageLeadership.ts` — lines 1012-1020 | 891-900 (with 902-905 as the only place that schedules an election; caller src/game/dailyWorldEvents.ts:230) · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `const leaderId = state.villageLeaderId;
  if (leaderId == null) return null;
...
  if (!state.entities.some((entity) => isEligibleForLeadership(entity, state))) {
    state.villageLeaderId = null;
    return null;
  }`
- **Impact:** When the leader dies (or is otherwise non-acting) and every remaining adult is ineligible (e.g. the only other adult is serving a scandal sentence, or only juveniles remain - isEligibleForLeadership requires alive/Human/player/adult/not imprisoned), this branch nulls villageLeaderId and returns WITHOUT setting pendingElectionYear. Every later daily call then returns at the `leaderId == null` guard, so the vacancy-election schedule is never re-established even after settlers grow up or are released from prison: the colony has no head, no 'leadership vacancy' notice (focusHints.ts:109 and getElectionCeremonyStatus both require pendingElectionYear != null), and only reaches a leader again at the next even-year term election (tryStartTermElectionCeremony requires year % 2 === 0) - up to 2 game years with no election, while the leader invariant check (simulationInvariants.ts:271) is silently skipped because villageLeaderId is null. tickLeaderVacancy is also the only scheduler of pendingElectionYear besides save-load (validateVillageLeaderOnLoad:1019), so this state survives until the player reloads.
- **Recommended change:** Schedule the vacancy election in this branch instead of only clearing the office, i.e. replace the early `return null` with the same `state.pendingElectionYear = state.year + (state.dayInYear ?? 0) / DAYS_PER_YEAR + VACANCY_ELECTION_DELAY_YEARS` write used below (keeping the office cleared and logging the vacancy), or re-arm when `state.villageLeaderId == null && state.pendingElectionYear == null && state.lastElectionYear !== 0`.

#### L76. Village Request accept rule has a second implementation in the bot because the request owner exports no eligibility function

- **File:** `src/game/virtualPlayer.ts` — lines 304-321 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Impact:** The inline gate at virtualPlayer.ts:304-321 is real duplication, but the described divergence is not reachable: the bot imports the same VILLAGE_REQUEST_PROVISIONS_COST_GOLD/FOOD from groupEvents.ts:61-62, and getAvailableStorageHeadroom (resourceUtils.ts:24-28) is arithmetically identical to the owner's private storageRoom (groupEvents.ts:1313-1315) for any finite storageMax; even the undefined-cap edge cases resolve to 'allow' on both sides. The bot also acts only through the typed resolveVillageRequest command, so groupEvents' §3 mutation ownership is not violated and no 'Offer unavailable' spam can arise from a gate mismatch. This is a maintainability nit; exporting a shared `getVillageRequestEligibility` is optional cleanup, not a bug fix.
- **Recommended change:** See the independent-verification paragraph below — it states the exact minimal change.

#### L77. Staffing-mode guard is unreachable; the intended 'already auto' check never runs

- **File:** `src/game/virtualPlayer.ts` — lines 850-853 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-7**
- **Impact:** In decideStaffingMode (virtualPlayer.ts:839-864) `desired = 'auto'` is assigned only inside the branch that also requires `building.staffingMode === 'manual'` (847-848), so the guard at 853 (`desired === 'auto' && building.staffingMode == null`) can never be true — the `else if` already excludes every non-manual building. Behaviour is currently correct (the guard is a no-op), so this is dead code contradicting its own comment; minimal fix is to delete line 853 or move the check into the branch that computes `desired`.
- **Recommended change:** See the independent-verification paragraph below — it states the exact minimal change.

#### L78. An imprisoned settler is never removed from a construction crew and keeps working the site

- **File:** `src/game/workforce.ts` — lines 483-490 · `edge-case` · **fixed — low-8**
- **Evidence:** `L483-490: "if (human.prisonBuildingId != null) {\n      if (human.homeBuildingId != null) {\n        human.homeBuildingId = undefined;\n        human.occupation = 'settler';\n        human.job = JobType.Settler;\n      }\n      continue;\n    }" — crew membership is never cleared here, syncJobBuildingOccupants (L294-323) rebuilds only completed workplaces and the Prison, and humanRelationships.arrestForScandal (humanRelationships.ts:1187-1212) clears homeBuildingId/residence but not crew membership.`
- **Impact:** A settler jailed while on a construction crew (crew members have homeBuildingId == null, so the arrest's homeBuildingId branch is skipped entirely) stays in the unfinished site's `occupants`: dailyBuildingEconomy.tickBuildingProgress keeps counting them as a worker (L145) and grants them builder skill daily (L159-163) in prison, and after release `isOnConstructionCrew` keeps them out of ordinary job assignment until that site completes.
- **Recommended change:** In the prisoner branch, drop the human from every incomplete site: `for (const b of buildings) if (!b.completed && b.occupants.includes(human.id)) b.occupants = b.occupants.filter((id) => id !== human.id);`

#### L79. Earthquake branch ignores the disaster's own x/y/radius and hits every building on the map

- **File:** `src/game/worldEvents.ts` — lines 253-259 · `logic-error` · **fixed — low-5**
- **Evidence:** `253:     } else if (type === 'earthquake') {
254:       impulseScreenShake(state, 15);
255:       for (let i = 0; i < state.buildings.length; i++) {
256:         const b = state.buildings[i];
257:         const currentHp = b.health ?? b.maxHealth;
258:         b.health = Math.max(10, Math.round(currentHp - 15 * resistMult));`
- **Impact:** Every other damage branch tests the stored radius (`dx*dx+dy*dy < radiusSq`: fire line 212, flood 232, tornado 245, plague 267), and the storm owner explicitly skips incomplete and rival buildings (line 116). The earthquake applies 15*resistMult damage to all buildings regardless of distance, completion or faction, so the x/y/radius pushed at lines 181-188 for the quake has no effect and a 'quake' anywhere damages distant rival structures.
- **Recommended change:** Skip buildings outside the radius and non-player/incomplete ones the way the storm path does, e.g. compute dx/dy and `if (dx*dx + dy*dy >= radiusSq) continue;` (plus `if (!b.completed || b.faction === 'rival') continue;`) before applying damage.

#### L80. InitGameOptions.width/height size the world state but never reach generateWorldMap

- **File:** `src/game/worldGen.ts` — lines 573-574, 687 | 579-681 (581) and 824-826 · `invariant-risk` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `581:    entityByType: emptyEntityByType(),
826:  rebuildEntityByIdMap(state);   // no buildEntityByType / entityByType fill anywhere in initGame`
- **Impact:** indexLivingEntity only maintains entityById (entityIndex.ts:42-45), so after initGame every bucket is a defined empty array. Probe on initGame({size:'medium',seed:1}): 694 living entities / entityById.size 694 / entityByType grass=0 rabbit=0 deer=0 wolf=0 fox=0 human=0 tree=0. Because an empty array is not nullish, the documented fallbacks are bypassed: entityCatalog.resolveAliveByType (158-159 `world.entityByType?.[type] ?? world.entities.filter(...)`) returns [] instead of scanning, renderSnapshot.ts:91-93 `?? buildEntityByType(entities)` likewise, and assertSimInvariants check #5 ('entityByType reflects the alive population', simInvariants.ts:66-74) is false for a fresh world. It self-heals on the first gameTick, so the window is a pre-tick world such as the paused quick-start overlay (App.tsx:902) plus any tooling/test that inspects a freshly initialized world; saveLoad deliberately strips entityByType to undefined (saveLoad.ts:176-190), so initGame is the only producer of a defined-but-empty index.
- **Recommended change:** At the end of initGame, next to rebuildEntityByIdMap(state) (line 826), add `state.entityByType = buildEntityByType(state.entities);` (import buildEntityByType from './simFocus').

#### L81. Wildlife replenish log throttle field is updated on every replenishing call, so the 40-day gap can only ever fire once

- **File:** `src/game/worldGen.ts` — lines 462-475 | 422-457 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `457:  if (!wildlifeSpawned && !grassReplenished) return false;`
- **Impact:** Line 422 already returns when `!needsWildlife && !grassReplenished`, and every needsX block (427-455) sets wildlifeSpawned = true, so needsWildlife implies wildlifeSpawned and the condition at 457 is always false (unreachable return). The function therefore always reports true once line 422 passes, even if spawnWildlifeRing placed no animal at all — the boolean reads as 'a refill happened' when it only means 'a refill was attempted'. (No production caller consumes the return value today: dailyWorldEvents.ts:142 discards it, so this is a latent trap for the next caller.)
- **Recommended change:** Remove the dead guard, or make the function return whether anything was actually placed (e.g. track spawns inside spawnWildlifeRing/spawnGrassPatch and return `placedCount > 0`).

#### L82. sufficientGrass can never be false, so the grass gate on prey respawn is dead

- **File:** `src/game/worldGen.ts` — lines 404-409 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `404:  const totalAvailableGrass = grassCount + (grassReplenished ? 98 : 0);
405:  const sufficientGrass = totalAvailableGrass >= 45;`
- **Impact:** grassReplenished is set exactly when grassCount < MIN_GRASS (65), so totalAvailableGrass is either grassCount+98 (>=98) or grassCount (>=65); both are >=45, so sufficientGrass can never be false. needsRabbits/needsDeer therefore reduce to `rabbits < 35` / `deer < 12` and the intended 'prey animals depend on grass' rule is inert: with grassCount = 0 (all grass eaten) the function still queues a full 55-rabbit / 20-deer refill because the +98 credit is a hard-coded estimate that no longer measures the resource it is compared against.
- **Recommended change:** Recount grass after the spawnGrassPatch calls (e.g. const grassAfter = computeWildlifeCounts(state.entities).grass) and compare that real post-refill count to a threshold expressed against MIN_GRASS.

#### L83. replenishDepletedWildlife final 'nothing happened' guard can never fire

- **File:** `src/game/worldGen.ts` — lines 422-457 · `dead-code` · **fixed — low-4**
- **Evidence:** `457:  if (!wildlifeSpawned && !grassReplenished) return false;`
- **Impact:** Line 422 already returns when `!needsWildlife && !grassReplenished`, and every needsX block (427-455) sets wildlifeSpawned = true, so needsWildlife implies wildlifeSpawned and the condition at 457 is always false (unreachable return). The function therefore always reports true once line 422 passes, even if spawnWildlifeRing placed no animal at all — the boolean reads as 'a refill happened' when it only means 'a refill was attempted'. (No production caller consumes the return value today: dailyWorldEvents.ts:142 discards it, so this is a latent trap for the next caller.)
- **Recommended change:** Remove the dead guard, or make the function return whether anything was actually placed (e.g. track spawns inside spawnWildlifeRing/spawnGrassPatch and return `placedCount > 0`).

</details>

---

## Cross-cutting findings (45)

These came from the six cross-cutting agents (determinism, cadence, invariants, mutation/caches/memory, persistence surface, duplicated owners/dead code). They were **not** put through the separate adversarial verification pass, so treat the "unverified" marker as a lower evidence tier than the findings above. The **Status** column names the fix that closed an entry; anything without one is still open.

### X1-determinism (6)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| critical | `simWorker/gameWorker.ts` | 118-124, 150-152, 174-178 | determinism | The simulation worker never receives or installs the run seed, so the entire authoritative simulation ignores it | fixed — worker seed adoption (`adoptSimSeedFromWorld` in `resetWorkerSession`) |
| high | `simRng.ts` | 132-137 | determinism | Seeded global Math.random is shared with the renderer/audio, so in the main-thread fallback the sim stream depends on frames rendered | resolved by construction — no `Math.random(` call remains in `src`, so the override is a third-party reach-through safety net only |
| high | `tickLayerRealtime.ts` | 97-105 | determinism | Every injectable-RNG owner is called without its rng, so the seeded seam is dead in production | fixed — low-7 |
| high | `saveLoad.ts` | 163-172, 374 | determinism | Loading a save never restores the colony seed, so the seed in the save file is ignored | fixed — low-4 |
| medium | `simWorker/simPrep.ts` | 10-13, 91-183 | mutation-safety | RNG stream position is not part of any rollback/save snapshot, so a resumed or retried world cannot reproduce its continuation | fixed — lead review (follow-up) — `simRng.snapshotSimRng`/`restoreSimRng` carried by the save file, the worker hand-off (`world.simRng`) and the prep payload |
| high | `simulation/humanRelationships.ts` | 640, 736, 777, 799 | determinism | Conception and pregnancy duration — the invariant-critical reproductive owner — draw from raw Math.random instead of a domain stream | fixed — low-3 (already fixed by the determinism pass) |

- **The simulation worker never receives or installs the run seed, so the entire authoritative simulation ignores it** — _Impact:_ The worker is the production owner (`isGameWorkerEnabled()` returns true by default, GameWorkerHost.ts:570-579; gameLoop.ts:216 uploads the world via `workerHost.importSave`). Inside that realm simRng keeps its module default `currentSeed = 1` (simRng.ts:4) and `Math.random` is native, so the run seed has no effect on the authoritative simulation: (a) all `getSimRng(owner)` streams collapse to seed 1, making entityFactory gender/age/`reproductionCooldown` (entityFactory.ts:61,104,113), migration edge (migration.ts:39), famine bite (famineDesperation.ts:68) and prison escapes (prisonGuardDuty.ts:55) identical across every new game; (b) every raw `Math.random()` gameplay roll becomes truly non-reproducible — grep finds 303 `Math.random` references under src (~296 in src/game, ~250 of them in modules reachable from gameTick, across ~27 simulation modules), including conception (humanRelationships.ts:736,777,799), birth/stillborn/gender (humanLifecycle.ts:118,139), old-age and illness death (humanRelationships.ts:828,842), weather (worldEvents.ts:72), wolf recruitment/breeding (tickLayerSystems.ts:104,109,575,706), daily festival/immigration (dailyWorldEvents.ts:71, dailyPopulation.ts:66) and group/rival event rolls (groupEvents.ts, rivalEvents.ts). The existing D1 test cannot catch this because it drives the main-thread path directly (tests/deterministicSeed.test.ts:29 `current = gameTick(current);`). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Seeded global Math.random is shared with the renderer/audio, so in the main-thread fallback the sim stream depends on frames rendered** — _Impact:_ When the worker is disabled or fails to start, gameLoop falls back to main-thread ticks (gameLoop.ts:198 `'[GameLoop] Worker init failed — falling back to main-thread ticks'`) and sim draws and frame draws interleave on one stream: `worldEvents.updateWeather` (worldEvents.ts:72), `humanLifecycle.tickPregnancyAndBirth` (humanLifecycle.ts:139) and the conception rolls (humanRelationships.ts:736) consume values whose position was advanced by particle churn, window size and screen-shake. The same seed therefore produces different worlds at 30 fps vs 144 fps, or after resizing. The deterministicSeed test only passes because rendering never runs under it. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Every injectable-RNG owner is called without its rng, so the seeded seam is dead in production** — _Impact:_ The Moon Howler lifecycle rolls that were explicitly made injectable — `shouldApplyNewMoonHowlerCurse` (moonHowler.ts:163 `&& rng() < MOON_HOWLER_REPLACEMENT_CHANCE`) and the church rite `rollMoonHowlerRiteOutcome` (moonHowler.ts:127 `const r = rng();`) plus the guard-save roll (moonHowler.ts:780) — are drawn from the unseeded/global stream in production, so the one-Howler-cursed/replaced lifecycle is not reproducible and tests pass only because they inject stubs (tests/moonHowler.exorcism.test.ts:151 `() => 0.49`). The same omitted-rng pattern affects `tryDailyAmicableDivorce` (humanTick.ts:357 → default humanRelationships.ts:868), `tryFormSchoolyardBond` (humanTick.ts:373 → default humanRelationships.ts:389) and `advanceYouthLove` (tickLayerDaily.ts:53 → default humanRelationships.ts:536), i.e. youth-love breakups, schoolyard bonds and divorce are all on the global stream. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Loading a save never restores the colony seed, so the seed in the save file is ignored** — _Impact:_ `simRng.currentSeed` and the cached per-owner streams (simRng.ts:4-5, `streams`) keep the values of the session that happens to be running, so `seededRandomForRun`-based systems read the wrong seed after a load: chat and courtship lines (humanChat.ts:387,438,461,472,523; humanTick.ts:874,876), venue/work chat (humanVenueBehavior.ts:57) and leisure bubbles (humanLeisureBehavior.ts:100,122). The same save file therefore produces different social content depending on which run loaded it (and differs from the original run), while seed-derived systems such as renffrStar/electionPromises/inventionFair that read `state.worldMap.seed` (renffrStar.ts:46) stay correct — the two notions of "the run seed" disagree. _Recommended change:_ 
- **RNG stream position is not part of any rollback/save snapshot, so a resumed or retried world cannot reproduce its continuation** — _Impact:_ Any rollback or resume continues from an advanced RNG position: (a) after a failed command/tick the world is reverted but the draws already consumed by the failed attempt are gone, so a retried command (or the next tick) yields different random outcomes than the un-failed run; (b) even if finding #4 is fixed by re-seeding on load, clearing the streams restarts entityFactory/migration/famine-desperation/prison-guard-duty at draw #1 while the world is at day N, so a save/load continuation diverges from the original run. Because none of this state is in the save, delta or prep payload, no code path can restore it. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Conception and pregnancy duration — the invariant-critical reproductive owner — draw from raw Math.random instead of a domain stream** — _Impact:_ Which married/affair/youth couples conceive, and on which tick each child is born, depend on an unseeded global stream, so a run seed cannot reproduce the colony's demographic history (and after fixing #1 the worker would still draw these from native Math.random). It also means one shared stream is used by every raw-Math.random owner, so adding or removing a single `Math.random()` call anywhere in the sim (or a render particle draw, in fallback mode) silently shifts conception, birth, gender, mortality and trait inheritance outcomes — the exact class of drift the per-owner `getSimRng` design (simRng.ts:82-89) exists to prevent. _Recommended change:_ 

### X2-cadence (5)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| high | `tickLayerSystems.ts` | 253-254 (constant at src/game/simFocus.ts:26) | cadence-violation | Off-screen wildlife throttle gate can never fire for 3/4 of entity ids (tick always a multiple of the layer interval) | fixed — M31 |
| high | `gameTick.ts` | 206-234 (entity commit at 234; offending spawn write at src/game/tradeCaravans.ts:246) | resource-economy | gameTick's `state.entities = allAlive` destroys entities pushed directly onto state.entities during the tick — trade-caravan carriers never survive, so exports are deducted every 8 days with no delivery | fixed — H5 |
| medium | `tickLayerRealtime.ts` | 117-123 | cadence-violation | Realtime layer performs a full-population scan and rewrites residence `occupants` every tick while a Moon Howler is active | fixed — low-3 / low-7 |
| medium | `moonHowler.ts` | 161 and 1016 (announcement duplicate at 924/983-986) | cadence-violation | Hour-granular full-moon gate is evaluated on all three ticks of hour 20 — replacement curse rolls 3x per full moon and the 'Full Moon!' announcement repeats 3x | fixed — M21 + low-3 |
| low | `moonHowler.ts` | 1014-1015 | cadence-violation | Per-tick full-population scans feed a decision that can only fire at nightfall on a full-moon day | fixed — low-3 |

- **Off-screen wildlife throttle gate can never fire for 3/4 of entity ids (tick always a multiple of the layer interval)** — _Impact:_ Because `state.tick` is always a multiple of 4 at this point, `(state.tick + id) % 8 === 0` is satisfiable only when `id % 8` is 0 or 4 — i.e. only for entities whose id is a multiple of 4 (25%). For every other id (1,2,3,5,6,7 mod 8) the fallback throttle is never true, so out-of-focus fauna permanently skips the whole AI block (movement, grazing, hunting, mating, block 6-16) while still paying the per-pulse energy drain at line 242 (`entity.energy -= (config.energyLossPerTick * grazerEase + winterPenalty) * step;`), so they starve to death at line 244-249 without ever grazing. In production the worker passes the camera viewport as focus (gameLoop.ts:825 -> requestTick -> gameWorker.ts:317), and at the default zoom 1.45 the focus rect is only ~1122x736 px, so on Medium/Large/Huge maps a large band of the map is out of focus: e.g. a wolf spawned by `tickWolfRecruitment` at the map edge with id 11 walks nowhere and dies of starvation, and autumn migration herds / `replenishDepletedWildlife` arrivals freeze in place. The intended cadence of the constant ('every N wildlife-layer calls', simFocus.ts:25) is also not what the code implements even for the lucky ids: multiples of 4 are active once per 8 ticks, i.e. every 2 layer calls. _Recommended change:_ 
- **gameTick's `state.entities = allAlive` destroys entities pushed directly onto state.entities during the tick — trade-caravan carriers never survive, so exports are deducted every 8 days with no delivery** — _Impact:_ `state.entities = allAlive` silently deletes the carrier at the end of the departure tick, so `hasActiveCarrier` (tradeCaravans.ts:256-259, `state.entities.some(...)`) is false on the very next systems pulse; because `spawnCaravan` returned true, `route.nextDepartureTick` was cleared (tradeCaravans.ts:275-277), so the route re-schedules a fresh departure ~8 days later (`scheduleTradeRouteDeparture`, EVENT_INTERVAL.tradeRoute). Every departure first calls `deductExports(state, route)` (tradeCaravans.ts:231) after `canAffordExports`, so the colony loses the route's wood/stone/food/gold every 8 days forever: no carrier ever walks, `applyImports` (tradeCaravans.ts:336) never runs, `caravansCompleted`/`tradeCaravansCompleted`/`goldFromTradeRoutes` never increase, and no refund is possible. `getCaravanMoveTarget`/`tryAdvanceCaravanLeg` (humanTick.ts:445) are dead code as a result. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Realtime layer performs a full-population scan and rewrites residence `occupants` every tick while a Moon Howler is active** — _Impact:_ SIMULATION_AUTHORITY section 3 assigns residence occupancy writes to the `dayCycle.ts` residence functions scheduled from `tickLayerAssign` (4x/day) plus immediate entries on place/recruit/death/divorce/arrest (confirmed by decisionRegistry.ts:78-89, scheduledFrom 'tickLayerAssign.syncResidenceOccupants + assignMissingResidences'), and section 4/9 forbid full-population scans in the realtime path. This block adds a second, realtime schedule for that write on every tick of every full-moon night (~30 ticks), and it is redundant: the same reconciliation already runs at the form change itself (moonHowler.ts:971-974, 1025-1028, 1056-1059) and at each assign pulse, and a werewolf-form howler has `residenceBuildingId === undefined` (moonHowler.ts:382, forceMoonHowlerOutside line 303), so it can never contribute an occupant id — the write can only reproduce the current value while allocating a fresh `occupants` array per residence per tick (array-identity churn for UI/render memoization) and dirtying the authoritative worker state every tick. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Hour-granular full-moon gate is evaluated on all three ticks of hour 20 — replacement curse rolls 3x per full moon and the 'Full Moon!' announcement repeats 3x** — _Impact:_ With no active curse the realtime layer rolls `rng() < MOON_HOWLER_REPLACEMENT_CHANCE` (0.15) three times per full moon, so the effective per-full-moon replacement probability is 1 - 0.85^3 = 0.386 -- 2.6x the declared constant and far from the constant's own stated budget of '~1-2 replacements per year' (moonHowler.ts:77-84) and section 5's 'rare replacement roll'. Separately, whenever a cursed howler is active (huntingTonight true at ticks 61 and 62), the player receives three identical '🌝 Full Moon!' big-news cards and three event-log entries per full moon. The cure rite does not have this problem because it is rate-limited by `state.lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick` (lines 673-678). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Per-tick full-population scans feed a decision that can only fire at nightfall on a full-moon day** — _Impact:_ Three O(N) scans plus two array allocations of length N run on every one of the 72 ticks/day in the realtime layer even though the curse decision needs them at most once per full-moon night, which is exactly what section 4 and the section 9 pre-merge checklist ('No new full-population scan was added to a realtime path') forbid. The maintainers already removed one per-tick O(N) rebuild from this function (tests/moonHowler.byTypeReuse.test.ts), so this is the same class of regression; the wasted work is proportional to the whole living-entity set and runs all year on every tick. _Recommended change:_ 

### X3-invariants (8)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| high | `prisonGuardDuty.ts` | 58-61 | invariant-risk | Prison escape clears prisonBuildingId but leaves the id in prison.occupants | fixed — L51 (low-2) |
| high | `tradeCaravans.ts` | 231-248 | mutation-safety | spawnCaravan's carrier entity is discarded by the end-of-tick entity swap, so exports are spent and imports never arrive | fixed — H5 |
| medium | `simulation/humanLifecycle.ts` | 154 | invariant-risk | Newborn gets residenceBuildingId but is invisible to the assign layer, so a birth on a daily tick leaves residents/occupants inconsistent | fixed — low-3 |
| medium | `humanLifecycleCleanup.ts` | 145-169 | invariant-risk | Death cleanup never clears the survivor's youthLovePartnerId, leaving a one-sided youth-love link at the daily collector | fixed — low-3 |
| medium | `simulation/simulationInvariants.ts` | 281-286 | invariant-risk | Leader-residency check has no exemption for the leader's temporary Moon Howler form | fixed — lead review (follow-up) |
| medium | `settlerInteractionActions.ts` | 108-115 | invariant-risk | Debug curse command can create a second living cursed Moon Howler | fixed — low-3 |
| low | `buildingStaffingActions.ts` | 85-95 | logic-error | Manual builder assignment omits the pregnancy/alive checks its sibling paths enforce | fixed — M3 (B1 staffing) |
| low | `defenseStructures.ts` | 117-137 | dead-code | pruneDeadBarracksOccupants is never called and would break the reverse occupants invariant if it were | fixed — L15 (low-2) |

- **Prison escape clears prisonBuildingId but leaves the id in prison.occupants** — _Impact:_ tickPrisonGuardDuty runs inside the daily layer (dailyWorldEvents.ts:129 <- tickLayerDaily.ts:81), so at the collector run in the same tick the escaped settler is still listed in prison.occupants with prisonBuildingId undefined and homeBuildingId undefined (arrestForScandal cleared it, humanRelationships.ts:1192). simulationInvariants.ts:139-145 therefore reports 'human N in prison #M is neither prisoner (prisonBuildingId) nor guard (homeBuildingId)' — §5's occupants-vs-ownership invariant is false until the next assign pulse (up to 18 ticks, 6 in-game hours) rebuilds the list via workforce.syncJobBuildingOccupants. Same missing cleanup as the 'stale id left in building.occupants' class the audit targets. _Recommended change:_ 
- **spawnCaravan's carrier entity is discarded by the end-of-tick entity swap, so exports are spent and imports never arrive** — _Impact:_ spawnCaravan is reached from tickTradeCaravans (tickLayerSystems.ts:142), i.e. during a tick, but gameTick.ts:234 unconditionally does `state.entities = allAlive`, and allAlive (gameTick.ts:206-212) contains only the pre-tick aliveEntities plus ctx.newEntities. The carrier is in neither, so it is dropped the same tick; it is never processed by humanTick (humanTick.ts:445-456) and never reaches applyImports (line 336). The deducted export goods are lost, route.caravanCarrierId is left pointing at an entity no longer in state.entities (cleared only by the next systems pulse, hasActiveCarrier line 258), and every departure pushes one more permanent ghost into state.entityById (indexLivingEntity -> ensureEntityByIdMap is cached, killHuman/removeCarrier never see it) — a per-departure leak. This is exactly the mechanism the codebase already documented and fixed for the autumn herd (migration.ts:56-57 'BUG-12: gameTick replaces state.entities with allAlive ... pushing into state.entities would discard the herd'). Net effect: no trade caravan ever completes a round trip (caravansCompleted / Trade Empire victory unreachable). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Newborn gets residenceBuildingId but is invisible to the assign layer, so a birth on a daily tick leaves residents/occupants inconsistent** — _Impact:_ tickPregnancyAndBirth runs in the realtime layer (humanTick.ts:801) and only appends the child to ctx.newEntities, so it is absent from state.entities, ctx.byType and ctx.playerHumans for the rest of the tick. tickLayerAssign then rebuilds the residence lists from the stale bucket: tickLayerAssign.ts:30-35 `const humanBucket = byType[EntityType.Human]; ... syncResidenceOccupants(allHumans, updatedBuildings)` replaces every residence occupants array (residencyReconciliation.ts:116) with a list that cannot contain the newborn. On a tick that is also a daily boundary (state.tick % 72 === 0 — the assign interval is 18, so it always coincides) the daily collector sees the child in state.entities with residenceBuildingId set but missing from that residence's occupants and reports 'human N residenceBuildingId B missing from that residence's occupants' (simulationInvariants.ts:191-195). The mismatch persists until the next assign pulse (up to 18 ticks) and also hides the baby from occupants consumers (housingDiagnostics, night effects, resident panels). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Death cleanup never clears the survivor's youthLovePartnerId, leaving a one-sided youth-love link at the daily collector** — _Impact:_ Neither finalizeHumanDeath (lines 42-98) nor reconcileFamilyReferencesAfterRemoval (lines 145-169) clears `youthLovePartnerId`, and killHuman (line 182) calls both. When a 12-17 year old is killed inside the daily layer after advanceYouthLove has already run (tickLayerDaily.ts:53 does the reconciliation, line 81 tickDailyWorldEvents kills: raids via frontierCombat.ts:570 from tickPendingRaidEvents, disasters via worldEvents.ts:272), the surviving sweetheart keeps `youthLovePartnerId = <dead id>`. The dead settler is pruned from state.entities at gameTick.ts:234 while youthLovePartnerId is not, so the collector reports 'human B youthLovePartnerId A references a missing or invalid settler' (simulationInvariants.ts:216-224) — §5's 'a youth-love link joins two living colony settlers' is false until the next day's advanceYouthLove reconciliation. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Leader-residency check has no exemption for the leader's temporary Moon Howler form** — _Impact:_ transformToWerewolfForm clears `human.residenceBuildingId = undefined` (moonHowler.ts:381-383) and isActingVillageHead deliberately accepts a cursed werewolf as acting head (villageLeadership.ts:357-359, covered by the test at simulation.invariants.test.ts:288). The daily boundary tick is hour 0, and isFullMoonNight(colonyDay, 0) is true for the morning after a full-moon day (dayCycleConstants.ts:28-34), so a cursed leader is still transformed when the collector runs (transform at hour 20 of day D, revert at hour 6 of D+1). leader.prisonBuildingId is also cleared by the transform, so the guard at line 284 does not exempt it and the collector reports a false 'not residing in the Leader's House' violation for a fully legal state; the turn-around sync (tickLayerDaily.ts:59 -> leaderHouse.collectLeaderHousehold, which requires type Human) cannot repair it while the leader is a werewolf. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Debug curse command can create a second living cursed Moon Howler** — _Impact:_ canMoonHowlerCurse (moonHowler.ts:253-263) only checks the target, never the colony, so `spawnMoonHowlerDebug` (registered as a production command op in simWorker/commands.ts:99/416 and wired to the UI in App.tsx:1942) curses a second settler while a curse is already live. §5 states there is at most one living cursed Moon Howler, and the daily collector reports 'multiple living Moon Howlers: 2' (simulationInvariants.ts:262-267); the replacement owner in moonHowler.ts:1016 explicitly requires `activeCursed === 0`, so the command is a second, ungated owner of the same decision (the second path at line 129 spawns a cursed settler for a similar effect). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Manual builder assignment omits the pregnancy/alive checks its sibling paths enforce** — _Impact:_ isPlayerHuman (playerHuman.ts:12-17) is a type/faction predicate and does not test `alive` or `pregnant`. Every other crew/job path excludes pregnancy — workforce.isEligibleIdleWorker (workforce.ts:58-68), assignWorkerTransition (workforce.ts:202) and assignBuilderInPlace (workforce.ts:399-409) — and addToConstructionCrew itself does not check pregnancy (workforce.ts:234-242), so the manual 'assign builder' command (assignBuilderToBuilding -> _applyBuilderAssignmentMut) can put a pregnant settler on a construction crew where auto-staffing never would. The same filter gap can select a dead settler: addToConstructionCrew returns false silently (workforce.ts:236) while lines 103-109 still announce 'Builder Assigned'. _Recommended change:_ 
- **pruneDeadBarracksOccupants is never called and would break the reverse occupants invariant if it were** — _Impact:_ No module calls this function (verified by repo-wide grep: only the definition matches), so the intended barracks-occupant pruning never runs — it is dead repair code. Worse, if it were called it would remove any barracks occupant who is not currently a non-imprisoned Soldier (e.g. a just-conscripted settler, or a Soldier serving a prison sentence whose homeBuildingId still points at the barracks) from `b.occupants` while leaving `homeBuildingId` set, which is precisely the mismatch simulationInvariants.ts:174-178 reports as 'homeBuildingId ... missing from that workplace's occupants'. _Recommended change:_ 

### X4-mutation-memory (6)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| high | `spatialGrid.ts` | 481-487 | mutation-safety | syncGrassRenderGrid never refreshes a reusable grid, but the render-SoA caller relies on it rebuilding every tick | fixed — low-1 |
| high | `spatialGrid.ts` | 489-501 | mutation-safety | Tree and grass sim grids are never reconciled, so mid-run spawns are invisible and dead trees are retained | fixed — L67 (low-1) |
| medium | `simWorker/simPrep.ts` | 103 | mutation-safety | extractSimPrep shallow-clones entities, so nested mutable state is aliased and applySimPrep does not actually roll back | fixed — L66 (low-1) |
| medium | `simWorker/simPrep.ts` | 10-87 | save-persistence | SimPrepKeys omits simulation state that a tick writes (yearlyStats, lifetimeStats, populationHistory) | fixed — worker-tick-rollback fix (lead) |
| medium | `tickLayerSystems.ts` | 684-704 | logic-error | Wildkin reproduces but is absent from REPRO_WILDLIFE_TYPES, so its population cap and scarcity boost are computed from a permanently zero count | fixed — lead review (follow-up) |
| medium | `simBuffers/simDelta.ts` | 52-139 | save-persistence | Worker tick delta carries neither huntVisuals nor chronicleChapters, so worker-authored state never reaches the display world | fixed — L59 (low-1) + M34 |

- **syncGrassRenderGrid never refreshes a reusable grid, but the render-SoA caller relies on it rebuilding every tick** — _Impact:_ In the default worker path (isGameWorkerEnabled() returns true unless VITE_USE_GAME_WORKER is off, GameWorkerHost.ts:570-579) the render grass index is frozen at the first rendered tick for the whole session: resolveSpatialGrid returns the same instance, so `grid !== existing` is false and rebuild is skipped, yet `grassGridTick = tick` claims freshness. Renderer draws grass exclusively from this grid (renderer/entityCache.ts:189-201 passes `[]` as the fallback list), so grass that spawns later (dailyGrassEcology.ts:57-65, groupEvents.ts:1744-1748) is never drawn, and grass that dies later keeps being drawn forever because the retained tick-1 shims were built with `let flags = RENDER_FLAG_ALIVE` (packRenderSoA.ts:48) and are never refreshed. Player-visible: permanent ghost grass / missing new grass after the first frame. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Tree and grass sim grids are never reconciled, so mid-run spawns are invisible and dead trees are retained** — _Impact:_ update()/reconcile() are only ever driven by syncSpatialGridEntity (grass + mobile) and by syncMobileSimGrid/syncHumanSocialGrid; tickLayerRealtime.ts:148-158 calls syncGrassRenderGrid/syncTreeGrid with a reusable instance, which no-ops. Therefore: (a) the 15 trees and 30 grass spawned by the yearly 'nature_boom' event (groupEvents.ts:1738-1748 push straight into allAlive, bypassing pushNewEntity) never enter treeGrid/grassGrid, and since findClosestInEntityGrid ignores its fallback array whenever the grid is non-null (simQueries.ts:113-117), grazers (tickLayerSystems.ts:540-548) cannot find the new grass and settlers cannot visit the new trees (humanTick.ts:1403-1412) for the rest of the session; (b) trees killed by clearTreesUnderFootprint when a building is placed (buildingPlacementActions.ts:95-98) are never removed from treeGrid, retaining one dead Entity per chopped tree for the session and tripping EntitySpatialGrid.validateInvariant ('stale dead entity') — which is never run for treeGrid because assertSpatialGridInvariants only validates grassGrid and mobileGrid (spatialGrid.ts:819-829). _Recommended change:_ 
- **extractSimPrep shallow-clones entities, so nested mutable state is aliased and applySimPrep does not actually roll back** — _Impact:_ Entity `skills`, `friendships`, `feuds`, `childrenIds`, `childhoodFriendsIds` are mutated in place during a tick (skills.ts:37 `skills[job] = ...`, relationships.ts:105-109, residencySelection.ts:215) and are shared with the backup object, so a rollback restore keeps them. The same aliasing applies to object-valued arrays that are copied shallowly: `tradeRoutes: [...(state.tradeRoutes ?? [])]` (line 149) while tradeCaravans.ts:287-305 mutates `route.caravanLeg`/`caravanWaitTicks`/`caravansCompleted` in place, `disasters: [...(state.disasters ?? [])]` (line 150) while worldEvents.ts:299 does `d.progress++`, plus visitorGroups/rivalSettlements. Concrete path: gameWorker.ts:343-350 calls applySimPrep after a *successful* gameTick when the render buffer pool is exhausted; `state.tick`/`nextEntityId` are restored, so the next tick re-runs the same tick number and the same in-place skill/relationship gains are applied a second time (skills.ts:37 has no idempotence guard). _Recommended change:_ 
- **SimPrepKeys omits simulation state that a tick writes (yearlyStats, lifetimeStats, populationHistory)** — _Impact:_ applySimPrep restores only its own key list, so the worker's rollback path (gameWorker.ts:339, 345, 355 — e.g. render-buffer-pool exhaustion after a completed gameTick) leaves the failed tick's year-rollover effects in place while state.tick is rewound. When the restored tick is re-run it crosses the same year boundary again, pushing a duplicate YearlyStats entry (and running the >50 shift twice) and double-counting lifetimeStats; populationHistory also gains a second sample for the same tick number. simDelta.ts:317-318 already ships yearlyStats/lifetimeStats to the main thread, so the field list exists — only the rollback backup is missing them. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Wildkin reproduces but is absent from REPRO_WILDLIFE_TYPES, so its population cap and scarcity boost are computed from a permanently zero count** — _Impact:_ Wildkin passes the reproduction gate (WILDLIFE_TICK_TYPES includes it, tickLayerSystems.ts:78; speciesConfig.ts:190-192 gives reproductionCooldown cd(288), threshold 200, chance 0.008) but wildlifeTypePopulation() returns 0 + 0 - 0 for it forever, so capacityFactor is always 1.0 and scarcityBoost is always the 1.55 scarcity multiplier: the intended `maxPop = 35` fallback cap never binds and wildkin breed at a permanently boosted rate once two are within 80px, growing without bound (matching the pattern the comment 'Keep the buffer bounded' style guards elsewhere exist for). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Worker tick delta carries neither huntVisuals nor chronicleChapters, so worker-authored state never reaches the display world** — _Impact:_ huntVisuals is written by sim code that runs in the worker (huntvisuals.ts:61 `state.huntVisuals.unshift(entry)` from dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:412 and humanHuntingBehavior.ts:125) and read by the renderer through RenderSnapshot.huntVisuals (renderSnapshot.ts:112 → renderer/humans.ts:353, drawn unconditionally from entityComposite.ts:42). Because the delta never carries it and GameWorkerHost applies only deltas plus invalidate/hydrate (GameWorkerHost.ts:546-548), the display world's huntVisuals stays at its last full-load value, so the hunting arrow that BUG_REPORTS/2026-08-28-hunting-projectile-visual.md was resolved for only appears in non-worker mode. chronicleChapters has the same shape: advanceValleyChronicle writes it daily in the worker (tickLayerDaily.ts:65, valleyChronicle.ts:89) but neither extract/apply delta nor prep reference it, so ValleyChroniclePanel.tsx:6 and useTransientGameFeedback.ts:83-144 (chapter moments) never see a completion until a save reload. _Recommended change:_ 

### X5-persistence-worker (10)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| medium | `saveLoad.ts` | 151-161; 163-173; 104 | save-persistence | Compact worldMap save discards in-play terrain edits, so building efficiency changes across a reload | deferred — the terrain generator is being replaced next version (save-format decision) |
| medium | `saveSchema.ts` | 4-26; src/game/moonHowler.ts:693, 812, 673, 659 | save-persistence | Moon Howler exorcism cooldown and priest-flee deadline are never persisted | fixed — M20 |
| medium | `simBuffers/simDelta.ts` | 52-139; 62-63; src/game/gameTick.ts:243-244 | save-persistence | Workforce/mood HUD fields written every tick are never transferred to the main thread | fixed — low-1 (X5) |
| medium | `simBuffers/simDelta.ts` | 52-139; src/game/valleyChronicle.ts:89; src/game/huntvisuals.ts:61 | save-persistence | chronicleChapters and huntVisuals are written in the worker but omitted from the tick delta | fixed — L59 (low-1) + M34 |
| medium | `simWorker/simPrep.ts` | 102-103; 141-149; 172 | mutation-safety | extractSimPrep shallow-copies entities/elements, so rollback cannot undo nested mutations | fixed — L66 (low-1) |
| medium | `simWorker/simPrep.ts` | 10-89; src/game/gameTick.ts:83-86; src/game/tickLayerRealtime.ts:204 | mutation-safety | simPrep omits fields the tick writes, so a failed tick is only partially rolled back | fixed — worker-tick-rollback fix (lead) |
| low | `saveLoad.ts` | 80-82; 478; 496-537 | dead-code | All legacy save-migration branches are unreachable behind the exact-version gate | fixed — low-4 |
| low | `saveLoad.ts` | 344-346; 218-288 | edge-case | Missing _ticksPerDay defaults to the legacy 24-tick day and rescales every tick deadline 3x | fixed — low-4 |

- **Church 'one-time' migration runs on every load and unassigns the player's priest** — _Impact:_ In a current-version save a player Church can only be staffed by the manually chosen priest, and that priest is stored exactly the way the destructive branch detects: `assignIdleWorkerToBuilding` sets `occupants=[priestId]` + `homeBuildingId=church.id` + job Priest (tests/church.manualStaffing.test.ts:107-113, tests/workerCommand.roundtrip.test.ts:143-150). Because the call is unconditional and the same-version gate (saveLoad.ts:80-82) means no pre-0.6.4 save can reach it, every load of a save with a staffed Church empties the Church and resets the priest to settler (`removeWorkerTransition` clears homeBuildingId/job/occupation). The recorded migration id only dedupes the log line (applySaveMigration, saveLoad.ts:489-494), not the destructive clear, and `assignMissingWorkers` never refills a manual workplace, so the priest stays idle after every reload. Player-visible: the priest assignment is lost on each load/save cycle. _Recommended change:_ 
- **visitorQuest is written by the sim but carried by neither the save allow-list nor the worker delta** — _Impact:_ The traveling-smith quest is created inside the sim (groupEvents.ts:416 -> maybeStartVisitorQuest) and expires day-by-day (visitorQuest.ts:43-50). It is not in the save payload, so saving during an active quest silently deletes it: the card (App.tsx:1545 `const q = getVisitorQuest(world);`) disappears and a later `deliverVisitorQuest` command returns false (visitorQuest.ts:55) with a valid payment in store. It is also absent from every SimTickDelta field (simDelta.ts:52-139), so in worker mode the authoritative quest never reaches the main-thread world at all: the card cannot appear and virtualPlayer's quest step (virtualPlayer.ts:847) can never fire, until the next full-world export (which only happens on save, gameLoop.ts:654-683). Same class as the documented whitelist-miss bug in tests/saveMigration.roundtrip.test.ts. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Compact worldMap save discards in-play terrain edits, so building efficiency changes across a reload** — _Impact:_ Every placement mutates authoritative terrain: `clearTreesUnderFootprint` converts Forest/DarkForest to Grassland under each footprint (buildingPlacementActions.ts:100-114, called from startBuilding:162 and placeStripChain:300). The save keeps only seed/preset/size, and restoreWorldMapFromSave regenerates pristine tiles from the seed (saveLoad.ts:168-172), so those edits are lost. `getTerrainEfficiencyMultiplier` reads the tile under the building (terrainSystems.ts:59-75) and is used for production (dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:314, workshopEconomy.ts:48): a Farm on a cleared forest tile drops 1.4 -> 0.8 and a LumberMill rises 0.9 -> 1.5 after a save/load, so the same colony produces different output before and after reloading (and forest art returns under the village). _Recommended change:_ 
- **Moon Howler exorcism cooldown and priest-flee deadline are never persisted** — _Impact:_ Both are tick deadlines read by later decisions: `const last = state.lastMoonHowlerExorcismTick ?? -9999;` (moonHowler.ts:673) rate-limits the rite, and `state.tick < (state.moonHowlerPriestsFleeUntil ?? -1)` suppresses it when priests fled (moonHowler.ts:659, humanTick.ts:609). Saving and reloading on a full-moon night therefore resets the cooldown so the Church rite can be re-attempted immediately on the same night (save-scumming re-rolls `rollMoonHowlerRiteOutcome`, can kill/cure repeatedly), and a priest who just watched a colleague die stops being scared after a reload. Being absent from simPrep, they also survive a failed tick/command rollback. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Workforce/mood HUD fields written every tick are never transferred to the main thread** — _Impact:_ In worker mode the main thread's world is only advanced by applySimTickDelta (gameLoop.ts:546), so these fields keep their creation values forever: worldGen.ts:605-606 sets workingSettlers/idleSettlers to 0 and villageHappiness is undefined. PopulationPanel.tsx:110-111 (`const working = state.workingSettlers; const idle = state.idleSettlers;`) shows 0 working / 0 idle and line 154 renders village mood as `Math.round(state.villageHappiness ?? 50)` = 50 permanently; citizenOverview.ts:171 reads `world.villageCanHeat !== false` = true regardless of the worker's winter heating result (dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:111). They are in simPrep (rollback) but in no save key and no delta, so only a save-time full export refreshes them. _Recommended change:_ 
- **chronicleChapters and huntVisuals are written in the worker but omitted from the tick delta** — _Impact:_ Both are produced only inside the sim (valleyChronicle from the daily layer; addHuntVisual from humanHuntingBehavior.ts:125 / dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:412, pruned at tickLayerRealtime.ts:238) but are read from the main-thread world: ValleyChroniclePanel.tsx:6 and useTransientGameFeedback.ts:83 (`world.chronicleChapters`) and the renderer via renderSnapshot.ts:112 `huntVisuals: world.huntVisuals` built from `this.world` at gameLoop.ts:943. With the worker active (the default, isGameWorkerEnabled) new chronicle chapters and hunt arrow visuals never reach the UI: the chronicle panel/chapter moments stay empty and hunt animations never draw until a save exports the whole world. _Recommended change:_ 
- **extractSimPrep shallow-copies entities/elements, so rollback cannot undo nested mutations** — _Impact:_ Nested objects keep the same reference between the backup and the live world, so gameWorker.ts:228/360 `applySimPrep(world, prepBackup)` restores only top-level properties. In-place nested writers during ticks/commands include skills.ts:37 `skills[job] = ...`, humanLifecycle.ts:40 `parent.childrenIds.push(childId)`, relationships.ts:108 `a.friendships[friendKey(b.id)] = next`, groupEvents.ts:516 `group.daysLeft--`, rivalProfiles.ts:89 `ledger.food -= 5`, and renffrOmen.letters. If a tick or command throws after one of these (e.g. a birth is rolled back at entity level but the parent's `childrenIds` still lists the removed child), the authoritative worker world stays mutated although the tick/command is reported failed; the next delta ships the 'failed' change to the player, or a retry applies it twice. _Recommended change:_ 
- **simPrep omits fields the tick writes, so a failed tick is only partially rolled back** — _Impact:_ The declared purpose of extractSimPrep is failure recovery (simPrep.ts:7-9), but a tick that throws after the year-rollover block is only partially restored: `tick`/`dayInYear` come back (they are in the list) while yearlyStats/lifetimeStats keep the first application. The tick error then falls back to main-thread ticks (gameLoop.ts:385-404 via GameWorkerHost.handleMessage -> onWorkerFault), the next gameTick re-crosses the same day boundary and re-runs recordYearlyStats/updateLifetimeStats, so the year is recorded twice and lifetime birth/death/marriage totals are double-counted. The spurious populationHistory sample survives too and is shipped to the player because populationHistory is in the delta. _Recommended change:_ 
- **All legacy save-migration branches are unreachable behind the exact-version gate** — _Impact:_ parseSaveJson rejects every save whose `_version` is not 0.6.4 (a policy pinned by tests/saveVersion.gate.test.ts:19-23), so loadGameFromParsed can only ever see `saveVersion === GAME_VERSION`: forceAgeMigration is always false, the v0.4/v0.4.1/v0.4.2/v0.5.0/v0.5.1 applySaveMigration branches and the legacy `clearAutoFilledChurches` call can never apply to a real save. This is dead code that hides the fact that the only remaining same-version repairs are the iron backfills at lines 460-466, and it is the reason the destructive Church block above runs unconditionally instead of behind its version check. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Missing _ticksPerDay defaults to the legacy 24-tick day and rescales every tick deadline 3x** — _Impact:_ The version gate and the cadence field are validated separately: parseSaveJson accepts a payload that has the right `_version` but no `_ticksPerDay` (exactly the minimal object used in tests/saveVersion.gate.test.ts:15), and loadGameFromParsed then treats it as a 24-tick legacy save, multiplying `world.tick` and every absolute deadline by TICKS_PER_DAY/24 = 3 in migrateTickTimeline (world.tick:226, pregnancyProgress:245, prisonerUntilTick/scandalCooldownUntilTick/griefUntilTick/hotelStayUntilTick:238-241, raid/diplomacy/festival/election expiries:260-287). A hand-edited or partially-written same-version file therefore loads with a tripled calendar and pregnancies/prison sentences three times too long instead of being rejected. _Recommended change:_ 

### X6-duplication-deadcode (10)

| Severity | File | Lines | Category | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| high | `economy.ts` | 8-21 | dead-code | updateStorageCaps is never called: live storage caps and food spoilage never recompute (Barn/Silo/Wood Storehouse deliver nothing) | fixed — H3 |
| high | `settlerInteractionActions.ts` | 107-136 | authority-violation | Second Moon Howler spawn path: the debug command curses a settler even while a cursed Howler already exists | fixed — low-3 |
| medium | `frontierCombat.ts` | 679-701 | resource-economy | Hand-rolled storage-cap clamps instead of addCappedResource: raid spoils go negative and subtract resources when stock is above the cap | fixed — L26 (low-5) |
| medium | `groupEvents.ts` | 1313-1334 | authority-violation | groupEvents privately re-implements the affordability, deduction and storage-headroom rules with weaker guards | fixed — L32/L33 (low-2) |
| medium | `workforce.ts` | 580-584 | authority-violation | assignAllWorkers: dead second auto-staffing entry point that also fills manual-staff buildings | fixed — lead review (deleted) |
| medium | `humanChat.ts` | 343, 485-496, 509-526, 529-543, 546-559 | dead-code | Seven unreferenced legacy chat/courtship implementations survive the social-feel extraction | fixed — low-6 |
| low | `humanLifecycleCleanup.ts` | 188-193 | authority-violation | isLivingMarriagePartner duplicates moonHowler.isSettlerRelationshipEntity verbatim (with a third variant elsewhere) | fixed — low-3 |
| low | `frontierCombat.ts` | 193-197 | dead-code | countArmedMilitia: dead militia-count rule that disagrees with the militia owner | fixed — low-5 |
| low | `villageLeadership.ts` | 34-36 | authority-violation | Second calendar definition: DAYS_PER_YEAR / MONTHS_PER_YEAR / DAYS_PER_MONTH re-declared locally | fixed — lead review (follow-up) |
| low | `moonHowler.ts` | 140-143, 219-247 | dead-code | Dead/duplicated Moon Howler window helpers (next-full-moon, cure tick, transform, revert) | fixed — low-3 |

- **updateStorageCaps is never called: live storage caps and food spoilage never recompute (Barn/Silo/Wood Storehouse deliver nothing)** — _Impact:_ As §6 forbids duplicate rules and the decision registry lists `storageMax`/`foodSpoilageRate` as daily production writes, the game runs on frozen world-gen values: Barns (`src/game/buildings.ts:205 '... extends wood storage +300'`), Siloes (`buildings.ts:218 'Passive food storage bonus.'`) and Wood Storehouses (`buildings.ts:231 '+800 wood storage'`) add no storage at all, spoilage stays 3% instead of the audited 2% (and never drops to 0.8% with a Silo), and gold stays capped at 2000 (shown in `src/components/GameHeader.tsx:315`) instead of 20000 — exactly the numbers the passing test asserts but nothing produces. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Second Moon Howler spawn path: the debug command curses a settler even while a cursed Howler already exists** — _Impact:_ Because `canMoonHowlerCurse` (moonHowler.ts:253-263) only rejects the already-cursed settler, pressing the button while one cursed Howler lives curses a second living settler, breaking §5 'There is at most one living cursed Moon Howler' (detected by `src/game/simulation/simulationInvariants.ts:250-258`) and §3's single owner for curse/spawn (moonHowler.ts). It also ignores the §4 `full-moon-event` cadence — a Howler can be created at any hour of any day. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Hand-rolled storage-cap clamps instead of addCappedResource: raid spoils go negative and subtract resources when stock is above the cap** — _Impact:_ Stock legitimately exceeds the cap because `src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts:180-182 refundHalfBuildingCost` adds wood/stone/gold with no cap check (called on strip-build replacement at line 280). With `resources.wood` above `storageMax.wood`, `room` is negative, `gained.wood = Math.min(negative, spoils.wood)` is negative, and `state.resources.wood += gained.wood` therefore DELETES wood on a successful raid while `formatLootParts(gained)` (line 1167) logs a negative haul — the opposite of the comment `/** Add spoils ... (respects storage caps). */`. The `Math.min` variants silently destroy the same excess instead of leaving it untouched. _Recommended change:_ 
- **groupEvents privately re-implements the affordability, deduction and storage-headroom rules with weaker guards** — _Impact:_ Two owners for one economy rule, and the private copies lack the `?? 0` guards: `canPayTradeCost` tests `(state.resources[key] as number) < cost`, so a missing key passes (undefined < n is false) and `applyTradeCost` then writes `Math.max(0, (state.resources[key] as number) - cost)` = NaN into `state.resources[key]`; `storageRoom` returns NaN for a missing `storageMax[type]`, so the village-request gate at line 184 (`storageRoom(state, 'food') < VILLAGE_REQUEST_PROVISIONS_FOOD`) is false and an unaffordable/over-cap accept can pass. `canAfford`/`deductResource` cannot do either. _Recommended change:_ 
- **assignAllWorkers: dead second auto-staffing entry point that also fills manual-staff buildings** — _Impact:_ `includeManualStaff = true` skips the guard `src/game/workforce.ts:558 if (!includeManualStaff && isManualStaffingBuilding(building)) continue;`, so this path would generically auto-fill manual buildings (the Church priest selection §5 protects) and it passes `venueSchedules = undefined`, bypassing the Tavern/Hotel auto-staff targets. It is a second workforce rule exposed on the public barrel with no caller. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Seven unreferenced legacy chat/courtship implementations survive the social-feel extraction** — _Impact:_ A second, parallel chat/courtship-phrase implementation (hardcoded pairs, legacy `_legacyDurationTicks`/`_durationTicks` knobs, `@deprecated` banners) remains exported, so a future caller can bypass the dialogue-tree owner and its `isDialogueBusy`/duration bookkeeping; the underscore parameters hide that the courtship pair is no longer chosen from a table. _Recommended change:_ 
- **isLivingMarriagePartner duplicates moonHowler.isSettlerRelationshipEntity verbatim (with a third variant elsewhere)** — _Impact:_ The 'a cursed settler still counts as a marriage/social partner' rule has three owners. If the Moon Howler form rules change (e.g. aligning with `isActiveMoonHowler`, which also requires `alive`), `isLivingMarriagePartner` will not follow, and `reconcileOrphanedMarriages` (:205) can start clearing or keeping marriages differently from the social lookups it mirrors (a §5-adjacent marriage/pregnancy state divergence). _Recommended change:_ 
- **countArmedMilitia: dead militia-count rule that disagrees with the militia owner** — _Impact:_ This second militia rule is both unreachable and wrong: with iron swords researched (`hasIronSpears`/`hasStoneSpears` at combat.ts:119 terms) it returns 0 militia for a fully armed village, and it counts prisoners/children-free adults with no shields, guards or barricades. Reusing the exported name (it is on the public barrel) would feed raid math a number the owner never produced. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Second calendar definition: DAYS_PER_YEAR / MONTHS_PER_YEAR / DAYS_PER_MONTH re-declared locally** — _Impact:_ Election campaign length and the years-until-election figure derive from a private 30×12 calendar, so retuning `Time.DAYS_PER_YEAR`/`DAYS_PER_SEASON` in the declared single source (gameConstants.ts) would silently desync elections from the world calendar. No test imports this constant, so the drift would be invisible. _Recommended change:_ 
- **Dead/duplicated Moon Howler window helpers (next-full-moon, cure tick, transform, revert)** — _Impact:_ Four exported names encode one full-moon window rule, so a reader cannot tell which is authoritative; the deprecated alias can drift from `isMoonHowlerCureWindow`, and the test pins a `isMoonHowlerRevertTick(6)` contract that the simulation does not actually call (reverts happen through `shouldMoonHowlerTransform`). This is a §3 'one owner per decision' smell inside the declared owner module. _Recommended change:_

## Claims that were checked and refuted

The verification pass rejected 13 candidate findings. They are recorded here so the same non-issues are not re-reported later:

- `src/game/leaderHouse.ts` — Stepping down a leader in cursed werewolf form leaves the Moon Howler snapshot saying 'leader', which restores a stale LEADER_OCCUPATION on revert → **
- `src/game/simulation/humanLifecycle.ts` — birthsCompletedThisInterval is recorded before the outcome branches, so stillbirths count as completed births → **
- `src/game/workforce.ts` — releasePrisoners runs full housing + workforce reconciliation from the realtime layer → **
- `src/game/economyLedger.ts` — Ledger/foodHistory are outside the worker rollback snapshot, so a reverted tick double-counts in the ledger → **
- `src/game/gameTick.ts` — Entities that die inside the daily layer after its prune stay in `state.entities` as dead for up to a full day → **
- `src/game/simulation/humanLifecycle.ts` — birthsCompletedThisInterval also counts stillbirths and Wildkin births → **
- `src/game/workforce.ts` — Construction crew cap reuses maxOccupants, which for housing is the bed capacity, so a house site absorbs 6-12 builders by draining workplaces → **
- `src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts` — placeStripChain removes a replaced building itself, skipping every cleanup the removal owner performs, and pays an uncapped refund → **
- `src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts` — placeStripChain indexes BUILDING_CONFIGS with the payload-supplied segment.placeType without a guard → **
- `src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts` — Naive radial scan lacks the grid's non-finite/negative radius guard, so grid and naive modes disagree for NaN inputs → **
- `src/game/simulation/decisionRegistry.ts` — The commands decision declares `building.occupants` as its own write, duplicating the workforce owner → **
- `src/game/saveLoad.ts` — Defense research migration is a no-op: mergeCombatResearchNodes' return value is discarded → **
- `src/game/simulation/humanLifecycle.ts` — Birth outcomes draw from unseeded Math.random while the same birth creates the child from the seeded stream → **

## Withdrawn after owner review (1)

These findings were confirmed by the audit and its verifier, but the owner ruled the code correct and corrected the document that contradicted it. They are kept here so the same non-issue is not re-reported, and because the audit trail matters more than the score.

### W1. YOUTH_LOVE_MIN_AGE = 12 lets 12-13 year olds enter youth love, violating the section 5 invariant and the feature spec

- **File:** `src/game/simulation/humanRelationships.ts` — lines 441, 469-492, 533-556, 1229 | 441, 473, 553 · original severity `high` · withdrawn — intended behaviour (documentation corrected)
- **Why the audit reported it:** SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md section 5 states "Youth love begins only from age 14 through 17" (and section 3 row: "Youth love (ages 14-17)"); docs/archive/YOUTH_LOVE_FEATURE.md:29 lists ages 12-13 as "Not eligible". advanceYouthLove iterates every entry of ctx.playerHumans with no isJuvenile filter (line 541), so a 12- or 13-year-old passes isEligibleForYouthLove, can be matched (YOUTH_LOVE_MAX_AGE_GAP = 4 at line 443 even lets a 12-year-old pair with a 16-year-old via line 490), receives mutual youthLovePartnerId/progress 1 plus the "became sweethearts" Chronicle event (lines 599-611), and that link then blocks adult courtship at isEligibleToCourt (line 1229). No existing test covers youth-love ages, so the drift is unguarded.

- **Owner resolution.** Owner decision (2026-09-13): the youth-love minimum age of 12 is intended, so `YOUTH_LOVE_MIN_AGE = 12` in `src/game/simulation/humanRelationships.ts` stays as it is. The defect was in the documents, which still described the older age-14 gate, and they were corrected to 12: `docs/archive/SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md` §3 (ownership row) and §5 (youth-love invariant), `docs/archive/YOUTH_LOVE_FEATURE.md` (age timeline, start-eligibility age row, daily age-protection step, automated-coverage row) and the README feature table. The same decision then closed the other half of the same drift in **code** rather than in prose: youth conception now begins at 12 (`HUMAN_FERTILITY_START` = 12 with `YOUTH_CONCEPTION_MULTIPLIERS` 12: 0.25 and 13: 0.25, 14–17 unchanged), so ages 12–17 conceive only through the mutual youth-love gate, and an affair is now adult-only on **both** sides (`Relationship.AFFAIR_MIN_AGE` = 18) — which also resolves this report's affair-conception finding (M-tier, `humanRelationships.ts:789`). New regression tests: `tests/youthConception.ageFloor.test.ts` (4) and `tests/affairAge.adultOnly.test.ts` (4). No save field, format or migration change.
