Confirmed findings from this group: 9

Files read: `src/game/tradeCaravans.ts`, `src/game/travelingTheatre.ts`, `src/game/inventionFair.ts`, `src/game/visitorQuest.ts`, `src/game/rivalEvents.ts`, `src/game/rivalPresence.ts`, `src/game/rivalPeace.ts`, `src/game/rivalProfiles.ts`, `src/game/rumourLedger.ts`, `src/game/storyEvents.ts`, `src/game/storyHelpers.ts`, `src/game/eventLog.ts`, `src/game/dailyWorldEvents.ts`, `src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts`, `src/game/saveSchema.ts`, `src/game/saveLoad.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/GameWorkerHost.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/commands.ts`, `src/game/gameLoop.ts`, `src/game/gameTick.ts`, `src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts`, `src/game/entityCounts.ts`, `src/game/economy.ts`, `src/game/economy.ts`, `src/game/resourceUtils.ts`, `src/game/entityIndex.ts`, `src/game/entityFactory.ts`, `src/game/dayCycleClock.ts`, `src/game/groupEvents.ts`, `src/game/guidedCampaign.ts`, `src/game/playerHuman.ts`, `src/game/gameTypes.ts`, `src/App.tsx`, `tests/storyEvents.test.ts`, `tests/simDelta.storyRoundtrip.test.ts`, `tests/rivalProfiles.test.ts`, `tests/rivalDiplomacy.test.ts`, `tests/deterministicSeed.test.ts`

Read-only audit; nothing was created, modified, or deleted. Per-file verdicts: (1) tradeCaravans.ts — one confirmed resource defect (iron never imported) plus the storage-race question below; the departure/leg state machine, reservation at departure, and save-load rescheduling are otherwise consistent with ER-12 and the 72-tick day. (2) travelingTheatre.ts — broken stage-3 routing (high), inverted recent-event window, inert script flag. (3) inventionFair.ts — duplicate stage-2 cards with repeatable rewards (high), unused invention flag; its wood table and eligibility mirror are correctly shared. (4) visitorQuest.ts — not saved and not delivered to the main thread, so the feature is dead in worker mode; the expiry/delivery arithmetic itself is correct. (5) rivalEvents.ts — correctly gated to one new-calendar-day pass and the population recount/deer picker are sound, but every decision roll is unseeded. (6) rivalPresence.ts — presentation only, no findings (its `.slice(0, 3)` is correct precisely because eventLog is newest-first). (7) rivalPeace.ts — correct single-expression predicate. (8) rivalProfiles.ts — fortify threshold mismatch; create/normalize/clamp behaviour is otherwise correct and test-covered (tests/rivalProfiles.test.ts). (9) rumourLedger.ts — inverted recent-event scan; the response/truth reputation matrix (229-268) matches its own comments. No existing test covers trade caravans, the theatre, the invention fair, the rumour ledger, or the visitor quest, so none of the reported behaviour is locked in by a passing test (tests/rivalDiplomacy.test.ts, rivalProfiles.test.ts, storyEvents.test.ts, simDelta.storyRoundtrip.test.ts and deterministicSeed.test.ts were read and do not touch these paths).

Unresolved / needs a design decision: (a) tradeCaravans.ts:298 checks `canStoreImports` at the moment the carrier leaves the partner, but imports are applied at the hub on arrival (314) with a freshly recomputed trade multiplier (282 vs 314); storage can fill or the multiplier rise during the walk home, in which case addCappedResource (resourceUtils.ts:38-48) silently truncates goods the colony already paid for — I could not tell whether this residual window is intended (the ER-12 comment only claims exports are no longer at risk). (b) Whether the offscreen-human throttle (OFFSCREEN_HUMAN_THROTTLE = 24, humanTick.ts:384) making caravan travel and the 72-tick partner wait advance on only 1 tick in 24 once the merchant leaves the camera view is intended pacing for trade routes or an oversight; it makes round-trip time camera-dependent. (c) All authored-story modules stamp FLAG_OFFERED with `state.tick` and test `> 0`, so a tick-0 offer would read as unset; unreachable today because every MIN_DAY is >= 30, but it is a latent sentinel hazard shared by travelingTheatre/inventionFair/rumourLedger. (d) There is no row in SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md section 3 or in simulation/decisionRegistry.ts for visitor quests, trade routes, rival daily actions, or the authored story chains, so no declared owner/cadence contract bounds these writes — worth a documentation decision rather than a code change.

### A21-worker-persistence

Confirmed findings from this group: 6

Files read: `src/game/saveLoad.ts`, `src/game/virtualPlayer.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/commands.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/protocol.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/GameWorkerHost.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.node.ts`, `src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts`, `src/game/simBuffers/applyKinematics.ts`, `src/game/simBuffers/schema.ts`

VERDICT PER FILE
- src/game/saveLoad.ts — load/parse/migration chain is otherwise sound; one high defect (Church migration on every load) and one latent unit-scaling defect (pregnancyDueProgress / story tick deadlines).
- src/game/virtualPlayer.ts — pure, deterministic, fully delegated to domain owners; no defect found in its own logic (it is only starved by the missing `visitorQuest` delta field, reported against simDelta.ts).
- src/game/simWorker/commands.ts — validator is catalog-driven and the op set is exhaustively enforced against `WorkerCommand`; dispatch delegates every op to its owner; no defect found.
- src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts — backup/restore pair is complete field-wise but shallow per entity (reported); it is also taken on every tick where its rollback can never change the outcome.
- src/game/simWorker/protocol.ts — thin, consistent handshake/types; no defect found.
- src/game/simWorker/GameWorkerHost.ts — transport, ordering, idle-waiters and pool accounting are coherent; two low findings (presentation write-back, dead `pendingFocus`).
- src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.ts — message handling, rollback ordering and buffer release paths are correct; its per-tick `extractSimPrep` backup is dead weight (folded into the simPrep finding).
- src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.node.ts — the `self` polyfill, message queueing before load and transfer-list filtering are coherent; no defect found.
- src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts — three findings, all the same root cause: the delta omits worker-authored WorldState fields (`visitorQuest`, `huntVisuals`, `workingSettlers`/`idleSettlers`, `villageHappiness`/`villageCanHeat`, `chronicleChapters`) that still have live main-thread consumers.
- src/game/simBuffers/applyKinematics.ts — faithful SoA→catalog patch, guards missing/dead entities and sentinel values; no defect found.
- src/game/simBuffers/schema.ts — constants, header/field offsets and flag helpers are internally consistent; no defect found.

UNRESOLVED / DESIGN QUESTIONS
1. The delta is already large; should `visitorQuest`/`huntVisuals`/workforce counters ride the tick delta or a separate presentation channel that is only sent when they change? Both fixes are behavioural, not mechanical, so an owner decision is needed.
2. Is `clearAutoFilledChurches` deliberately meant to run on every load (i.e. 'a Church never keeps a priest across a reload')? The doc comment says 'one-time legacy migration', so I treated the every-load call as the bug; the existing test only pins the function's own behaviour, not the call site.
3. `migrateTickTimeline` cannot fire for any save the version gate accepts (`_ticksPerDay` is always written as TICKS_PER_DAY). Should it be deleted, or is a same-version cadence change intended (in which case the field list must also cover `pregnancyDueProgress` and `pendingStoryEvents`)?
4. Lower-confidence observations left out of findings (all in assigned files, no definite user-visible failure constructed): (a) `applySimTickDelta`'s changed-buildings branch appends changed buildings to the end of `world.buildings` (simDelta.ts:410-417), so the display world's building order can diverge from the worker's until the next command delta ships the full array; (b) `adoptRenderBuffer` returns the previously held buffer to the worker (GameWorkerHost.ts:450-453 → `returnRenderBuffer` transfers it), detaching a buffer that GameLoop's cached `RenderSnapshot` may still reference — the draw-cache buckets are keyed by tick so I could not build a definite visible frame; (c) `applySimPrep` restores entities as new shallow clones, which also breaks object identity for any cache not covered by `invalidateWorldRuntimeCaches` (e.g. `beautyGrid` is not invalidated there).
5. Dead code noticed in neighbouring (non-assigned) files and deliberately not reported: `EntityCatalog.applyTickDelta` (entityCatalog.ts:69) has no callers, and `WorldState.leaderPromise` has no readers.

METHOD NOTE
Supporting files read to verify call sites and consumers (not part of the group): gameLoop.ts, gameTick.ts, workforce.ts, buildingStaffingActions.ts, workSchedule.ts, venueSchedule.ts, viewState.ts, saveSchema.ts, worldRuntimeCaches.ts, entityIndex.ts, renderBufferPool.ts, packRenderSoA.ts, renderSoAReader.ts, entityRenderMeta.ts, renderSnapshot.ts, renderer/entityCache.ts, beautyGrid.ts, huntvisuals.ts, visitorQuest.ts, territory helpers (worldGen/terrainGen via grep), plus tests church.manualStaffing, workerCommand.roundtrip, gameLoop.commandDispatch, gameWorker.transport, commands.validation, simDelta.eventLog, simDelta.storyRoundtrip, saveMigration.roundtrip, saveVersion.gate, useGamePersistence, virtualPlayer (describe index). No file was modified.

### A22-misc-log-tutorial

Confirmed findings from this group: 7

Files read: `src/game/dialogueTrees.ts`, `src/game/guidedCampaign.ts`, `src/game/tutorialCampaign.ts`, `src/game/uiSimSummary.ts`, `src/game/eventLog.ts`, `src/game/eventLogExport.ts`, `src/game/eventLogFilters.ts`, `src/game/preferences.ts`, `src/game/EventLogPanel.tsx`, `src/game/gameTick.ts`, `src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts`, `src/game/tickLayerAssign.ts`, `src/game/dayCycleClock.ts`, `src/game/residencyReconciliation.ts`, `src/game/housingDiagnostics.ts`, `src/game/villagePortrait.ts`, `src/game/groupEvents.ts`, `src/game/humanChat.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/simPrep.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.ts`, `src/game/simWorker/GameWorkerHost.ts`, `src/game/simBuffers/simDelta.ts`, `src/game/gameLoop.ts`, `src/game/worldRuntimeCaches.ts`, `src/game/saveLoad.ts`, `src/game/worldGen.ts`, `src/components/GuidedCampaignPanel.tsx`, `src/components/TutorialCampaignBanner.tsx`, `src/components/CombatLogPanel.tsx`, `src/game/nodeRuntime.ts`, `tests/eventLogPanel.filters.test.ts`, `tests/guidedCampaign.test.ts`, `tests/tutorialCampaign.test.ts`, `tests/simDelta.eventLog.test.ts`, `tests/housingDiagnostics.test.ts`

VERDICT PER FILE
- src/game/eventLog.ts — bounded ring buffer and newest-first contract are correct; the defect is the per-realm, never-resynced `nextEventLogId` (finding 1) plus `resolveCombatLogKind`'s missing `type === 'combat'` guard (finding 3). Not compliant with the authority doc's delta/worker-state expectations until the allocator is carried through worker prep.
- src/game/guidedCampaign.ts — clean: single owner, correct new-calendar-day cadence via dailyWorldEvents.ts:132 (tickLayerDaily only runs on `tick % TICKS_PER_DAY === 0`), state carried in saveSchema.ts:25 / simPrep.ts:84 / simDelta.ts:134, only observes story flags, no UI writes, chapter completion flags match the real writers (deerParliament.ts:16 etc.). No finding.
- src/game/tutorialCampaign.ts — pure read-only predicates, but two predicate defects (findings 2 and 4).
- src/game/uiSimSummary.ts — read-only aggregation; mirrors `workforce.countWorkingAndIdleSettlers` (workforce.ts:586-616) with identical guards, `openBeds` clamped, no division, no mutation. Duplication is a mild one-source-of-truth smell (its construction-worker scan additionally excludes rival buildings, which the sim copy does not) but I found no behavioural divergence: every human faction in the repo is one of visitor/rival/trade_caravan, so `isPlayerHuman` and `!e.faction` agree today. No finding.
- src/game/eventLogExport.ts — formatting/filenames are safe (quotes/newlines escaped, empty-name fallback, `|| 'village'`), no state writes, no NaN paths. No finding.
- src/game/eventLogFilters.ts — one omission (finding 5).
- src/game/preferences.ts — defaults and read/write symmetry are correct (`loadFirstNightWarningDismissed` false default matches `removeItem` on false); `getStorage()` and both try/catch paths behave; the juice cache is written through by `saveJuiceEffectsEnabled`. No finding.
- src/game/dialogueTrees.ts — deterministic (no Math.random; separate `seededRandomForRun` roll lives in humanChat), category/tree validation is solid and I verified all 7 loaded JSON files have every `line.speaker` inside `tree.speakers` and `tree.category === file.category`; the two defects are dead code (findings 6 and 7).

UNRESOLVED / DESIGN QUESTIONS
1. `src/game/data/` contains 11 dialogue JSON files but only the 7 named in `DIALOGUE_SOURCE_FILES`/`DIALOGUE_SOURCE_FILE_NAMES` are ever loaded. `Elections.json` (category 'needs'), `Family.json` ('family'), `Famine.json` ('famine') and `pregnancy.json` ('pregnancy') are referenced nowhere in the repo, and three of their categories are not in `DIALOGUE_CATEGORIES` (so they could not be added without extending the union). CHANGELOG.md:468-476 states the 7 files "now form the only dialogue bank", so I treated the exclusion as intentional and did not file it — but 41 authored trees (incl. all pregnancy/family content) are unreachable; please confirm these are retired rather than accidentally orphaned.
2. `recordGuidedCampaignChoice` (guidedCampaign.ts:120) is dispatched and validated (commands.ts:267, 368) but no component ever sends it — I grepped `choiceId` across src and only the story/raid/diplomacy handlers and `virtualPlayer.ts` (which does not use it) appear. So `GuidedCampaignState.choices` is always empty and the journal's choice records are a prepared-but-unwired path; treated as a missing feature, not a finding.
3. `winter_watch` uses `dayInYear >= 250` while winter starts at `DAYS_PER_SEASON * 3 = 270` (simHelpers.ts:11-14). 'Prepare for winter' firing in late autumn looks deliberate and tests/tutorialCampaign.test.ts:92 locks the 250 threshold, so I did not file it — confirm 250 is the intended trigger.
4. `downloadTextFile` (eventLogExport.ts:56) calls `URL.revokeObjectURL(url)` synchronously right after `anchor.click()`. This is the common pattern and I have no evidence of a real failure, so it is noted only; if downloads ever silently fail in a target browser, revoking inside a `setTimeout` is the usual fix.
5. Preference plumbing is split: `eventLogExport.ts:105` owns its own `wilderfolk_export_chronicle_on_save` key with raw `localStorage`, and `useGameShellState.ts:24-25` still reads the tutorial-done/build-panel keys directly, bypassing `preferences.ts`/`getStorage()`. No behavioural bug found (all sites catch), but it weakens the single-source-of-truth rule from AGENTS.md §5.2.
6. `resetPreferencesCache` (preferences.ts:121) has no callers, so the juice-effects cache is never reset between sessions; harmless today (the only writer updates the cache), noted for completeness.
7. `resolveCombatLogKind`'s string heuristic exists for "saves logged before combatKind existed", but saveLoad rejects any other `_version` (documented beta save policy) and all current combat writers pass a kind, so that heuristic is effectively unreachable for current-version data; my finding 3 concerns the false positives it produces for non-combat entries, not its legacy purpose.

NOTES ON METHOD: no files were modified; verification was static (read + grep + a read-only PowerShell JSON consistency check over src/game/data). I did not run the test suite, so all findings are code-path arguments, each with the exact reachable call chain quoted above.

### A3-daycycle-residency

Confirmed findings from this group: 6

Files read: `src/game/dayCycle.ts`, `src/game/dayCycleClock.ts`, `src/game/residencyOccupancy.ts`, `src/game/residencyReconciliation.ts`, `src/game/residencySelection.ts`, `src/game/householdComposition.ts`, `src/game/housingDiagnostics.ts`, `docs/archive/SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md`, `src/game/gameTick.ts`, `src/game/tickLayerAssign.ts`, `src/game/tickLayerDaily.ts`, `src/game/humanTick.ts (lines 161-380 read in full context of both call sites)`, `src/game/humanLifecycleCleanup.ts`, `src/game/leaderHouse.ts`, `src/game/buildingResidencyActions.ts`, `src/game/simulation/simulationInvariants.ts`, `tests/fullYear.integration.test.ts`, `tests/housingDiagnostics.test.ts`, `tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts`, `tests/leaderRemarriage.residency.test.ts`

Per-file verdicts (read in full): dayCycle.ts — mostly sound constants/formulas; one unreachable-transition bug (finding 1); dead month/day branch in setHumanBirthFromAge is unreachable from all 5 call sites (entityFactory.ts:159/190, dayCycle.ts:375, humanLifecycle.ts:155, settlerInteractionActions.ts:128 all pass 3 args) and lines 360-382 duplicate what setHumanBirthFromAge already set. dayCycleClock.ts — no finding: TICKS_PER_DAY/TICKS_PER_HOUR, weekday, nextTickAtClockHour (handles fromTick===target), isNewCalendarDayTick (lastProcessedCalendarDay is persisted in saveLoad.ts:360/420, simPrep.ts:174, simDelta.ts:333/477) and isProductionTick all behave as documented; the `Math.max(1, Math.round(interval/TICKS_PER_DAY))` day quantisation is lossless for every current caller (all intervals are whole days: PRODUCTION_INTERVAL, EVENT_INTERVAL). residencyOccupancy.ts — no finding (occupancyMove's fromId===toId guard, residenceRoomFor's self-decrement, and the id-0 comment are all consistent with call sites; getResidenceCapacity's `level || 1` also absorbs undefined/NaN). residencyReconciliation.ts — findings 3-5. residencySelection.ts — finding 2; the scoring constants/ties are deterministic (id-ordered, stable sort) and `Math.abs(child.id*7919)%n` is used instead of Math.random, so no determinism defect; auditHousingSharingIssues/isUnnecessarilySharingHousing (lines 487-518) have no production or test caller (only the dayCycle.ts re-export) — unused exported audit helper, not reported as a finding. householdComposition.ts — no finding; collectOwnHousehold/collectFamilyMembers/getChildCustodian/isMinorChild match their comments, and no caller array is mutated (sort calls are on locally filtered copies). housingDiagnostics.ts — finding 6; also note collectHousingDiagnostics runs `state.entities.find` per occupant id inside a per-render memo in VillageTabPanel.tsx:34 (O(entities x occupants) per render) — performance only, no correctness claim, so not reported.

Unresolved / for a design decision: (1) `tickLayerRealtime.ts:117-122` calls `syncResidenceOccupants` on a full `aliveEntities` scan every tick while a Moon Howler is active, i.e. residence sync outside the assign cadence named in SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md §3. I traced why it exists (moonHowler.ts:971/1025 sync only Human-typed entities, and tickLayerAssign.ts:31-35 syncs from byType[Human], so a settler in werewolf form is dropped from its residence occupants for one tick) and the daily layer's assignMissingResidences(aliveEntities) repairs it at day boundaries, so it looks like a deliberate exception rather than a defect — but it is a second scheduling site for the residence owner and worth a documented decision. (2) `tickLayerDaily` indirectly runs `assignMissingResidences` via leaderHouse.ts:119 and dailyPopulation.ts:124, a third cadence (daily) for a decision the authority table lists as assign-cadence; tickLayerDaily.ts:59 documents the leader-house case, dailyPopulation.ts:124 does not. Is this sanctioned 'daily maintenance'? (3) rebuildChildrenIds (residencyReconciliation.ts:50-53) uses truthy id checks (`if (child.motherId)`) while the rest of the residency code deliberately uses `!= null` for id 0 (residencyOccupancy.ts:31). It is only safe because worldGen.ts:691-695 always spawns grass first, so entity id 0 is grass and no living human can hold id 0; if the id allocator ever changes, motherId/fatherId 0 links would be silently dropped. (4) Housing capacity is not in SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md §5, yet findings 3 and 6 both hinge on it being a real invariant rather than a diagnostics warning — worth stating explicitly in the contract.

Method notes: this was a read-only audit; no files were created, modified or deleted and no test/build command was run (running vitest would write cache artifacts), so every claim above is from static reading of the quoted lines plus grep-verified call sites. The existing tests were treated as locking behavior: tests/housingDiagnostics.test.ts deliberately treats unassigned humans as 'healthy' and tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts pins the weekend gate in isProductionTick, so neither was reported.

### A4-workforce-buildings

Confirmed findings from this group: 7

Files read: `src/game/workforce.ts`, `src/game/buildingStaffingActions.ts`, `src/game/buildingActionRouting.ts`, `src/game/buildingActions.ts`, `src/game/buildingConfigurationActions.ts`, `src/game/buildingResidencyActions.ts`, `src/game/prisonGuardDuty.ts`, `src/game/settlerInteractionActions.ts`, `src/game/buildings.ts`, `src/game/buildCatalog.ts`, `src/game/buildingConfig.ts`

Per-file verdicts: workforce.ts — the assignment owner is largely faithful to §3/§5 (duplicate prevention, leader office preservation, prison/prisoner occupants, venue caps all verified against tests), but it writes residence occupants it does not own (F1) and its crew cap and realtime release path are the other defects; buildingStaffingActions.ts — manual staffing owner with three drifting copies of the builder-eligibility rule (F4, F5) and an unreachable skill scan (F7); buildingActionRouting.ts — correct thin routing, no defect; buildingActions.ts — pure documented re-export facade, no logic; buildingConfigurationActions.ts — all four setters validate against the original state then mutate a clone, no defect found (they deliberately allow mode changes on unfinished buildings); buildingResidencyActions.ts — clone/reconcile order is correct; buildingResidencyActions.removeResidentFromBuilding clears residenceBuildingId then immediately re-runs automatic housing, so the resident is usually put straight back (verified via residencySelection.pickResidenceForHuman L725-732 preferring the partner's residence) — this matches its own doc comment ('then immediately restore housing') but contradicts the function name, and it is only reachable through the generic `removeWorker` command because the UI has no remove button for residents (SelectedBuildingPanel.tsx:715 requires !isHousing); prisonGuardDuty.ts — daily owner with an isolated deterministic RNG stream and correct coverage math (guards × 8h clamp), but its escape path leaves stale prison state (F2) and its guard count is a second implementation of humanRelationships.countGuardsAtPrison (L1025-1034) with slightly different criteria (occupant list + job string vs job + homeBuildingId + prisonBuildingId == null) — they agree in practice because arrestForScandal stamps job = Settler; settlerInteractionActions.ts — recruit/tame/debug are consistent (eligibility helpers are shared with virtualPlayer; Math.random is seeded globally by worldGen.enableSeededGlobalRandom, so this is not a determinism defect); buildings.ts — the config table is complete and matches BuildingType (all 37 keys), mineOreForMode matches MINE_ORES, but the `mineMode` field comment (L118 'extracts stone (default) or iron') is stale/wrong versus MINE_ORES/mineOreForMode (test mineOreMode.test.ts locks iron as the fallback), and buildings.getBuildingConfig (L687), isDecorBuilding, isResidentialBuilding/isResidenceBuilding alias and isUniqueBuilding have no importer anywhere in src or tests (dead exports, and getBuildingConfig also shadows buildingConfig.getBuildingConfig with different semantics — undefined vs FALLBACK_BUILDING_CONFIG); buildCatalog.ts — category table omits WildlifePreserve (F6) and formatBuildingCost (L125) has no caller; buildingConfig.ts — 20-line fallback accessor, correct.

Unresolved / design questions: (1) Is the Wildlife Preserve intentionally retired (then valleyChronicle.ts:61-64 and dailyEcology.ts:76 should go) or was it accidentally dropped from the catalog? (2) Is a House site really meant to hold up to 6 builders and steal from every non-Farm workplace, or should a dedicated builder cap exist? (3) Should a jailed settler keep a construction-crew slot (F9 fix belongs either in workforce.prepareWorkforce or in the arrest owner)? (4) buildingActionRouting.assignIdleWorkerToBuilding silently discards `preferredHumanId` when it routes a completed residence to assignResidentToBuilding, so the App.tsx multi-select 'Assign N selected settlers here' button on a House re-runs global auto-housing and may house the chosen settlers elsewhere — is that intended (residence owner picks homes) or should the UI hide the button for residences? (5) Should prison release be an enumerated 'immediate' residence trigger in SIMULATION_AUTHORITY §3, which would legitimise F8? (6) findOverstaffedDonorBuilding's `>= 2` threshold plus ascending sort makes a full 2-of-2 Farm the preferred donor over a 4-of-4 Mine; the name says 'overstaffed' but the heuristic may be intentional, so it is recorded as a question rather than a finding. No files were modified; this was a read-only audit.

### A5-placement-strips

Confirmed findings from this group: 8

Files read: `src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts`, `src/game/buildingMaintenanceActions.ts`, `src/game/buildingRotation.ts`, `src/game/placementUtils.ts`, `src/game/adjacencyIndex.ts`, `src/game/stripBuild.ts`, `src/game/stripJunction.ts`, `src/game/stripTopology.ts`

METHOD: every file was read in full (321/217/92/195/200/120/260/385 lines) and each function, including unexported helpers, was traced to its call sites with grep. No test in C:\Wilderfolk\tests references these eight modules by name (grep for stripTopology|stripJunction|stripBuild|adjacencyIndex|placementUtils|buildingRotation|buildingPlacementActions|buildingMaintenanceActions in tests/ = 0 matches), so nothing here is test-locked except the one item noted below.

ONE-LINE VERDICT PER FILE
- src/game/buildingPlacementActions.ts — one real logic error (footprint-clearing region vs the placement gate, finding 1) plus a second removal owner with no cap-aware refund (finding 2); startBuilding itself is otherwise sound (affordability checked before any deduction, unique/research/terrain/bounds order correct).
- src/game/buildingMaintenanceActions.ts — no cadence or ownership violation, and its own cleanup is complete for the fields it owns (homeBuildingId/residenceBuildingId/prison* chain, `totalBuildingsCompleted` matching the load-time recompute at saveLoad.ts:447, `unindexAdjacency` + `state.adjacency = undefined` which tests/demolition.adjacencyHydration.test.ts:58 deliberately locks in); gaps are the missing rival guard (finding 4) and the refund message (finding 7).
- src/game/buildingRotation.ts — no logic error in the functions themselves; the repo carries two different `BuildingRotation` types (`buildings.ts:79` = 0|90|180|270 for `Building['rotation']`, `buildingRotation.ts:4` = 0|90) and only 0/90 ever reach `createBuilding` because both callers normalize, so the 180/270 corner angles in `structures.ts` doc comment are latent, not live; Fence is missing from both metadata sets (finding 5).
- src/game/placementUtils.ts — clean. `canPlaceBuildingSnapshot` duplicates the domain gate in the same branch order and is currently equivalent to `getPlaceBuildingFailureReason` (the duplication is acknowledged in its comment at line 174); the flood-free tile indexing and the strict-inequality overlap test (edge-touching allowed) are correct; bridge/fishing branches are internally consistent with their comments.
- src/game/adjacencyIndex.ts — clean. Self-adjacency is correctly impossible by construction (Farm/Greenhouse are never in barnMap, the road query explicitly skips Roads, Store/Workshop are never in marketMap), the cell-radius scan cannot miss a source inside the radius (80*ceil(r/80) >= r), bucket/`idToCell`/`indexedIds` are all pruned, `rebuild` clears before inserting, and `syncAdjacency`'s early return for a never-completed non-source is correct. The only hazard is external (finding 2: a removal path that skips `unindexAdjacency`).
- src/game/stripBuild.ts — arithmetic clean (pitch>0 guard, `MAX_STRIP_SEGMENTS-1` + `<=` yields exactly 72 centres, direction preserved via stepDir, negative/zero drag collapses to one segment); its only issue is the `replacesBuildingId` field it advertises (finding 6).
- src/game/stripJunction.ts — classification arithmetic is sound for the shapes it is actually used on (road elbows/tees/crosses: worked through the L-corner case and `cornerRotationFromConnections` returns the matching 270/0/180 frames); two dead-code items (findings 8, 9). Worth noting: `detectBuildingJunction` is called only with family 'road' (renderer/buildings.ts:49), so the wall-junction path of this module — and `stripRender.drawProceduralWallJunction`/`drawProceduralWallCorner`/`cornerArms` — have no caller at all, which is also why `resolveWallStripPlan` never emits `junctionInfo`.
- src/game/stripTopology.ts — the wall-plan existing-piece skip is asymmetric (finding 3); `findEnclosedWallAreas` is sound in effect (the raw flat-index neighbour arithmetic at lines 335-343 can wrap at column 0/cols-1, but a wrap can only ever involve a border or blocked cell, and component cells can be neither, so it is unreachable rather than harmful).

UNRESOLVED QUESTIONS (need an owner's decision, not a code fact I can settle)
1. Building anchor convention is not settled repo-wide and is the one thing that could flip finding 1's direction. `getBuildingCenter` (simulation/humanRelationships.ts:223-225), residencyOccupancy.ts:53, beautyGrid.ts:31, hotelStay.ts:224, hospitalCare.ts and ~100 other sites compute the centre as `building.x + building.width / 2`, and rivalEvents.ts:130 explicitly converts a camp centre to a top-left x (`rival.campX + slot.dx - config.width / 2`) — all of which imply building.x,y is TOP-LEFT. But the placement subsystem (placementUtils bounds/terrain/overlap, useCanvasInteractions.findBuildingAt:51-54, the build ghost at renderer/grid.ts:378-381 and the strip preview at grid.ts:335-336) treats it as the CENTRE, and MAP_EDGE_INSET's comment ('so sprites are not clipped') plus the centre-anchored sprite draw (spriteDrawing.ts:73) and centred procedural strip draw match that reading. Whichever is authoritative, finding 1 stands as an internal contradiction (a tree/terrain edit applied to a region the gate never validated), but the correct global fix is a design decision for the simulation owner.
2. `resolveRoadStripPlan` deliberately has no existing-piece skip, unlike the wall plan, so a road drag that crosses an existing road emits a piece on the occupied cell, the UI's all-segments-valid gate refuses the entire chain, and crossing/joining roads cannot be laid in one drag. `detectBuildingJunction`/`drawProceduralRoadJunction` exist to draw exactly those crossings, so this looks like a gap rather than intent — but it is a behaviour/design choice, so it is reported here rather than as a finding.
3. Is the wall corner/junction feature meant to be live? `stripJunction` computes it, `resolveWallStripPlan` discards it, and `stripRender`'s wall junction/corner renderers have no caller. Additionally, completed Fences are drawn by the `isDecorType` branch (renderer/buildings.ts:146) because `BUILDING_CONFIGS[Fence].decor === true` (buildings.ts:678), so `drawProceduralFence`/rotation is only ever used for ghosts and construction sites — a presentational inconsistency that follows from Fence being in `STRIP_BUILD_TYPES` (stripBuild.ts:14) while being configured as decor.
4. `placementUtils.isFootprintOnBuildableTerrain`'s fishing branch rejects RiverBank tiles (RiverBank is in UNBUILDABLE_TERRAIN but not WATER_TERRAIN, so the line-112 test reduces to 'RiverBank OR Mountains OR Snow') although the comment says only mountains/snow are refused and the building is described as a 'Riverside fishing post' — a dock can therefore never sit on the bank. This may be intended; it is recorded as a question.
5. `isBuildingTechUnlocked` requires BOTH `unlockedTechs.includes(id)` AND `node.researched`, while `notifyBuildingLocked` (research.ts:77) returns silently when only `unlockedTechs` contains the id. `syncResearchUnlocks` keeps the two in step for normal play, so the divergence is only reachable from a hand-edited/legacy save, where a click on a locked building would produce no feedback at all. Not reported as a finding because I could not show a reachable path.

### A6-spatial-pathfinding

Confirmed findings from this group: 5

Files read: `src/game/spatialGrid.ts`, `src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts`, `src/game/spatialQueryMetrics.ts`, `src/game/scentGrid.ts`, `src/game/simQueries.ts`, `src/game/beautyGrid.ts`, `src/game/pathfinding.ts`

VERDICT PER FILE
- src/game/pathfinding.ts — UNSOUND: two independent high-severity defects break path following (origin-seeking waypoint loop; double position integration), plus the sampling cap that can miss blocked tiles. The A* core itself (heap, octile heuristic, corner guard, path reconstruction) is correct and deterministic, and tests/pathfinding.test.ts only covers findPath/getPathGrid/lineCrossesBlocked, never steerWithPath.
- src/game/spatialGrid.ts — Mostly sound: swap-remove, insert/update, radial and rect bounds, reconciles, road index dedup (lastAvoidQueryId) and computeRoadLayoutStamp all check out. Two gaps: syncTreeGrid never reconciles, and a block of unused API.
- src/game/adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts — Sound; cost model and both routing paths agree with the grid helpers. No finding: only latent edge-case/perf notes below.
- src/game/spatialQueryMetrics.ts — Sound and internally consistent (category list is the single source of truth; flush/zero logic correct for the per-tick flush performed by gameTick.ts:71/270).
- src/game/scentGrid.ts — Sound: decay-then-deposit cadence is per-tick/realtime, values clamped to [0,255], NaN-tolerant sampling/reader, sidecar header indices match SCENT_HEADER_WORDS=6. No finding.
- src/game/simQueries.ts — Sound: resident/occupant index, naive vs grid query parity, and the grass/wildlife snapshot bookkeeping (absorbedEntityIds, bornAfterSnapshot, self-spawn subtraction) are consistent with their callers in tickLayerSystems/simulationEntities/dailyGrassEcology. No finding.
- src/game/beautyGrid.ts — Functionally correct (stamp falloff, clamps and test-locked behaviour verified numerically against tests/beauty.decor.test.ts), but the happiness input set and its save round-trip are both wrong.

UNRESOLVED / DESIGN QUESTIONS
1. syncTreeGrid: the fix depends on whether mid-game trees are supposed to exist at all. Today the only mid-game tree spawn, groupEvents.ts:1688-1693 (nature_boom), pushes its trees into `allAlive` + `entityById` only — never into `state.entities` — so those 15 trees are visible for one tick and then silently missing from the world and from `byType`. That is a cross-group defect (groupEvents/entityIndex, not A6) and it currently masks the tree-grid staleness. Whoever fixes nature_boom should add the reconcile line in the same change, otherwise newly spawned trees become invisible to every tree query.
2. Should `steerWithPath` own the integration at all? The JSDoc says it does, the two callers act as if it does not. I recommended reverting to velocity-only because that is the minimal edit that makes both existing callers correct; the alternative (keep the move, skip humanTick.ts:1467 for the 'path' branch and drop hotelStay.ts:234-235) touches more code and needs a caller-visible flag.
3. `EntitySpatialGrid.insert` (161) returns early for non-finite x/y WITHOUT removing the entity's previous cell entry, while `update` (183) only removes on `!alive`; `clampToMapBounds` (mapBounds.ts:17-20) does not guard NaN, and `validateInvariant` cannot detect a stale-in-place cell. So a single NaN position would leave the entity queryable in its old cell (dSq NaN passes `dSq > radiusSq`) and unflagged by the invariant checker. I found no reachable NaN source today, so I did not raise it as a finding — but insert/update are the wrong place to swallow it.
4. `forEachInArrayRadius` (adaptiveSpatialQuery.ts:42-67) lacks the `Number.isFinite(radius) / radius < 0` guard that `forEachInRadius` has. A NaN radius routes to the naive path (the cost model compares NaN < x = false) and then `dSq > NaN` is false for every entity, so the callback runs for the entire fallback array. No caller passes a non-finite radius today (all radii are constants), so this is latent only.
5. Perf-only, out of group but caused by my group's options builder: humanRelationships.ts:154/1303/1396 call `socialAdaptiveOptions('social', n, width ?? 0, height ?? 0)`. With worldWidth/worldHeight = 0 the model computes totalCells=1 and always chooses 'naive', so those social scans silently bypass `humanSocialGrid` and do an O(population) array scan per settler per SOCIAL_STAGGER tick. Correctness is unaffected (the predicate is applied in both paths).
6. Diagnostics: `getSpatialQueryReport` (spatialQueryMetrics.ts:182) divides session totals by `measuredTicks`, which is incremented only for ticks that had activity (128-146), so the printed "queries/tick" is an average over active ticks, not over elapsed ticks. Its only consumer is scripts/perf-all.ts, and `formatSpatialQueryReport` (224) / `getCurrentTickMetrics` (178) are unused anywhere — diagnostics-only, not raised as a finding.
7. pathfinding's module-level `gridCache`/`pathCache` are keyed on `seed|wallSignature|WxH`; the terrain tiles and the map preset are not part of the key, so two worlds with the same seed/size but a different preset (or any future terraforming) would reuse a stale blocked grid until the wall signature or dimensions change. The UI never passes a seed (App.tsx:892 uses a random one), so this needs an explicit `initGame({seed})`, which is why I left it out of the findings.
8. hotelStay.ts:232 builds its path cache key from rounded pixel coordinates, so the key changes almost every tick: A* (up to 6000 nodes) is re-run per visitor per tick and `pathCache` is cleared every 200 inserts, evicting the stable settler commute paths too. Performance only, no correctness claim, so not reported.
9. I verified against git history that the latest commit 349f49e added the two position-integration lines to steerWithPath (finding 2) and changed the lineCrossesBlocked step formula (finding 3); the origin-seeking waypoint loop (finding 1) and the non-reconciling syncTreeGrid predate that commit, and neither is locked in by any existing test (no test anywhere imports or exercises steerWithPath).

Verdict per file — src/game/humanTick.ts: correct on ownership (it writes no building.occupants / homeBuildingId / residenceBuildingId / pregnancy-start state, and all daily relationship decisions reach the single owners behind the isNewCalendarDay gate exactly once per colony day), but it contains one misaligned stagger gate, a duplicated energy formula whose result depends on the camera viewport, one ungated daily mortality call, and several dead/low-value guards; no invariant-breaking or authority-violating write was found.

Checks performed that produced no finding (recorded so they are not re-litigated): (a) every daily relationship call site (tryDailyConception, tryDailyAffairEncounter, tryDailyAffairGossip, tryDailyAmicableDivorce, tryDailyHumanMortality, trySchoolyardGossip, tryFormSchoolyardBond, creditChildSchoolDay, syncHumanAgeFromCalendar, flushRelationshipDiagnostics) has exactly one caller — humanTick — so no second owner exists; (b) isNewCalendarDayTick is read before gameTick's markCalendarDayProcessed, so the daily block cannot fire twice or be skipped on a normal tick; (c) reproductionCooldown is decremented per real tick only here (Humans are absent from WILDLIFE_TICK_TYPES, tickLayerSystems.ts:72-79), so it is not double-decremented, and the per-tick decrement matches REPRODUCTION_COOLDOWN_TICKS = ticksForDays(150); (d) the realtime affair path never writes affairPartnerId and never rolls a scandal for an unestablished pair (it requires hasAffairPartner(...) && entity.affairPartnerId === paramour.id, humanTick.ts:1010-1015), matching tests/affair.cadence.test.ts; exposure requires intimate === true and spouse presence (humanRelationships.ts:1076-1082); (e) all positional calls (tickHumanHunting, commuteHumanToBuilding, tryExposeCaughtAffairForPair, findCourtshipPartner, findClosestAdaptiveInRadius, tryDailyAffairEncounter/Gossip) match their signatures argument-for-argument; (f) every candidate/partner list predicate re-checks entity.alive, so a settler killed earlier in the loop cannot be selected later in the same pass; (g) the offscreen throttle (state.tick + id) % 24 never suppresses the daily block, which sits above the `active` gate, so no daily decision is lost for off-screen settlers; (h) no division-by-zero / NaN path found in the movement and leisure math (all `Math.hypot(...) || 1`, fract() guards non-finite, energy/velocity writes are clamped or bounded); (i) per-entity Maps (workersByWorkplace, schoolReserved) are tick-local and do not grow across ticks.

Not reported deliberately: Math.random() is used in ~10 places (humanTick.ts:326, 896-906, 995-1006, 1241-1252, 1311), but worldGen.ts:570 installs a seeded global Math.random (simRng.enableSeededGlobalRandom) for the whole run, so this is seed-deterministic in production and consistent with the conception/arrest rolls in the owner modules — the only residual risk is that the focus-dependent `active` gate changes how many draws a tick consumes, which is inherent to that design and is not test-locked either way. Pure performance observations (state.entities.filter inside getPlayerCampCenter called per visitor/rival per tick, updatedBuildings.filter(isResidenceBuilding) allocated per courtship tick at :914, kids/coworkers/shiftMates filters per tick at :1199-1205 and :1075, allHumans.filter at :220) are correctness-neutral and therefore excluded.

Unresolved/design questions: (1) tradeCaravans.ts:212 creates the caravan carrier without `ageYears`, so birthYear/birthDay stay 0 (entityFactory.ts:105-107) and humanTick.ts:300's daily syncHumanAgeFromCalendar computes its age from colony day 0 — a fresh trader is 'juvenile' (age 6-11, isJuvenile set true) during year 1 and 'elderly' after ~48 in-game years. The root cause is the creation site (visitors/rivals do pass ageYears, groupEvents.ts:277-285), but humanTick is what applies the bogus calendar; decide whether the daily age sync should be restricted to isPlayerHuman or whether every foreign creation must pass ageYears/colonyDay. (2) Whether the off-screen `minimalEnergyLoss` path was an intentional cheap approximation or (as the near-verbatim copy of 4 of humanNeeds' 7 modifiers suggests) drift — if intentional, the resting reduction is the modifier whose absence most changes survival. (3) The mutant check at humanTick.ts:324-327 is a legacy-save repair (entityFactory always sets relationshipStatus), so it is reached only for pre-migration saves; confirm it is still wanted there rather than in migration.

2. tryDailyAffairGossip line 321 uses `if ((entity.affairProgress ?? 0) < 85 && (lover.affairProgress ?? 0) < 85) return;` (proceeds when EITHER partner is >= 85) while the parallel guard at line 333 and school gossip line 369 use the opposite combination. Because exposeAffair runs arrest + `tryDivorceOnCaughtCheater(..., caughtInAct = true)` (divorceChance = 1) for 'caught' (lines 1149-1153), the daily gossip path can arrest and force a divorce with no witness present, while the realtime path requires the spouse within AFFAIR_SPOUSE_BLOCK_RADIUS. docs/archive/PRISON_FUNCTION_AUDIT.md:24 calls this function an acceptable owner, so I did not report it - but the unused parameters plus the authority doc's \"spatial caught-in-the-act event (spouse/guard physically present)\" wording suggest a missing witness check.

'eligibility')` at line 815, so a candidate can be counted in `conceptionCandidates` with no gate bucket. Needs a design ruling.
6. tryDailyHumanMortality lines 840-853 wrap the illness roll in a bare `{ ... }` scope block with no condition - harmless leftover, no behaviour change.
7. Outside this file: decisionRegistry.ts has no `youthLove` key although SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md section 3 declares youth love an owned decision (tests/simulation.decisionRegistry.test.ts REQUIRED_DECISIONS omits it).

Determinism: this file calls Math.random() directly in most paths (tryDailyConception, tryDailyAffairEncounter, tryDailyHumanMortality, exposeAffair/arrest), while advanceYouthLove, trySchoolyardGossip, tryFormSchoolyardBond and tryDailyAmicableDivorce accept an injectable rng. Production is still seeded because worldGen.ts:570 calls enableSeededGlobalRandom(), so I did not report a determinism finding; the mixed style does make the conception/affair rolls harder to unit test than the rng-parameterised paths.

Verified-OK (no finding raised): (1) Layer cadence law holds — gameTick calls realtime every tick, systems when tick%4===0, assign when tick%18===0 (4x/day, 18 = 6*TICKS_PER_HOUR), daily when tick%72===0; 72 is divisible by 4 and 18, so the day boundary always includes a systems and an assign pulse, and isProductionTick (dayCycleClock.ts:79-89) always lands on a systems tick. (2) Year/day/hour arithmetic is consistent between dayCycle.ts and dayCycleClock.ts: the `prevCalendarDay > 0` guard (gameTick.ts:78-79, dailyWorldEvents.ts:221-222) correctly suppresses a double year increment when a world is reloaded at a tick that is an exact TICKS_PER_DAY multiple, dayInYear stays 0 for the whole day-360 boundary day, and markCalendarDayProcessed/isNewCalendarDayTick agree because lastProcessedCalendarDay is only advanced at the end of the boundary tick. (3) tickLayerSystems' multi-tick scaling is correct: flash/combatTicks/reproductionCooldown/animFrame/energy and the wander+breeding probabilities are all scaled by `step = WILDLIFE_LAYER_INTERVAL`, and winterPenalty/config.energyLossPerTick are per-tick values that are only multiplied once (no double scaling); updateResearch's per-pulse `PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE` (1/3) gives the same per-day total as the legacy cadence (18 pulses/day x 1/3 = 6). (4) gameTick's paused early-return cannot desync humanPopulation/wildlifeCounts/workingSettlers/idleSettlers/entityByType: it returns before any mutation, and computePopulationCounts/countWorkingAndIdleSettlers re-filter `alive` from allAlive, so dead entities left in allAlive cannot inflate counters. (5) I checked the `deathsThisTick = aliveEntities.length - (allAlive.length - newEntities.length)` undercount (gameTick.ts:250) and concluded it is harmless: the guard at line 257 also ORs `newEntities.length > 0`, and when newEntities is empty the expression equals the true death count, so the stale-byType path is not reachable — deliberately not reported.

Deliberately not reported (redundant/dead guards, not behaviour): tickLayerDaily.ts:51 `if (state.tick > 0)` can never be false on the gameTick path (daily requires tick%TICKS_PER_DAY===0 and tick is incremented first); dailyWorldEvents.ts:84 `state.tick % TICKS_PER_DAY === 0` is likewise always true when reached from tickLayerDaily.ts:81; and tickElectionPromises is called twice in the same tick (tickLayerDaily.ts:62 and dailyWorldEvents.ts:131) but is idempotent through its `evaluated` story flag, so it is not a double effect.

Unresolved design question: OFFSCREEN_WILDLIFE_THROTTLE has two incompatible readings — simFocus.ts:25 documents it as 'every N wildlife-layer calls' (8 calls = 32 ticks) while the human analogue OFFSCREEN_HUMAN_THROTTLE is expressed in ticks and locked by tests/dayCycle.cadence.test.ts:95-99 ('~8 clock hours' = 24 ticks). The fix for finding 1 preserves the documented 'layer calls' reading; if the intended value is really 8 ticks, the constant and its comment should be restated and the stagger made id-preserving.

Method limitation: this was a static read-only audit (no files modified, no test suite or game run), so findings 1-5 are code-path proofs from the cited lines and call sites rather than runtime reproductions.

No ordinary test asserts it after worker command results, although simulationInvariants.ts:31 claims it is safe to run there. The violations above would therefore surface only as dev console warnings (slice(0, 8)) and would never fail CI — worth deciding whether the full-year gate should check more ticks (a birth or a raid only needs to land on one daily tick) or whether the collector should be asserted in the command round-trip tests.

2. Is the one-Howler gate owned by `curseMoonHowler` or by each caller? spawnMoonHowlerDebug (finding 6) shows the caller-side convention is not enforced. If the debug command is intentionally allowed to break §5 for testing, that intent is not documented anywhere I could find, and the collector has no debug exemption.

3. May a pregnant settler be on a construction crew (finding 7)? The §5 table does not say, but the workforce owner excludes pregnancy from jobs and from auto-crewed sites, so the manual builder command looks like the odd path out; either the check belongs in addToConstructionCrew (one rule) or the exclusion is only meant for jobs.

However parseSaveJson (saveLoad.ts:79-83) rejects every save whose `_version !== GAME_VERSION`, and buildSaveData writes `_ticksPerDay: TICKS_PER_DAY` (saveLoad.ts:107-108), so no loadable save can differ in day length, and no legacy 24-tick/day save can reach this code. If the version gate is temporary (the file still carries applySaveMigration records), both the missing `scaleField(rec, 'pregnancyDueProgress')` and a load-time backfill (`entity.pregnancyDueProgress = Math.round(PREGNANCY_TICKS * (0.85 + Math.random() * 0.3))` when `pregnant && pregnancyDueProgress == null`) should be added before that gate is lifted, otherwise the §5 'pregnant has a valid pregnancyDueProgress' invariant is false from load until birth.

5. CATALOG_PATCH_KEYS (simBuffers/simDelta.ts:168-179) omits youthLovePartnerId/youthLoveProgress (and childrenIds/motherId/fatherId), so youth-love changes never reach the display entity catalog. Both partners stay mutually consistent but stale, so I did not raise it as an invariant finding; flagging it because it is the same field family as finding 4 if the catalog is meant to mirror sim state.

Second-Howler creation from a save is also conceivable via migrateLegacyMoonHowler (moonHowler.ts:849-867), which curses every uncursed `EntityType.Werewolf` unconditionally; I could not find any current spawn/reproduction path that creates a wild werewolf (tickLayerSystems.ts:684-685 explicitly excludes werewolves from breeding), so I left it out — but a hand-edited or legacy save with two werewolves would convert into two cursed villagers and trip the collector.

## Context: in-flight uncommitted changes present in the working tree during the audit

The audit read the **working tree**, not `HEAD`. While it ran, the tree also contained uncommitted work from another session:

- a determinism refactor replacing roughly 228 `Math.random()` call sites across 17 simulation files with seeded streams (`getSimRng('<module>')()`, `seededRandomForRun(...)`, and `personDayRoll(...)`), plus a `getSimRng` → `seededRandomForRun` change in `dailyGrassEcology.ts`;
- a worker tick-rollback payload fix recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-13-worker-tick-rollback-leaves-stats.md` (adding `yearlyStats` / `lifetimeStats` / `populationHistory` to the prep payload and deep-cloning it).

Those edits were made by another session; **this audit modified no source file**. Line references in this report are the ones the audit agents read (roughly 19:58–20:30); in the 17 refactored files a line number may since have shifted, so locate the cited code by content.

Two interactions matter, because they belong to the same defect class this audit reports:

1. **`Math.random()` → `personDayRoll()` converts a per-tick gate into a day-stable gate.** `personDayRoll` hashes `(entityId, absolute calendar day, salt)` and is therefore constant for all 72 ticks of a day. Where the enclosing function runs per tick, the branch now fires on *every tick of a lucky day* instead of ~25% of ticks. Measured in the current tree: `hospitalCare.ts:99` (charges 1 food), `:110` (floating text) and `:120` (chat) are now day-stable, and the caller gate at `humanHospitalBehavior.ts:94` is day-stable too, so a patient standing near a staffed hospital repeats the food cost, the doctor skill gain (`:106-108`) and the chat attempt on every tick until `heal < 0.5` stops the call. This is exactly the H7/M31 pattern (`townHall.ts:207-295`, `humanVenueBehavior.ts:115-123`): **a day-stable roll must be paired with a per-day latch, or the effect must move to the daily owner.**
2. **`getSimRng('<module>')()` is one stateful stream per module, so draw order becomes part of simulation state.** Any newly added or newly *conditional* draw (or a change in entity/Map iteration order) shifts every later draw in that module and changes downstream outcomes. That is fine for a fixed order but couples reproducibility to iteration order; the X1 cross-cutting findings list the places where iteration order is not stable (bucket splice order, Map insertion order). A stateless per-site salt (`seededRandomForRun('<module>:<purpose>:<id>')`) or a stream keyed per entity is robust to reordering.

---

## Appendix B — method limits (what this audit does not prove)

- **Static evidence only.** No failing runtime reproduction was produced for any finding. Each finding cites the exact code and the path that reaches it; a fix should still add the regression test named in the individual bug reports.
- **Presentation code excluded.** `src/game/renderer/**`, `*.tsx` panels/screens, sprite drawing, `viewState.ts`, `dashboardData.ts` and content files (guide/roadmap/tutorial text, name pools) were not audited as simulation logic; where a presentation file duplicates a simulation rule (for example the Nature-tab breakdown) that duplication is reported from the simulation side.
- **Balance/tuning not judged.** Whether a probability, price or cap is *fun* or correctly tuned was out of scope; only whether the code does what it declares.
- **Cross-cutting findings are single-pass.** See the note in that section.
- **Exact-version save policy.** The audit checked that state is carried, not that historical saves migrate; the project policy is that only the current `GAME_VERSION` loads.
- **Two evidence tiers.** Findings marked *confirmed by adversarial verification* (all high/medium/low entries above) were re-derived from source by a second agent that was instructed to refute them. The cross-cutting section carries a lower tier. For a small number of entries the verifier paraphrased the finding title, so the long-form impact text could not be matched to the audit candidate and the verification paragraph itself is used as the description — those entries still carry the same verified code evidence.
- **This report was remediated, not left open.** The findings were fixed after the audit at their owning modules (see *Post-audit changes*), and the low and cross-cutting tables carry a per-finding status. An entry marked `open` is still open. A fix must follow the Simulation Change Record in `docs/archive/SIMULATION_AUTHORITY.md` §8 and add a regression test.
